# Uniform excess charge for Coulomb molecules and the outer radius of neutral atoms

Coordinate `EN-117-000-0020-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-B-PH-H-0549` | `PM-263.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Uniform-excess-charge-for-Coulomb-molecules-and-the-outer-radius-of-neutral-atoms-September-24-2026` | date 2026-09-24 | derived from the openai/math preprint Uniform-excess-charge-for-Coulomb-molecules-and-the-outer-radius-of-neutral-atoms-September-24-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove that a molecule with <i>M</i> fixed nuclei of real charges at least one and total nuclear charge <i>Z</i> strictly binds at most $`Z+CM`$ electrons, where <i>C</i> is universal. The result holds for the full nonrelativistic Coulomb Hamiltonian with two spin states and arbitrary distinct nuclear positions. For neutral atoms with integer nuclear charge <i>Z</i> ≥ 1, the first ionization energy and, for every ground state, the radius outside which one half of an electron remains are each bounded above and below by positive universal constants.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that a molecule with <i>M</i> fixed nuclei of real charges at least one and total nuclear charge <i>Z</i> strictly binds at most $`Z+CM`$ electrons, where <i>C</i> is universal. The result holds for the full nonrelativistic Coulomb Hamiltonian with two spin states and arbitrary distinct nuclear positions. For neutral atoms with integer nuclear charge <i>Z</i> ≥ 1, the first ionization energy and, for every ground state, the radius outside which one half of an electron remains are each bounded above and below by positive universal constants.

- **claim-uniform-excess-charge** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a universal finite constant $C$ such that every nuclear system specified above and every integer $n\ge1$ satisfy $$E_n<E_{n-1}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad n\le Z+CM.$$ Moreover, $E_n=E_{n-1}$ whenever $n>Z+CM$. For one nucleus this gives $n\le Z+C$ under strict binding, for every real $Z\ge1$. The same constant is independent of all charges and nuclear positions.
  - Explanation: Strict binding permits only a uniformly bounded excess per nucleus, independently of nuclear charges and positions. Above that bound adjacent sector energies coincide; this does not exclude threshold eigenstates.
- **claim-outer-half-electron-radius** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-radius)): There are universal constants $0<c\le C<\infty$ such that, for every integer $Z\ge1$ and every normalized ground state of $H_{Z,Z}$, $$c\le R(\Psi_Z)\le C.$$
  - Explanation: Every normalized neutral atomic ground state has a half-electron exterior radius bounded above and below independently of charge, without symmetry or uniqueness assumptions.
- **claim-first-ionization-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-first-ionization)): For the full nonrelativistic Coulomb atom specified above, with integer nuclear charge $Z\ge1$, one-electron kinetic term $-\Delta/2$, and two electron spin states, write $E_Z(n)=E(n,Z)$. There are universal constants $0<c\le C<\infty$ such that the first ionization energy of the neutral atom satisfies $$c\le I_1(Z):=E_Z(Z-1)-E_Z(Z)\le C \qquad\text{for every integer }Z\ge1.$$
  - Explanation: The neutral atom's first removal energy stays uniformly positive and finite in the stated kinetic and spin normalization.
- **claim-sector-monotonicity-and-attainment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sector-compactness)): For every fixed nuclear system, $E_{n+1}\leq E_n$ for $n\geq0$, and $E_1<0$. If $E_n<E_{n-1}$, there is a normalized $\Psi\in\mathcal Q_n$ with $q_n[\Psi]=E_n$.
  - Explanation: Sector energies decrease with particle number, the first sector is negative, and a strict adjacent energy drop yields a normalized minimizing state.
- **claim-reciprocal-potential-number-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sector-crude)): If $E_n<E_{n-1}$ and every $z_m\geq1$, then $n\leq3Z$.
  - Explanation: A weighted reciprocal-potential test gives a coarse charge-linear bound on strictly binding particle numbers.
- **claim-global-sector-minimum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sector-global-minimum)): For every fixed nuclear system there is a largest integer $N$ such that $E_N<E_{N-1}$. It satisfies $N\leq3Z$, has a normalized minimizer $\Psi$, and $$\label{eq:sector-global-minimum} E:=E_N=\inf_{n\geq0}E_n.$$ Any bound on this $N$ bounds every strictly bound sector of the same system.
  - Explanation: The largest strictly binding sector attains the minimum over all sectors and bounds the index of every other strict sector.
- **claim-symmetric-multiplier-energy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sector-multiplier)): Let $\Psi\in\mathcal Q_n$ be a normalized eigenstate with eigenvalue $e_\Psi$. If $F$ is a bounded real Lipschitz function of the spatial configuration, invariant under particle permutations, then $$\label{eq:sector-multiplier} q_n[F\Psi]-e_\Psi\\|F\Psi\\|_2^2 =\frac12\mathbb E_\Psi\|\nabla F\|^2.$$ Here $\nabla$ denotes the full spatial gradient in $\mathbb R^{3n}$.
  - Explanation: Multiplying an eigenstate by a symmetric real Lipschitz function changes its energy relative to its norm by an exact gradient contribution.
- **claim-atomic-binding-and-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-atomic-binding)): For integer $Z\ge1$, $E_n<E_{n-1}$ for $1\le n\le Z$. Each of these sectors has a ground state, and every normalized minimizing sequence has an $L^2$ convergent subsequence with normalized ground-state limit. The multiplier identity of Lemma <a href="#lem:sector-multiplier" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:sector-multiplier">7</a> holds for every such ground state with $e_\Psi=E_n$.
  - Explanation: All positive-ion and neutral sectors bind strictly for integer atomic charge. Their minimizing sequences have normalized ground-state limits, and the multiplier identity applies.
- **claim-atomic-global-minimum** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-atomic-sector)): Every strict sector, namely every $n$ with $E_n<E_{n-1}$, satisfies $n\leq2Z+1$. There is consequently a largest strict index $N_*$, and $$\label{a:eq:found-sector} Z\leq N_*\leq2Z+1\leq3Z, \qquad E:=\inf_{n\geq0}E_n=E_{N_*}.$$ The infimum $E$ is attained by a normalized ground state in sector $N_*$.
  - Explanation: The atomic reciprocal-radius argument refines the coarse sector bound and identifies an attained minimum after the last strict drop, allowing earlier plateaus.
- **claim-coulomb-duality-and-smearing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-loc-coulomb)): For compactly supported real $f\in L^{5/3}(\mathbb R^3)$, its Coulomb potential is continuous and belongs to $\dot H^1$, and $$\label{eq:loc-coulomb-duality} D(f)=\frac1{8\pi}\\|\nabla(K*f)\\|_2^2, \qquad \left\|\int fv\right\|\leq C\sqrt{D(f)}\\|\nabla v\\|_2 \quad(v\in\dot H^1).$$ The potential and energy assertions also hold for a nonnegative $f\in L^{5/3}$ whose distribution belongs to $(\dot H^1)^*$, with $K*f$ defined by its nonnegative convolution. Its dual pairing with any $v\in\dot H^1$ is the absolutely convergent integral $\int fv$.* *If $\eta$ is a radial nonnegative probability density supported in $B(0,b)$, then $K*\eta\leq K$, with equality outside that ball. In particular, $D(\eta*\rho)\leq D(\rho)$ for every nonnegative compactly supported $\rho\in L^{5/3}$.
  - Explanation: Coulomb energy is a gradient norm and controls Sobolev test pairings. Radial probability smearing lowers the potential and direct energy in the stated density classes.
- **claim-fermionic-occupation-and-kinetic-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-loc-kinetic)): If $0\leq\gamma\leq1$ has finite trace, then $$\label{eq:loc-coarse-kinetic} \operatorname{Tr}(-\Delta/2)\gamma \geq c\int\rho_\gamma^{5/3}$$ for a universal $c>0$. The one-body density matrix of an antisymmetric $k$-particle vector $f$ is bounded above by $\\|f\\|_2^2$ times the identity. The same bound holds after integration in auxiliary variables, and such matrices may be summed whenever the corresponding squared norms sum to one.
  - Explanation: The occupation constraint gives a positive coarse kinetic density bound. Antisymmetry preserves the occupation constraint under auxiliary integration and normalized mixtures.
- **claim-atomic-global-density-offset** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-atomic-global-density)): For one nucleus of charge $Z\ge1$, every $\delta$-state in a sector $n\le3Z$ satisfies $$\label{eq:atomic-global-density} \int\rho_\psi^{5/3}\le C(Z^{7/3}+\delta).$$
  - Explanation: Atomic density integrability is controlled by nuclear charge and the energy offset when the sector obeys the coarse particle bound.
- **claim-conditional-localization-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-loc-identity)): With the preceding definitions, set $$T_o=\frac12\sum_I\sum_{i\in I}\\|\nabla_i\psi_I\\|_2^2.$$ There is a nonnegative localization error satisfying $$\label{eq:loc-error} \mathrm{IMS}\leq\frac12\mathbb E_\psi\sum_i\sum_h \big(\|\nabla s_h\|^2+\|\nabla c_h\|^2\big)(x_i)$$ such that $$\label{eq:loc-cut} q_n[\psi]+\mathrm{IMS} =\mathbb Ee_X+T_o-\mathbb E\sum_{i\ \mathrm{out}}\Phi_X(x_i) +\mathbb E\sum_{i<j\ \mathrm{out}}K(x_i-x_j).$$ The out kinetic energy satisfies $$T_o\geq c\int(o^2\rho_\psi)^{5/3},$$ where $\rho_\psi$ is the raw one-body density. Conditional quantities have versions jointly measurable in the data and the field evaluation point. Appending deterministic cuts admits a coupling in which these decorated data are nested, and hence conditional expectations of raw quantities satisfy the tower property.
  - Explanation: Recorded out particles leave an antisymmetric conditional core. The exact energy splitting retains its screened field, a controlled localization error, an out-density kinetic bound, and nested data towers.
- **claim-coherent-hole-insertion** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-loc-hole-trial)): Fix a conditional core slice with finite energy $e_X$ and field $\Phi_X$ as above. Let $\rho\geq0$ belong to $L^{5/3}$ and have compact support. Suppose its packet region $\operatorname{supp}\rho+\overline{B(0,b)}$ has positive distance from the spatial support allowed to the core particles. Then $$\label{eq:loc-trial} E\leq e_X+c_{\mathrm{TF}}\int\rho^{5/3} +Cb^{-2}\int\rho -\int\Phi_X(g_b^2*\rho)+D(\rho).$$ The constant depends only on the fixed function $g$. The vacuum trial also gives $E\leq e_X$.
  - Explanation: Coherent packets and Bernoulli occupation supply genuine integer-sector fermionic trials with arbitrary expected mass in a hole separated from the core.
- **claim-fine-density-lower-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-loc-cubes)): In the setting of Lemma <a href="#lem:loc-identity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:loc-identity">13</a>, let $n_o$ be the out particle count. Choose, measurably, any subset of the out particles and set $$\sigma(x)=\sum_{i\ \mathrm{retained}}\vartheta_b(x-x_i).$$ Then $$\begin{aligned} T_o&\geq\mathbb E\left[c_{\mathrm{TF}}\int\sigma^{5/3} -Cb^{-2}(n_o^{4/3}+n_o)\right], \label{eq:loc-fine-kinetic}\\ \sum_{i<j\ \mathrm{out}}K(x_i-x_j) &\geq D(\sigma)-Cn_o/b \quad\text{almost surely}. \label{eq:loc-fine-pair} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Averaged Neumann cube counting supplies the sharp leading kinetic coefficient for the radial fine density of any measurably retained subset; radial smearing supplies the pair lower bound with self-energy error.
- **claim-nuclear-scale-properties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-m-lem-screen-scale)): In the nuclear-scale geometry, $a:\mathbb R^3\to(0,\infty)$ is $L^{-1}$-Lipschitz and satisfies $$\label{m:eq:screen-scale-properties} Z^{-1/3}\le a_y<\infty,\quad \nu(B(y,La_y))\le a_y^{-3},\quad \nu(B(y,2La_y))>(2a_y)^{-3},\quad d_y\le La_y.$$ If $d_y/L>1$, then $a_y=d_y/L$. For every fixed $c<L/2$, $B(y,ca_y)$ is covered by a bounded number of balls $B_z$ with $a_z\asymp a_y$. The covering bound depends only on $c$ and $L$.
  - Explanation: The nuclear scale is positive, finite and Lipschitz, controls nearby nuclear mass, and permits bounded covers by comparable cells. Its large-distance value is the distance to the nearest nucleus divided by the scale factor.
- **claim-conditional-positive-field** (bound, [source](#stmt-a-lem-screen-provisional)): Fix $C_0\ge1$. Suppose the initial sequence is absent, or its total out support is covered by at most $C_0$ balls $B_z$ and the sum of its cuts’ squared gradients is bounded by $$\sum_z D_z^2a_z^{-2}\mathbf 1_{B_z}, \qquad \frac{D_z^2}{a_zm_z}\le C_0\sqrt P.$$ If $X$ denotes its data (trivial for an absent sequence), and $e_*$ is the maximum of $e_y$ and the energies $e_z$ of these covering cells, then $$\label{m:eq:screen-positive} \big\\|(\mathbb E[J_y\mid X])_+\big\\|_2 \le C(C_0)\sqrt{\frac{P e_*}{a_y}}.$$
  - Explanation: A finite admissible initial cut cannot produce an excessively large positive conditional far field. The bound retains the largest energy unit among the target and covering cells.
- **claim-local-thomas-fermi-patch** (construction, [source](#stmt-a-lem-screen-patch)): Fix $y\in\mathcal Y$, write $a=a_y$, $m=m_y$, $e=e_y$, and choose $0<\beta<1/100$ such that $$\label{m:eq:screen-patch-condition} \frac{\beta^{-2}}{am}\le\sqrt P.$$ Set $b=\beta a$. There is a deterministic $t_0\in[5a,6a]$ and a cut full out on $B(y,t_0)$, supported in $B(y,t_0+b)$, with gradients at most $C/b$. Let $X$ be its out data, $\rho_X^c$ the conditional core density, $\Phi_X=V-K*\rho_X^c$, and $\Omega=B(y,t_0-4b)$. There is a unique minimizer $\rho=\rho_X\ge0$, extended by zero outside $\Omega$, of $$\label{m:eq:screen-tf-functional} \mathcal T_X(\rho)=c_{\mathrm{TF}}\int\rho^{5/3}-\int\Phi_X\rho+D(\rho).$$ It is measurable in $X$ and, with $W=\Phi_X-K*\rho$, satisfies $$\label{m:eq:screen-tf-equation} \rho=kW_+^{3/2}\quad\hbox{in }\Omega.$$ Retain the out particles with $\|x_i-y\|<t_0-7b$ and put $\sigma=\sum_{i\ \mathrm{retained}}\vartheta_b(\cdot-x_i)$, using the radial fine kernel from Lemma <a href="#lem:loc-cubes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:loc-cubes">15</a>. Write $$\label{m:eq:screen-remainder} \mathcal L=c_{\mathrm{TF}}\int\left(\sigma^{5/3}-\rho^{5/3} -\frac53\rho^{2/3}(\sigma-\rho)\right).$$ For every sufficiently small $\epsilon>0$, $$\begin{aligned} \label{m:eq:screen-comparison} &\mathbb E\left[D(\sigma-\rho)+\mathcal L+\int W_-\sigma\right]\\ &\quad\le C\left[\delta+(\epsilon+\sqrt\beta)Pe +\epsilon^{-3/2}\beta^{1/2}a^{-7} +b^{-1}\sqrt Pm +b^{-2}\left(P^{2/3}m^{4/3}+\sqrt Pm\right)\right].\notag \end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $\mathbb E\int\sigma^{5/3}$ is bounded by $CPe$ plus a constant times this right-hand side, and the out kinetic energy obeys $$\label{m:eq:screen-out-kinetic} T_o\le C\left(Pe+b^{-2}\sqrt Pm\right).$$ Finally, independently of the data, $$\label{m:eq:screen-interior-upper} W\le V_{\rm loc}+Ca^{-4}\quad\hbox{on }B(y,3a),\qquad \int_{B(y,2a)}\rho\le Ca^{-3},$$ where $V_{\rm loc}$ is the potential of nuclei in $B(y,20a)$. If this local nuclear measure is zero, as it always is in the atomic distance geometry, the same construction has the sharper bound $$\label{a:eq:screen-patch} \mathbb E\left[D(\sigma-\rho)+\mathcal L+\int W_-\sigma\right] \le C\left[\delta+\sqrt\beta Pe+b^{-1}\sqrt Pm +b^{-2}\left(P^{2/3}m^{4/3}+\sqrt Pm\right)\right],$$ and its interior bounds include $$\label{a:eq:screen-interior} W\le Ca^{-4},\qquad \rho\le Ca^{-6} \quad\hbox{on }B(y,3a),\qquad \int_{B(y,2a)}\rho\le Ca^{-3}.$$
  - Explanation: A deterministic shell cut produces a unique measurable local Thomas–Fermi minimizer. Matching upper and lower energy comparisons control Coulomb discrepancy, convex remainder and negative-field penalty; nucleus-free patches have a sharper comparison.
- **claim-local-count-second-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-m-lem-screen-count)): For every normalized $\delta$-state in any finite sector and every $y\in\mathcal Y$, $$\label{m:eq:screen-count} \mathbb En_y^2\le C\left(\max(a_y^{-6},1)+\delta a_y\right).$$ In particular the number $P$ in <a href="#m:eq:screen-count-normalization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:screen-count-normalization">[m:eq:screen-count-normalization]</a> is at most a universal constant.
  - Explanation: The patch comparison closes the provisional count bootstrap uniformly in the state, finite sector, target and nuclear system.
- **claim-uniform-counts-and-conditional-fields** (bound, [source](#stmt-a-prop-screen-local)): In either geometry, every normalized $\delta$-state in a finite sector satisfies $P\le C$. Fix $y\in\mathcal Y$. Suppose the initial finite sequence of cuts has total out support covered by at most $C_0$ cells $B_z$, with $C_0^{-1}\le a_z/a_y\le C_0$, and satisfies the gradient hypothesis of Lemma <a href="#m:lem:screen-positive-field" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:screen-positive-field">[m:lem:screen-positive-field]</a>. The absent-cut case is allowed. Then $$\label{a:eq:screen-local} P\le C,\qquad \big\\|(\mathbb E[J_y\mid X])_+\big\\|_2 \le C(C_0)\frac{m_y}{a_y}.$$ Neither constant depends on the nuclear system, particle number, state, target, or $\delta$.
  - Explanation: The closed count bound and comparable covering scales yield a uniform positive conditional far-field estimate for admissible finite cuts, including no cut.
- **claim-refined-small-scale-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-a-cor-screen-refined)): There is a universal $a_0>0$ with the following property. If $a=a_y<a_0$ and $\psi$ is a $\delta$-state with $\delta\le a^{-7+0.02}$, choose the patch of Lemma <a href="#m:lem:screen-patch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:screen-patch">[m:lem:screen-patch]</a> with $b=a^{1+0.2}$. Its densities obey $$\label{m:eq:screen-fine-error} \mathbb E\left[D(\sigma-\rho)+\mathcal L+\int W_-\sigma\right] \le Ca^{-7+0.02},\qquad \mathbb E\int\sigma^{5/3}\le Ca^{-7}.$$ The raw one-body density satisfies $$\label{m:eq:screen-density} \int_{B(y,4a)}\rho_\psi^{5/3}\le Ca^{-7}.$$ In particular, the two estimates needed in the atomic application are $$\label{a:eq:screen-refined} \mathbb E\left[D(\sigma-\rho)+\int W_-\sigma\right]\le Ca^{-7+.02}, \qquad \int_{B(y,4a)}\rho_\psi^{5/3}\le Ca^{-7}.$$
  - Explanation: At sufficiently small scale and sufficiently small energy offset, a prescribed fine width makes the Coulomb discrepancy, convex remainder and negative-field penalty smaller than the local energy unit, while controlling the raw density.
- **claim-raw-near-coulomb-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-auto-corollary-1)): Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#m:lem:screen-density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:screen-density">[m:lem:screen-density]</a>, for $0<u\le4a$, $$\label{a:eq:screen-near-coulomb} \mathbb E\sum_{\|x_i-y\|<u}\frac1{\|x_i-y\|} \le Ca^{-4}(u/a)^{1/5}.$$
  - Explanation: The refined local density estimate bounds the expected Coulomb contribution from particles arbitrarily close to the target.
- **claim-source-free-field-oscillation** (bound, [source](#stmt-a-lem-screen-oscillation)): For every patch in Lemma <a href="#a:lem:screen-patch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:lem:screen-patch">18</a> with $\nu(B(y,20a))=0$, and every $0<s'\le a/2$, almost surely in its data, $$\label{a:eq:screen-oscillation} \sup_{x\in B(y,s')}\|W(x)-W(y)\| \le C\frac{s'}a\bigl(a^{-4}+W(y)_-\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: A nucleus-free patch has a field oscillation estimate that retains dependence on an arbitrarily negative central value, rather than assuming a lower field cap.
- **claim-atomic-annular-counts** (bound, [source](#stmt-a-cor-screen-annular)): In the atomic distance geometry, for every $0<\lambda<\Lambda<\infty$ and $u>0$, every normalized $\delta$-state of finite particle number satisfies $$\label{a:eq:screen-annular} \left\\|\#\{i:\lambda u<\|x_i\|<\Lambda u\}\right\\|_2 \le C_{\lambda,\Lambda} \bigl(\max(u^{-3},1)+\sqrt{\delta u}\bigr).$$ All constants are independent of $Z$ and of the particle number.
  - Explanation: A comparable-cell cover controls annular particle counts for arbitrary finite offsets, uniformly in charge and particle number.
- **claim-observation-density-versions-and-towers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-obs-data)): There are jointly Borel versions of $$\mu_j(y)=\mathbb E_P\!\left[\sum_{i=1}^n\mathcal K_{x_i}(y) \mid\mathcal F_j\right], \qquad H_j(y)=V_s(y)-(K*\mu_j)(y),$$ with the following properties. At every positive-density data tuple, $\mu_j\ge0$, $\int\mu_j=n$, and $\mu_j$ is smooth in its spatial variable. Every spatial derivative has a deterministic bound for the fixed system. The potential $K*\mu_j$ is $C^1$, with bounded value and gradient, and $$\label{eq:obs-poisson} \Delta H_j=-4\pi\nu_s+4\pi\mu_j$$ in distributions. In particular, the always valid regularity statement is that $H_j-V_s$ is $C^1$ and bounded with bounded gradient. If $V_s$ and its gradient are bounded and continuous, these conclusions hold for $H_j$ itself. Conditional averaging satisfies $$\label{eq:obs-tower} \mathbb E_P[\mu_j(y)\mid\mathcal F_{j+1}]=\mu_{j+1}(y),\qquad \mathbb E_P[H_j(y)\mid\mathcal F_{j+1}]=H_{j+1}(y)$$ where $V_s(y)$ is finite. The same towers hold for nonnegative spatial integrals, and for integrable signed observables. These statements include the trivial data at $j=m$. None of the fixed-system regularity bounds is asserted to be uniform in the nuclear system or in $t_{\min}$.
  - Explanation: Explicit likelihood ratios give jointly measurable conditional densities with conserved mass, smooth spatial dependence, Poisson identities and towers. Regularity of the observed field itself requires regularity of the retained nuclear potential.
- **claim-observation-event-energy-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-obs-tilt)): Suppose $\Psi$ is an eigenstate of its sector Hamiltonian with energy $e_\Psi$, where $0\le e_\Psi-E\le D_0$. In particular, $e_\Psi=E_n$ for a sector ground state. Suppose further that $$\sum_{h=j}^{m-1}\ell_h^{-2}\le C_\ell\ell_j^{-2}$$ with a fixed numerical $C_\ell$. This holds for the dyadic scales $\ell_h=r_h^{1.01}$, $r_{h+1}=2r_h$, with $C_\ell=(1-2^{-2.02})^{-1}$. If $A\in\mathcal F_j$ has probability $p=P(A)>0$, put $p_A(x)=P(A\mid x)$ and $$\Psi_A=\sqrt{p_A(x)/p}\,\Psi.$$ Then $\Psi_A$ is a normalized antisymmetric form-domain vector, its raw position-and-spin law is the raw marginal of $P$ conditioned on $A$, and $$\label{eq:obs-tilt} q_n[\Psi_A]\le e_\Psi+C\ell_j^{-2}\log^5(e/p) \le E+D_0+C\ell_j^{-2}\log^5(e/p).$$ The constant depends only on $C_\ell$ and the chosen noise law, not on $n$, the number of arrays, or the nuclear system. The same assertion holds for an event of one unordered observation at any width $\ell$, using $\ell$ in place of $\ell_j$. No upper bound on the particle number in terms of the nuclear charge is needed.
  - Explanation: An event of noisy unordered observations defines a symmetric likelihood multiplier whose energy cost depends on event probability and observation width without a particle-number or array-count factor.
- **claim-averaged-posterior-field-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-obs-transfer)): Fix a point $y$ where $V_s(y)$ is finite and a length $a>0$. Let $A\in\mathcal F_j$ have positive $P$-probability, and let $Q$ be $P$ conditioned on $A$, extended by fresh localization labels. Its raw marginal is the law of $\Psi_A$ from Lemma <a href="#lem:obs-tilt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:obs-tilt">26</a>. Let $X$ denote the fresh patch data, and let $\rho_X^c$ be the conditional density of core particles under this tilted localization law. Partition the remaining raw particles into retained and deleted particles. Let $$\sigma=\sum_{i\ {\rm retained}}\vartheta_b(\cdot-x_i),\qquad h_X=V_s-K*\rho_X^c-K*\rho_X,\qquad W_X=V_c+h_X,$$ where $\vartheta_b$ is the radial probability kernel of Lemma <a href="#lem:loc-cubes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:loc-cubes">15</a>, supported in $B(0,\sqrt3b)$, and $\rho_X\ge0$ is a measurably chosen patch comparison density. Assume $\rho_X$ and $\sigma$ belong to $L^{5/3}$ and have compact support for almost every patch datum. Put $t=t_y$, $\tau=t/a$, and $\beta=b/a$, and suppose $0<t,b\le a/10$. Assume the following estimates, with fixed constants:* 1. *The master width $z\mapsto t_z$ is Lipschitz with constant at most $1/2$. The raw one-particle density $\rho_{\Psi_A}$ satisfies $$\int_{B(y,4a)}\rho_{\Psi_A}^{5/3}\le Ca^{-7}.$$* 2. *Deleted particles have $\|x_i-y\|\ge ca$, and their count obeys $\mathbb E_Q n_{\rm del}\le Ca^{-3}\sqrt\beta$.* 3. *For some $\varepsilon_a\ge0$, $$\mathbb E_Q D(\rho_X-\sigma)\le Ca^{-7}\varepsilon_a^2.$$* 4. *On $B(y,t)$ either the source-free bound $\rho_X\le Ca^{-6}$ holds, or $$\rho_X=k(W_X)_+^{3/2},\qquad W_X\le V_{\rm loc}+Ca^{-4},\qquad V_{\rm loc}(z)=\sum_\alpha\frac{q_\alpha}{\|z-R_\alpha\|}, \qquad\sum_\alpha q_\alpha\le Ca^{-3},$$ with $q_\alpha\ge0$. The latter alternative permits arbitrary locations of the local nuclei.* *Then the two expectation towers are $$\label{eq:obs-two-towers} \mathbb E_Q(K*\mu_j)(y)=\mathbb E_Q P^{\rm ms}(y),\qquad \mathbb E_Q(K*\rho_X^c)(y)=\mathbb E_Q P^c(y),$$ where $P^{\rm ms}$ is the potential of the raw master packets and $P^c$ is the raw core potential. Moreover, $$\label{eq:obs-transfer} \begin{split} \|\mathbb E_Q(H_j-h_X)(y)\|\le Ca^{-4}\big[& \tau^{1/5}+\beta^{1/5}+\sqrt\beta +\varepsilon_a\tau^{-1/2}\\ &+R(\tau)+(\tau+\sqrt3\beta)^{1/5}\big], \end{split}$$ where $R(\tau)=\tau^2$ in the source-free alternative and $R(\tau)=\tau^2+\tau^{1/2}$ in the nuclear alternative. Thus the common upper bound $R(\tau)\le C\tau^{1/2}$ is always available. All constants are independent of the number of particles; they depend only on the constants in the displayed hypotheses.
  - Explanation: Separate expectation towers under the original observation posterior and the fresh tilted patch posterior yield an averaged field transfer. The estimate requires all displayed density, deletion, Coulomb-error and kernel hypotheses and does not identify posteriors pathwise.
- **claim-maxima-of-weak-subsolutions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-maximum)): Let $\mathcal O\subset\mathbb R^3$ be open, let $g$ be locally bounded, and let $F(y,t)$ be measurable in $y$, continuous in $t$, and bounded on compact spatial sets and bounded ranges of $t$. If finitely many functions $v_i\in C(\mathcal O)\cap H^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal O)$ satisfy $$\Delta v_i\ge g+F(y,v_i)$$ in distributions, their maximum satisfies the same inequality with its own value in the reaction term. The assertion also holds for functions $v_i=V+a_i$ with continuous locally $H^1$ offsets $a_i$, when all inequalities have the common singular term $-4\pi\nu$ and the reaction vanishes in a neighborhood of $\operatorname{supp}\nu$.
  - Explanation: Finite maxima preserve the semilinear weak inequality with the reaction evaluated at the maximum. Common nuclear singularities may be canceled when the stated reaction condition holds.
- **claim-large-nuclear-void-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-m-lem-void-shell)): There are universal constants $t_0,C>0$ with the following property. Let $O\in\mathbb R^3$, $t\ge t_0$ and $T=2^Jt$, where $J\ge1$ is an integer. Suppose that at least one nucleus lies in $B(O,t/100)$ and that every nucleus satisfies $$\|R_m-O\|<t/100\qquad\text{or}\qquad \|R_m-O\|>100T.$$ Then $$\label{m:eq:void-annulus-count} \left\\|\#\{i:t\le \|x_i-O\|<T\}\right\\|_{L^2(\Psi)}\le C.$$
  - Explanation: A nuclear void supports only a uniformly bounded count norm across an arbitrarily long dyadic radial range, avoiding a loss proportional to the number of shells.
- **claim-global-exterior-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-m-lem-void-count)): There is a universal constant $C$ such that, for $0<s\le 1$, $$\label{m:eq:void-count} \mathbb E\#\{i:a_{x_i}\ge s\}\le CMs^{-3}.$$ The constant is independent of the charges and the distances between nuclei.
  - Explanation: Nested nuclear representatives separate crowded scale pairs from isolated void runs and bound the exterior expected electron count linearly in the nuclear count.
- **claim-global-thomas-fermi-minimizer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-m-lem-barrier-global-tf)): Let $\zeta$ be as above, with total mass $Q>0$. The functional $$\mathcal T_\zeta(\rho) =c_{\mathrm{TF}}\int\rho^{5/3}-\int V_\zeta\rho+D(\rho), \qquad \rho\in\mathcal X_+,$$ has a unique minimizer. Its field $W=V_\zeta-K*\rho$ satisfies $$\label{m:eq:global-tf-equation} \rho=kW_+^p,\qquad \Delta W=-4\pi\zeta+c_{\mathrm F}W_+^p,\qquad W\ge0,\qquad \int\rho\le2Q.$$ The screening potential $K*\rho$ is continuous and tends to zero at infinity. Consequently $W$ tends to zero at infinity and is continuous away from point charges.
  - Explanation: The global Thomas–Fermi problem for the admitted point and radial smooth sources has a unique minimizer, a nonnegative decaying field and a finite mass bound.
- **claim-single-source-and-mixture-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-m-lem-barrier-mixture)): For one point charge $Q>0$ at $R$, the field in Lemma <a href="#m:lem:barrier-global-tf" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:barrier-global-tf">31</a> satisfies $$\label{m:eq:single-field-lower} W(x)\ge c\min\left(\frac{Q}{\|x-R\|},\|x-R\|^{-4}\right).$$ For a single point charge or radial smooth charge of mass $Q$, its screening potential is bounded by $CQ^{4/3}$.* *More generally, select source components of masses $Q_i$, truncate them to masses $q_i\le Q_i$, and put $q=\sum_iq_i>0$. Let $W_i$ be the single-source field of mass $q$ at the $i$th center with that source’s normalized profile. If $W$ is the full-source field, then $$\label{m:eq:tf-mixture} \sum_i\frac{q_i}{q}W_i\le W.$$
  - Explanation: Single-source fields have an explicit qualitative lower profile and a screening cap. Convexly mixing single-source fields after truncating source masses gives a lower comparison for the full-source field.
- **claim-scale-adapted-reference-fields** (bound, [source](#stmt-m-lem-barrier-fields)): There are fixed constants $c_0>0$, $\theta>0$, $c_\theta>0$, and $C<\infty$ such that, off the nuclei, $$\label{m:eq:reference-field-bounds} W_0(x)\ge c_0a_x^{-4},\qquad \|h_0(x)\|\le Ca_x^{-4},$$ and everywhere $$\label{m:eq:smooth-reference-bounds} c_\theta a_x^{-4}\le W_s(x)\le Ca_x^{-4},\qquad \|\nabla W_s(x)\|\le Ca_x^{-5},\qquad W_s(x)\le1.01W_0(x).$$ Here $h_0$ is continuous, and $W_s$ is locally $C^1$. Both fields decay at infinity.
  - Explanation: The original and smoothed reference fields have positive scale-dependent lower bounds, controlled continuous offsets and decay; the smooth field also has a gradient cap and a comparison to the original field.
- **claim-nuclear-offset-oscillation** (bound, [source](#stmt-m-lem-barrier-oscillation)): Fix $y$ and write $a=a_y$. Let $h=h_0$, or let $h=W-V_c$ for an interior Thomas–Fermi patch centered at $y$ satisfying the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#m:lem:screen-patch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:screen-patch">[m:lem:screen-patch]</a>. Then $h$ has a continuous representative. For $0<s\le a/10$ and $\|z-y\|\le s$, $$\label{m:eq:offset-oscillation} \|h(z)-h(y)\|\le Ca^{-4}(s/a)^{1/2}+C(s/a)h(y)_-.$$ For $h=h_0$ the final term can be omitted.
  - Explanation: Subtracting the confined nuclear potential produces continuous offsets with controlled variation even near point charges. The reference offset needs no negative-value term.
- **claim-master-kernel-and-potential-correction** (bound, [source](#stmt-m-lem-barrier-kernel)): Uniformly as $r_*\downarrow0$, a center contributing to $Rf(y)$ satisfies $\|z-y\|\le2t_y$ and $t_z/t_y=1+O(r_*^w)$. Moreover, $$\label{m:eq:master-kernel-bounds} R1(y)=1+O(r_*^w),\qquad \\|\nabla_z\mathcal K_z(y)\\|_\infty\le Ct_y^{-4},\qquad \\|\nabla_z\mathcal K_z(y)\\|_2\le Ct_y^{-5/2}.$$ The norms in these gradient estimates are taken in the center variable $z$. The operator $R$ is bounded on $L^{5/3}$ and preserves integrals. The correction $$\label{m:eq:potential-correction} q_0=K*(\rho_0-R\rho_0)$$ is nonnegative, continuous, belongs to $\dot H^1$, and tends to zero at infinity. It satisfies $$\label{m:eq:potential-correction-bound} 0\le q_0(y)\le Ca_y^{-4}\tau_y^{1/2}.$$
  - Explanation: Center-dependent smoothing preserves mass, controls kernel derivatives and produces a nonnegative decaying potential correction bounded below the reference field scale.
- **claim-damped-comparison-barriers** (construction, [source](#stmt-m-lem-barrier-subsolution)): There are fixed positive constants $\alpha,\epsilon_0,C'_0,c_b,c'$ with the following properties for all sufficiently small $r_*>0$. For $0<r\le r_*$ define $$\label{m:eq:barrier-definition} \Gamma_r=\epsilon_0W_s+C'_0r^{4\alpha}W_s^{1+\alpha},\qquad B_r=W_0+q_0-\Gamma_r,\qquad b_r=B_r-V_c, \qquad h_b=h_0-c_ba^{-4}.$$ The offsets $b_r$ are continuous and locally $H^1$ and decay at infinity. On $\{a\ge r\}$ their Laplacian densities satisfy $$\label{m:eq:barrier-subsolution} \Delta b_r\ge-4\pi\nu_s+f_y(b_r).$$ More precisely, this inequality holds almost everywhere there between the locally integrable densities representing the two sides; on every open set contained in this region it is also a distributional inequality. On the same region, $$\label{m:eq:barrier-bounds} B_r\ge0.9W_0>0,\qquad h_b+c'a^{-4}\le b_r\le Ca^{-4}.$$ For every contributing center $z$ in <a href="#m:eq:offset-reaction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:offset-reaction">[m:eq:offset-reaction]</a>, $$\label{m:eq:reaction-positive-margin} V_c(z)+h_b(y)\ge ca_y^{-4} \quad\text{almost everywhere in }z.$$ All these constants are independent of $r,r_*$ and the nuclear system.
  - Explanation: Damping the positive reference fields produces continuous comparison offsets with an averaged nonlinear subsolution inequality, an exterior positivity margin and a positive reaction margin on every contributing kernel center.
- **claim-barrier-gap-on-doubling** (bound, [source](#stmt-m-lem-barrier-gap)): Fix $\kappa\in(0,1/10)$. The barriers in Lemma <a href="#m:lem:barrier-subsolution" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:barrier-subsolution">36</a> have a fixed constant $\gamma>0$ such that, whenever $R_+=2r\le r_*$, $$\label{m:eq:barrier-gap} b_r-b_{R_+}\ge\gamma R_+^{-4} \qquad\text{on }\{R_+\le a\le(1+\kappa)R_+\}.$$
  - Explanation: Doubling the scale strictly lowers the barrier by a uniform margin on the transition band, supplying room for the unequal propagation shifts.
- **claim-positive-observed-field-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-m-lem-cond-tail)): If $r/2\le a_y\le5L_1r$, then, for a universal $C$ and every $s>C$, $$\label{m:eq:cond-tail} \mathbb P\{H_j(y)>s a_y^{-4}\} \le e\exp\{-c_{L_1}(s-C)^{2/5}a_y^{-1+.004}\}.$$ All estimates hold for sufficiently small $r_*$, uniformly in $j<n$.
  - Explanation: Event tilting and the local positive-field bound give a stretched-exponential upper tail on the participating scales, uniformly over observation indices.
- **claim-spatial-field-cap-and-cost** (construction, [source](#stmt-m-lem-cond-cap)): There are universal thresholds $C'_{\rm cap},C_{\rm cap}$ with the following properties. Define $$\begin{split} Q\text{ is bad}&\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \sup_QH_j>C'_{\rm cap}a_Q^{-4},\\ B_Q&=\mathbf 1_{\{Q\text{ bad}\}}\sup_Q(H_j)_+, \qquad \Pi=\sum_QB_Q\chi_Q,\qquad \mathcal E=\bigcup_{Q\text{ bad}}Q'. \end{split}$$ Then $H_j-\Pi\le C_{\rm cap}a^{-4}$ on the participating band. For each fixed $m\ge1$ and $A'>0$, $$\label{m:eq:cond-cap-moments} \mathbb P(Q\text{ bad})+a_Q^{4m}\mathbb EB_Q^m \le C_{L_1,m,A'}r^{A'}.$$ At each deterministic point, $\mathbb P(y\in\mathcal E)$ has the same arbitrarily high polynomial bound. The cube families are finite, and $$\label{m:eq:cond-band-volume} \sum_Q\|Q'\|\le C_{L_1}Mr^3.$$ All these random objects are jointly measurable in the data and the spatial variable. For the fixed system and scale, their sizes and supports have deterministic bounds.
  - Explanation: Bad-cube penalties cap the observed field across the band. Their probabilities and moments have arbitrarily high polynomial decay, while the enlarged cubes occupy controlled nuclear-scale volume.
- **claim-molecular-clipped-inverse-comparisons** (bound, [source](#stmt-m-lem-cond-inverse)): Fix $j<n$, and put $r=r_j$ and $R_+=2r$. For each deterministic $y$ with $r\le a_y\le2L_1R_+$, the failure probability of each implication below is less than $r^{40}$: $$\label{m:eq:cond-inverse} \begin{aligned} \mu_j(y)\ge T_y(h_b(y)) &\ \Longrightarrow\ H_j(y)\ge I_y(\mu_j(y))-\delta_0a_y^{-4},\\ \mu_j(y)\le T_y(h_{\rm top}(y)) &\ \Longrightarrow\ H_j(y)\le I_y(\mu_j(y))+\delta_0a_y^{-4}. \end{aligned}$$ The constants and the smallness threshold for $r_*$ are independent of the system and the number of scales.
  - Explanation: The clipped inverse reaction and the observed field satisfy one-sided comparisons at each deterministic target, with small failure probability. Negative exceptional patch fields are paid through the retained density penalty.
- **claim-integrated-exceptional-comparison-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-m-lem-cond-moments)): Fix $j<n$, put $r=r_j$ and $R_+=2r$, and let $r\le a_y\le2L_1R_+$. Under the original joint law $P=\mathbb P$, $$\label{m:eq:cond-field-moments} \\|\mu_j(y)\\|_2\le Ct_y^{-3}a_y^{-3},\qquad \\|f_y(H_j(y))\\|_2 \le Ca_y^{-6}(1+\tau_y^{-3/2}).$$ Consequently, if $F_y$ is either failure event from Lemma <a href="#m:lem:cond-inverse" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:cond-inverse">40</a>, then $$\label{m:eq:cond-integrated-cost} \int_{\{r\le a_y\le2L_1R_+\}} \mathbb E\big[\mathbf 1_{F_y}(\mu_j(y)+f_y(H_j(y)))\big]\,\mathrm dy \le CM r^{20-3-3w}.$$ The same bound holds with $F_y$ replaced by $\{y\in\mathcal E\}$, and the expected integral over all of $\mathbb R^3$ of $r^{-2}\sum_QB_Q\mathbf 1_{Q'}$ is bounded by the right side as well.
  - Explanation: Conditional density and reaction moments turn pointwise failure probabilities into an integrable spatial error, also paying the bad-cube penalty Laplacian.
- **claim-molecular-outward-layer-step** (construction, [source](#stmt-m-prop-outward-step)): Suppose that $u,d_j$ satisfy the inductive conditions above, including <a href="#m:eq:outward-invariant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-invariant">[m:eq:outward-invariant]</a>–<a href="#m:eq:outward-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-bounds">[m:eq:outward-bounds]</a>, at scale $r=r_j$. There are an offset $u'$ and a nonnegative error density $\widetilde d$, measurable with respect to the same data $\mathcal F_j$, that satisfy these inequalities at scale $R_+$ with $\mu_j$ still in the core term. One may take $$\begin{aligned} \label{m:eq:outward-error} \widetilde d={}&d_j+C r^{-2}\sum_Q B_Q\mathbf 1_{Q'}\notag\\ &+\mathbf 1_{\{r\le a<R_+\}}\mu_j \mathbf 1_{\mathcal E\cup\mathcal B^{\mathrm h}}\notag\\ &+\mathbf 1_{\{R_+\le a\le2L_1R_+\}}T_y(H) \mathbf 1_{\mathcal E\cup\mathcal B^{\mathrm l}}, \end{aligned}$$ where $C$ is a universal constant large enough to dominate the penalty Laplacian.
  - Explanation: Unequally shifted maxima replace the reaction by the actual conditional density on the next layer. Explicit nonnegative errors pay failed inverse comparisons and the cap penalty.
- **claim-outward-conditional-averaging** (theorem, [source](#stmt-m-lem-outward-average)): Set $$u_{j+1}=\mathbb E[u'\mid\mathcal F_{j+1}],\qquad d_{j+1}=\mathbb E[\widetilde d\mid\mathcal F_{j+1}].$$ Then $u_{j+1},d_{j+1}$ satisfy <a href="#m:eq:outward-invariant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-invariant">[m:eq:outward-invariant]</a>–<a href="#m:eq:outward-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-bounds">[m:eq:outward-bounds]</a> at scale $r_{j+1}$, with conditional density $\mu_{j+1}$. They retain continuity and local $H^1$ regularity for the offset and bounded compact support for the nonnegative error density.
  - Explanation: Forgetting the current observation array preserves the invariant through density towers and convexity of the reaction, with regularity and error support maintained.
- **claim-molecular-total-propagation-error** (bound, [source](#stmt-m-lem-outward-error)): Starting with $u=b_{r_0}$ and $d_0=0$, the preceding construction reaches scale $r_n=r_*$ and produces a deterministic error density with $$\label{m:eq:outward-total-error} \int d_n\le C M r_*^{17-3w}=o(M) \qquad\text{along the contradiction sequence}.$$ The constant is independent of the number of scales and of the nuclear configuration.
  - Explanation: The expected spatial errors sum geometrically without a scale-count loss. The terminal error is deterministic and negligible relative to the nuclear count along the contradiction sequence.
- **claim-simultaneous-atomic-inverse-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-a-prop-cond-comparison)): There is a universal constant $C_{\rm cap}$ with the following property. Fix $L_1\ge2$, $0<h_l<h_h<\infty$, and a sufficiently small $\xi>0$; it suffices to require $\xi<h_l/16$. There exists $s_*=s_*(D_0,L_1,h_l,h_h,\xi)>0$ such that, whenever $0<s\le s_*$, at each $j<m$, with $r=r_j$, there is an event $\mathcal G_j\in\mathcal F_j$ satisfying $\mathbb P(\mathcal G_j^c)\le Cr^{25}$ on which, simultaneously for $r\le d_y\le4L_1r$ and $h_l\le h\le h_h$, $$\label{a:eq:cond-inverse} \begin{aligned} d_y^6\mu_j(y)>kh^{3/2} &\quad\Longrightarrow\quad d_y^4H_j(y)\ge h-\xi,\\ d_y^6\mu_j(y)<kh^{3/2} &\quad\Longrightarrow\quad d_y^4H_j(y)\le h+\xi. \end{aligned}$$ On the same event, $H_j(y)\le C_{\rm cap}d_y^{-4}$ throughout that band. The constant $C$ may depend on the fixed parameters, whereas $C_{\rm cap}$ does not depend on $D_0,L_1,h_l,h_h,\xi$. All estimates are uniform in $Z$, the ground state, $j$, and $m$.
  - Explanation: In nucleus-free atomic annuli, spatial and height nets upgrade the inverse density-field comparisons to a simultaneous event, with a universal positive field cap and parameter-dependent smallness thresholds.
- **claim-atomic-fixed-radius-deficit** (bound, [source](#stmt-a-prop-prop-deficit)): There is a universal constant $c_{\mathrm{def}}>0$ with the following property. For every $D_0\in[0,\infty)$ there is $s_*(D_0)>0$ such that, for each $0<s\le s_*(D_0)$, there is an integer $Z_*(D_0,s)$ for which every integer $Z\ge Z_*(D_0,s)$ and every normalized sector ground state $\Psi$ satisfying $$n\le3Z,\qquad q_n[\Psi]=E_n\le E+D_0$$ obey $$\label{a:eq:prop-deficit} Z-\mathbb E_\Psi\#\{i:\|x_i\|<s/4\} \ge c_{\mathrm{def}}s^{-3}>1.$$ All thresholds are independent of the sector and of the choice of its ground state. In particular, admissible fixed radii $s$ can be taken arbitrarily small.
  - Explanation: A bounded-offset sector ground state leaves a positive charge deficit inside every sufficiently small admissible fixed ball once the integer charge is sufficiently large. Thresholds are uniform over sectors and ground states.
- **claim-intermediate-atomic-subsolutions** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-atomic-intermediate)): There are universal constants $\varepsilon,B,C_{\rm inv}>0$ and $L_1>2$ with the following property. Fix $D_0<\infty$, then take $s>0$ sufficiently small and integer $Z$ sufficiently large as in Proposition <a href="#a:prop:prop-deficit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="a:prop:prop-deficit">46</a>. For every sector ground state in that proposition use the common observations and master packets <a href="#a:eq:cond-observations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="a:eq:cond-observations">[a:eq:cond-observations]</a>–<a href="#a:eq:cond-master" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="a:eq:cond-master">[a:eq:cond-master]</a>, with $r_0=\varepsilon Z^{-1/3}$ and $s/2<r_m\le s$. For each $0\le j\le m$ there exist jointly measurable $\mathcal F_j$-measurable fields $u_j$ and densities $p_j^{\rm err}$ with the following properties. Put $d=\|y\|$, $r=r_j$, and $f(t)=4\pi k(t_+)^{3/2}$, and define $$b_r(y)=Bd^{-4}\left(1-\frac r{8d}\right)\quad(d\ge r).$$ $$\label{a:eq:prop-invariant} \begin{split} \Delta u_j&\ge-4\pi\nu+4\pi\mathbf 1_{\{d<r\}}\mu_j -4\pi p_j^{\mathrm{err}}+\mathbf 1_{\{d\ge r\}}f(u_j),\\ b_r\le u_j&\le C_{\mathrm{inv}}d^{-4}\quad(d\ge r), \qquad u_j=b_r\quad(d>L_1r). \end{split}$$ The differential inequality is global in distributions. The offsets $u_j-V$ are continuous and locally Lipschitz on all of $\mathbb R^3$; $p_j^{\rm err}\ge0$ is bounded and supported in $\|y\|\le r_j$. For each fixed system and step, the offsets have deterministic size and Lipschitz bounds on every compact set; the errors have deterministic size bounds. Universally, $$\label{eq:atomic-prefix-error} \mathbb E\int p_j^{\rm err} \le C r_0^{32}+C\sum_{i<j}r_i^{19/2-3w} \le C\left(r_0^{32}+r_j^{19/2-3w}\right),\qquad w=10^{-5}.$$ In particular this is at most $C(s^{32}+s^{19/2-3w})$. The expectations cannot in general be omitted for $j<m$; the terminal fields are deterministic. The same master kernels are used at every intermediate stopping index. The constants $\varepsilon,B,C_{\rm inv},L_1$ are independent of any additional inverse-comparison parameters. Such a comparison uses these same observations whenever its own smallness condition on $s$ is met.
  - Explanation: The same master observations yield globally valid atomic subsolutions at every stopping index. Their positive barriers and exterior equalities persist; prefix error control is in expectation until the terminal data become trivial.
- **claim-conditional-inner-field-annular-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-a-lem-tail-harmonic)): Fix positive constants $\alpha<\alpha'<\beta'<\beta$ and $\alpha_0<\beta_0$. Let $u>0$, $0<h\le\alpha u/2$, and let $\psi$ be a normalized $\delta$-state with $n\le3Z$, where $\delta<\infty$ is arbitrary. Let $Y$ be the data from a cut whose out support lies in $\{\alpha_0u<\|x\|<\beta_0u\}$ and whose squared gradient cost is at most $C u^{-2}$. The cut may also be absent, in which case $Y$ is trivial. There is a jointly measurable version $$H_Y(x)=\mathbb E_\psi[B_h(x)\mid Y]$$ which is harmonic on $\{\alpha u<\|x\|<\beta u\}$ for almost every datum. Put $$M(Y)=\sup_{\alpha'u\le\|x\|\le\beta'u}(H_Y(x))_+.$$ Then $$\label{a:eq:tail-harmonic-bound} \\|M\\|_2\le C\left\{ \frac{\max(u^{-3},1)+\sqrt{\delta u}}{u} +\sum_{j=0}^\infty \frac{\max((2^jh)^{-3},1)+\sqrt{\delta\,2^jh}} {\max(u,2^jh)}\right\}.$$ The constant depends only on the fixed annular ratios and cut-gradient constant, in addition to the fixed screening constants $L,A$. In particular it does not depend on $n,Z,\delta,h,u$.
  - Explanation: Conditional inner fields have harmonic spatial versions on separated annuli, and their positive supremum is bounded by a convergent annular count series with explicit dependence on arbitrary finite energy offset.
- **claim-inner-deficit-upper-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-a-lem-tail-deficit-upper)): For each fixed $t>0$ and every normalized $\delta$-state with $n\le3Z$, $$\label{a:eq:tail-deficit-upper} Z-\mathbb E\#\{i:\|x_i\|<t/4\}\le C_t(1+\sqrt\delta).$$
  - Explanation: The spherical mean of the conditional inner field bounds the expected charge deficit in a fixed inner ball by the energy-offset-dependent harmonic estimate.
- **claim-neutral-and-ion-offset** (bound, [source](#stmt-a-prop-tail-offset)): There are universal constants $C,Z_0<\infty$ such that, for $Z\ge Z_0$, $$\label{a:eq:tail-offset} E_{Z-1}\le E+C.$$
  - Explanation: Deletion from a global sector minimizer places the singly ionized energy within a universal offset of the all-sector minimum. Monotonicity then gives the same eligibility for neutral ground states.
- **claim-positive-atomic-ionization-gap** (bound, [source](#stmt-a-prop-tail-gap)): There are universal constants $c>0$ and $Z_2<\infty$ such that $$\label{a:eq:tail-gap} E_{Z-1}-E_Z\ge c\qquad(Z\ge Z_2).$$
  - Explanation: A distant insertion trial averaged over centers recovers the singly ionized atom's residual charge and dominates its kinetic, hole and discarded-field costs, giving a positive universal removal gap.
- **claim-neutral-exterior-mass-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-a-prop-tail-mass)): There exist universal $C,R_0,Z_3<\infty$ such that every normalized neutral ground state with $Z\ge Z_3$ satisfies $$\label{a:eq:tail-mass} \int_{\|x\|>R}\rho_{\Psi_Z}(x)\,\,\mathrm dx\le\frac C R \qquad(R\ge R_0).$$
  - Explanation: The positive ionization gap and a shell-weighted law give a reciprocal exterior mass bound for every neutral ground state at sufficiently large charge.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.claims.v2 -->

```json
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-loc-coulomb",
        "exact_quote": "For compactly supported real $f\\in L^{5/3}(\\mathbb R^3)$, its Coulomb potential is continuous and belongs to $\\dot H^1$, and $$\\label{eq:loc-coulomb-duality}\n D(f)=\\frac1{8\\pi}\\|\\nabla(K*f)\\|_2^2,\n \\qquad\n \\left|\\int fv\\right|\\leq C\\sqrt{D(f)}\\|\\nabla v\\|_2\n \\quad(v\\in\\dot H^1).$$ The potential and energy assertions also hold for a nonnegative $f\\in L^{5/3}$ whose distribution belongs to $(\\dot H^1)^*$, with $K*f$ defined by its nonnegative convolution. Its dual pairing with any $v\\in\\dot H^1$ is the absolutely convergent integral $\\int fv$.*\n\n*If $\\eta$ is a radial nonnegative probability density supported in $B(0,b)$, then $K*\\eta\\leq K$, with equality outside that ball. In particular, $D(\\eta*\\rho)\\leq D(\\rho)$ for every nonnegative compactly supported $\\rho\\in L^{5/3}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-loc-coulomb",
          "exact_quote": "For compactly supported real $f\\in L^{5/3}(\\mathbb R^3)$, its Coulomb potential is continuous and belongs to $\\dot H^1$, and $$\\label{eq:loc-coulomb-duality}\n D(f)=\\frac1{8\\pi}\\|\\nabla(K*f)\\|_2^2,\n \\qquad\n \\left|\\int fv\\right|\\leq C\\sqrt{D(f)}\\|\\nabla v\\|_2\n \\quad(v\\in\\dot H^1).$$ The potential and energy assertions also hold for a nonnegative $f\\in L^{5/3}$ whose distribution belongs to $(\\dot H^1)^*$, with $K*f$ defined by its nonnegative convolution. Its dual pairing with any $v\\in\\dot H^1$ is the absolutely convergent integral $\\int fv$.*\n\n*If $\\eta$ is a radial nonnegative probability density supported in $B(0,b)$, then $K*\\eta\\leq K$, with equality outside that ball. In particular, $D(\\eta*\\rho)\\leq D(\\rho)$ for every nonnegative compactly supported $\\rho\\in L^{5/3}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-loc-kinetic",
        "exact_quote": "If $0\\leq\\gamma\\leq1$ has finite trace, then $$\\label{eq:loc-coarse-kinetic}\n \\operatorname{Tr}(-\\Delta/2)\\gamma\n \\geq c\\int\\rho_\\gamma^{5/3}$$ for a universal $c>0$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-loc-identity",
        "exact_quote": "With the preceding definitions, set $$T_o=\\frac12\\sum_I\\sum_{i\\in I}\\|\\nabla_i\\psi_I\\|_2^2.$$ There is a nonnegative localization error satisfying $$\\label{eq:loc-error}\n \\mathrm{IMS}\\leq\\frac12\\mathbb E_\\psi\\sum_i\\sum_h\n       \\big(|\\nabla s_h|^2+|\\nabla c_h|^2\\big)(x_i)$$ such that $$\\label{eq:loc-cut}\n q_n[\\psi]+\\mathrm{IMS}\n =\\mathbb Ee_X+T_o-\\mathbb E\\sum_{i\\ \\mathrm{out}}\\Phi_X(x_i)\n                   +\\mathbb E\\sum_{i<j\\ \\mathrm{out}}K(x_i-x_j).$$ The out kinetic energy satisfies $$T_o\\geq c\\int(o^2\\rho_\\psi)^{5/3},$$ where $\\rho_\\psi$ is the raw one-body density. Conditional quantities have versions jointly measurable in the data and the field evaluation point. Appending deterministic cuts admits a coupling in which these decorated data are nested, and hence conditional expectations of raw quantities satisfy the tower property.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-loc-hole-trial",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a conditional core slice with finite energy $e_X$ and field $\\Phi_X$ as above. Let $\\rho\\geq0$ belong to $L^{5/3}$ and have compact support. Suppose its packet region $\\operatorname{supp}\\rho+\\overline{B(0,b)}$ has positive distance from the spatial support allowed to the core particles. Then $$\\label{eq:loc-trial}\n E\\leq e_X+c_{\\mathrm{TF}}\\int\\rho^{5/3}\n       +Cb^{-2}\\int\\rho\n       -\\int\\Phi_X(g_b^2*\\rho)+D(\\rho).$$ The constant depends only on the fixed function $g$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-loc-cubes",
        "exact_quote": "In the setting of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:loc-identity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:loc-identity\">13</a>, let $n_o$ be the out particle count. Choose, measurably, any subset of the out particles and set $$\\sigma(x)=\\sum_{i\\ \\mathrm{retained}}\\vartheta_b(x-x_i).$$ Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n T_o&\\geq\\mathbb E\\left[c_{\\mathrm{TF}}\\int\\sigma^{5/3}\n               -Cb^{-2}(n_o^{4/3}+n_o)\\right],\n \\label{eq:loc-fine-kinetic}\\\\\n \\sum_{i<j\\ \\mathrm{out}}K(x_i-x_j)\n   &\\geq D(\\sigma)-Cn_o/b\n \\quad\\text{almost surely}.\n \\label{eq:loc-fine-pair}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-screen-scale",
        "exact_quote": "In the nuclear-scale geometry, $a:\\mathbb R^3\\to(0,\\infty)$ is $L^{-1}$-Lipschitz and satisfies $$\\label{m:eq:screen-scale-properties}\n Z^{-1/3}\\le a_y<\\infty,\\quad\n \\nu(B(y,La_y))\\le a_y^{-3},\\quad\n \\nu(B(y,2La_y))>(2a_y)^{-3},\\quad d_y\\le La_y.$$ If $d_y/L>1$, then $a_y=d_y/L$. For every fixed $c<L/2$, $B(y,ca_y)$ is covered by a bounded number of balls $B_z$ with $a_z\\asymp a_y$. The covering bound depends only on $c$ and $L$.",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-screen-scale",
          "exact_quote": "In the nuclear-scale geometry, $a:\\mathbb R^3\\to(0,\\infty)$ is $L^{-1}$-Lipschitz and satisfies $$\\label{m:eq:screen-scale-properties}\n Z^{-1/3}\\le a_y<\\infty,\\quad\n \\nu(B(y,La_y))\\le a_y^{-3},\\quad\n \\nu(B(y,2La_y))>(2a_y)^{-3},\\quad d_y\\le La_y.$$ If $d_y/L>1$, then $a_y=d_y/L$. For every fixed $c<L/2$, $B(y,ca_y)$ is covered by a bounded number of balls $B_z$ with $a_z\\asymp a_y$. The covering bound depends only on $c$ and $L$.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "In the nuclear-scale geometry, $a:\\mathbb R^3\\to(0,\\infty)$ is $L^{-1}$-Lipschitz and satisfies $$\\label{m:eq:screen-scale-properties}\n Z^{-1/3}\\le a_y<\\infty,\\quad\n \\nu(B(y,La_y))\\le a_y^{-3},\\quad\n \\nu(B(y,2La_y))>(2a_y)^{-3},\\quad d_y\\le La_y.$$ If $d_y/L>1$, then $a_y=d_y/L$. For every fixed $c<L/2$, $B(y,ca_y)$ is covered by a bounded number of balls $B_z$ with $a_z\\asymp a_y$. The covering bound depends only on $c$ and $L$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-a-lem-screen-provisional",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"m:lem:screen-positive-field\" label=\"m:lem:screen-positive-field\"></span>Fix $C_0\\ge1$. Suppose the initial sequence is absent, or its total out support is covered by at most $C_0$ balls $B_z$ and the sum of its cuts’ squared gradients is bounded by $$\\sum_z D_z^2a_z^{-2}\\mathbf 1_{B_z},\n \\qquad \\frac{D_z^2}{a_zm_z}\\le C_0\\sqrt P.$$ If $X$ denotes its data (trivial for an absent sequence), and $e_*$ is the maximum of $e_y$ and the energies $e_z$ of these covering cells, then $$\\label{m:eq:screen-positive}\n \\big\\|(\\mathbb E[J_y\\mid X])_+\\big\\|_2\n       \\le C(C_0)\\sqrt{\\frac{P e_*}{a_y}}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-a-lem-screen-provisional",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"m:lem:screen-positive-field\" label=\"m:lem:screen-positive-field\"></span>Fix $C_0\\ge1$. Suppose the initial sequence is absent, or its total out support is covered by at most $C_0$ balls $B_z$ and the sum of its cuts’ squared gradients is bounded by $$\\sum_z D_z^2a_z^{-2}\\mathbf 1_{B_z},\n \\qquad \\frac{D_z^2}{a_zm_z}\\le C_0\\sqrt P.$$ If $X$ denotes its data (trivial for an absent sequence), and $e_*$ is the maximum of $e_y$ and the energies $e_z$ of these covering cells, then $$\\label{m:eq:screen-positive}\n \\big\\|(\\mathbb E[J_y\\mid X])_+\\big\\|_2\n       \\le C(C_0)\\sqrt{\\frac{P e_*}{a_y}}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"m:lem:screen-positive-field\" label=\"m:lem:screen-positive-field\"></span>Fix $C_0\\ge1$. Suppose the initial sequence is absent, or its total out support is covered by at most $C_0$ balls $B_z$ and the sum of its cuts’ squared gradients is bounded by $$\\sum_z D_z^2a_z^{-2}\\mathbf 1_{B_z},\n \\qquad \\frac{D_z^2}{a_zm_z}\\le C_0\\sqrt P.$$ If $X$ denotes its data (trivial for an absent sequence), and $e_*$ is the maximum of $e_y$ and the energies $e_z$ of these covering cells, then $$\\label{m:eq:screen-positive}\n \\big\\|(\\mathbb E[J_y\\mid X])_+\\big\\|_2\n       \\le C(C_0)\\sqrt{\\frac{P e_*}{a_y}}.$$",
      "explanation": "A finite admissible initial cut cannot produce an excessively large positive conditional far field. The bound retains the largest energy unit among the target and covering cells.",

      "id": "claim-conditional-positive-field",

    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-a-lem-screen-patch",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"m:lem:screen-patch\" label=\"m:lem:screen-patch\"></span>Fix $y\\in\\mathcal Y$, write $a=a_y$, $m=m_y$, $e=e_y$, and choose $0<\\beta<1/100$ such that $$\\label{m:eq:screen-patch-condition}\n \\frac{\\beta^{-2}}{am}\\le\\sqrt P.$$ Set $b=\\beta a$. There is a deterministic $t_0\\in[5a,6a]$ and a cut full out on $B(y,t_0)$, supported in $B(y,t_0+b)$, with gradients at most $C/b$. Let $X$ be its out data, $\\rho_X^c$ the conditional core density, $\\Phi_X=V-K*\\rho_X^c$, and $\\Omega=B(y,t_0-4b)$. There is a unique minimizer $\\rho=\\rho_X\\ge0$, extended by zero outside $\\Omega$, of $$\\label{m:eq:screen-tf-functional}\n \\mathcal T_X(\\rho)=c_{\\mathrm{TF}}\\int\\rho^{5/3}-\\int\\Phi_X\\rho+D(\\rho).$$ It is measurable in $X$ and, with $W=\\Phi_X-K*\\rho$, satisfies $$\\label{m:eq:screen-tf-equation}\n \\rho=kW_+^{3/2}\\quad\\hbox{in }\\Omega.$$ Retain the out particles with $|x_i-y|<t_0-7b$ and put $\\sigma=\\sum_{i\\ \\mathrm{retained}}\\vartheta_b(\\cdot-x_i)$, using the radial fine kernel from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:loc-cubes\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:loc-cubes\">15</a>. Write $$\\label{m:eq:screen-remainder}\n \\mathcal L=c_{\\mathrm{TF}}\\int\\left(\\sigma^{5/3}-\\rho^{5/3}\n              -\\frac53\\rho^{2/3}(\\sigma-\\rho)\\right).$$ For every sufficiently small $\\epsilon>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{m:eq:screen-comparison}\n &\\mathbb E\\left[D(\\sigma-\\rho)+\\mathcal L+\\int W_-\\sigma\\right]\\\\\n &\\quad\\le C\\left[\\delta+(\\epsilon+\\sqrt\\beta)Pe\n       +\\epsilon^{-3/2}\\beta^{1/2}a^{-7}\n       +b^{-1}\\sqrt Pm\n       +b^{-2}\\left(P^{2/3}m^{4/3}+\\sqrt Pm\\right)\\right].\\notag\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $\\mathbb E\\int\\sigma^{5/3}$ is bounded by $CPe$ plus a constant times this right-hand side, and the out kinetic energy obeys $$\\label{m:eq:screen-out-kinetic}\n T_o\\le C\\left(Pe+b^{-2}\\sqrt Pm\\right).$$ Finally, independently of the data, $$\\label{m:eq:screen-interior-upper}\n W\\le V_{\\rm loc}+Ca^{-4}\\quad\\hbox{on }B(y,3a),\\qquad\n \\int_{B(y,2a)}\\rho\\le Ca^{-3},$$ where $V_{\\rm loc}$ is the potential of nuclei in $B(y,20a)$. If this local nuclear measure is zero, as it always is in the atomic distance geometry, the same construction has the sharper bound $$\\label{a:eq:screen-patch}\n \\mathbb E\\left[D(\\sigma-\\rho)+\\mathcal L+\\int W_-\\sigma\\right]\n \\le C\\left[\\delta+\\sqrt\\beta Pe+b^{-1}\\sqrt Pm\n       +b^{-2}\\left(P^{2/3}m^{4/3}+\\sqrt Pm\\right)\\right],$$ and its interior bounds include $$\\label{a:eq:screen-interior}\n W\\le Ca^{-4},\\qquad \\rho\\le Ca^{-6}\n       \\quad\\hbox{on }B(y,3a),\\qquad\n \\int_{B(y,2a)}\\rho\\le Ca^{-3}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-a-lem-screen-patch",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"m:lem:screen-patch\" label=\"m:lem:screen-patch\"></span>Fix $y\\in\\mathcal Y$, write $a=a_y$, $m=m_y$, $e=e_y$, and choose $0<\\beta<1/100$ such that $$\\label{m:eq:screen-patch-condition}\n \\frac{\\beta^{-2}}{am}\\le\\sqrt P.$$ Set $b=\\beta a$. There is a deterministic $t_0\\in[5a,6a]$ and a cut full out on $B(y,t_0)$, supported in $B(y,t_0+b)$, with gradients at most $C/b$. Let $X$ be its out data, $\\rho_X^c$ the conditional core density, $\\Phi_X=V-K*\\rho_X^c$, and $\\Omega=B(y,t_0-4b)$. There is a unique minimizer $\\rho=\\rho_X\\ge0$, extended by zero outside $\\Omega$, of $$\\label{m:eq:screen-tf-functional}\n \\mathcal T_X(\\rho)=c_{\\mathrm{TF}}\\int\\rho^{5/3}-\\int\\Phi_X\\rho+D(\\rho).$$ It is measurable in $X$ and, with $W=\\Phi_X-K*\\rho$, satisfies $$\\label{m:eq:screen-tf-equation}\n \\rho=kW_+^{3/2}\\quad\\hbox{in }\\Omega.$$ Retain the out particles with $|x_i-y|<t_0-7b$ and put $\\sigma=\\sum_{i\\ \\mathrm{retained}}\\vartheta_b(\\cdot-x_i)$, using the radial fine kernel from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:loc-cubes\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:loc-cubes\">15</a>. Write $$\\label{m:eq:screen-remainder}\n \\mathcal L=c_{\\mathrm{TF}}\\int\\left(\\sigma^{5/3}-\\rho^{5/3}\n              -\\frac53\\rho^{2/3}(\\sigma-\\rho)\\right).$$ For every sufficiently small $\\epsilon>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{m:eq:screen-comparison}\n &\\mathbb E\\left[D(\\sigma-\\rho)+\\mathcal L+\\int W_-\\sigma\\right]\\\\\n &\\quad\\le C\\left[\\delta+(\\epsilon+\\sqrt\\beta)Pe\n       +\\epsilon^{-3/2}\\beta^{1/2}a^{-7}\n       +b^{-1}\\sqrt Pm\n       +b^{-2}\\left(P^{2/3}m^{4/3}+\\sqrt Pm\\right)\\right].\\notag\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $\\mathbb E\\int\\sigma^{5/3}$ is bounded by $CPe$ plus a constant times this right-hand side, and the out kinetic energy obeys $$\\label{m:eq:screen-out-kinetic}\n T_o\\le C\\left(Pe+b^{-2}\\sqrt Pm\\right).$$ Finally, independently of the data, $$\\label{m:eq:screen-interior-upper}\n W\\le V_{\\rm loc}+Ca^{-4}\\quad\\hbox{on }B(y,3a),\\qquad\n \\int_{B(y,2a)}\\rho\\le Ca^{-3},$$ where $V_{\\rm loc}$ is the potential of nuclei in $B(y,20a)$. If this local nuclear measure is zero, as it always is in the atomic distance geometry, the same construction has the sharper bound $$\\label{a:eq:screen-patch}\n \\mathbb E\\left[D(\\sigma-\\rho)+\\mathcal L+\\int W_-\\sigma\\right]\n \\le C\\left[\\delta+\\sqrt\\beta Pe+b^{-1}\\sqrt Pm\n       +b^{-2}\\left(P^{2/3}m^{4/3}+\\sqrt Pm\\right)\\right],$$ and its interior bounds include $$\\label{a:eq:screen-interior}\n W\\le Ca^{-4},\\qquad \\rho\\le Ca^{-6}\n       \\quad\\hbox{on }B(y,3a),\\qquad\n \\int_{B(y,2a)}\\rho\\le Ca^{-3}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"m:lem:screen-patch\" label=\"m:lem:screen-patch\"></span>Fix $y\\in\\mathcal Y$, write $a=a_y$, $m=m_y$, $e=e_y$, and choose $0<\\beta<1/100$ such that $$\\label{m:eq:screen-patch-condition}\n \\frac{\\beta^{-2}}{am}\\le\\sqrt P.$$ Set $b=\\beta a$. There is a deterministic $t_0\\in[5a,6a]$ and a cut full out on $B(y,t_0)$, supported in $B(y,t_0+b)$, with gradients at most $C/b$. Let $X$ be its out data, $\\rho_X^c$ the conditional core density, $\\Phi_X=V-K*\\rho_X^c$, and $\\Omega=B(y,t_0-4b)$. There is a unique minimizer $\\rho=\\rho_X\\ge0$, extended by zero outside $\\Omega$, of $$\\label{m:eq:screen-tf-functional}\n \\mathcal T_X(\\rho)=c_{\\mathrm{TF}}\\int\\rho^{5/3}-\\int\\Phi_X\\rho+D(\\rho).$$ It is measurable in $X$ and, with $W=\\Phi_X-K*\\rho$, satisfies $$\\label{m:eq:screen-tf-equation}\n \\rho=kW_+^{3/2}\\quad\\hbox{in }\\Omega.$$ Retain the out particles with $|x_i-y|<t_0-7b$ and put $\\sigma=\\sum_{i\\ \\mathrm{retained}}\\vartheta_b(\\cdot-x_i)$, using the radial fine kernel from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:loc-cubes\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:loc-cubes\">15</a>. Write $$\\label{m:eq:screen-remainder}\n \\mathcal L=c_{\\mathrm{TF}}\\int\\left(\\sigma^{5/3}-\\rho^{5/3}\n              -\\frac53\\rho^{2/3}(\\sigma-\\rho)\\right).$$ For every sufficiently small $\\epsilon>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{m:eq:screen-comparison}\n &\\mathbb E\\left[D(\\sigma-\\rho)+\\mathcal L+\\int W_-\\sigma\\right]\\\\\n &\\quad\\le C\\left[\\delta+(\\epsilon+\\sqrt\\beta)Pe\n       +\\epsilon^{-3/2}\\beta^{1/2}a^{-7}\n       +b^{-1}\\sqrt Pm\n       +b^{-2}\\left(P^{2/3}m^{4/3}+\\sqrt Pm\\right)\\right].\\notag\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $\\mathbb E\\int\\sigma^{5/3}$ is bounded by $CPe$ plus a constant times this right-hand side, and the out kinetic energy obeys $$\\label{m:eq:screen-out-kinetic}\n T_o\\le C\\left(Pe+b^{-2}\\sqrt Pm\\right).$$ Finally, independently of the data, $$\\label{m:eq:screen-interior-upper}\n W\\le V_{\\rm loc}+Ca^{-4}\\quad\\hbox{on }B(y,3a),\\qquad\n \\int_{B(y,2a)}\\rho\\le Ca^{-3},$$ where $V_{\\rm loc}$ is the potential of nuclei in $B(y,20a)$. If this local nuclear measure is zero, as it always is in the atomic distance geometry, the same construction has the sharper bound $$\\label{a:eq:screen-patch}\n \\mathbb E\\left[D(\\sigma-\\rho)+\\mathcal L+\\int W_-\\sigma\\right]\n \\le C\\left[\\delta+\\sqrt\\beta Pe+b^{-1}\\sqrt Pm\n       +b^{-2}\\left(P^{2/3}m^{4/3}+\\sqrt Pm\\right)\\right],$$ and its interior bounds include $$\\label{a:eq:screen-interior}\n W\\le Ca^{-4},\\qquad \\rho\\le Ca^{-6}\n       \\quad\\hbox{on }B(y,3a),\\qquad\n \\int_{B(y,2a)}\\rho\\le Ca^{-3}.$$",
      "explanation": "A deterministic shell cut produces a unique measurable local Thomas–Fermi minimizer. Matching upper and lower energy comparisons control Coulomb discrepancy, convex remainder and negative-field penalty; nucleus-free patches have a sharper comparison.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-screen-count",
        "exact_quote": "For every normalized $\\delta$-state in any finite sector and every $y\\in\\mathcal Y$, $$\\label{m:eq:screen-count}\n \\mathbb En_y^2\\le C\\left(\\max(a_y^{-6},1)+\\delta a_y\\right).$$ In particular the number $P$ in <a href=\"#m:eq:screen-count-normalization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:screen-count-normalization\">[m:eq:screen-count-normalization]</a> is at most a universal constant.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-a-prop-screen-local",
        "exact_quote": "In either geometry, every normalized $\\delta$-state in a finite sector satisfies $P\\le C$. Fix $y\\in\\mathcal Y$. Suppose the initial finite sequence of cuts has total out support covered by at most $C_0$ cells $B_z$, with $C_0^{-1}\\le a_z/a_y\\le C_0$, and satisfies the gradient hypothesis of Lemma <a href=\"#m:lem:screen-positive-field\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"m:lem:screen-positive-field\">[m:lem:screen-positive-field]</a>. The absent-cut case is allowed. Then $$\\label{a:eq:screen-local}\n P\\le C,\\qquad\n \\big\\|(\\mathbb E[J_y\\mid X])_+\\big\\|_2\n             \\le C(C_0)\\frac{m_y}{a_y}.$$ Neither constant depends on the nuclear system, particle number, state, target, or $\\delta$.",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

The absent-cut case is allowed. Then $$\\label{a:eq:screen-local}\n P\\le C,\\qquad\n \\big\\|(\\mathbb E[J_y\\mid X])_+\\big\\|_2\n             \\le C(C_0)\\frac{m_y}{a_y}.$$ Neither constant depends on the nuclear system, particle number, state, target, or $\\delta$.",

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-a-cor-screen-refined",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"m:lem:screen-density\" label=\"m:lem:screen-density\"></span>There is a universal $a_0>0$ with the following property. If $a=a_y<a_0$ and $\\psi$ is a $\\delta$-state with $\\delta\\le a^{-7+0.02}$, choose the patch of Lemma <a href=\"#m:lem:screen-patch\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"m:lem:screen-patch\">[m:lem:screen-patch]</a> with $b=a^{1+0.2}$. Its densities obey $$\\label{m:eq:screen-fine-error}\n \\mathbb E\\left[D(\\sigma-\\rho)+\\mathcal L+\\int W_-\\sigma\\right]\n       \\le Ca^{-7+0.02},\\qquad\n \\mathbb E\\int\\sigma^{5/3}\\le Ca^{-7}.$$ The raw one-body density satisfies $$\\label{m:eq:screen-density}\n \\int_{B(y,4a)}\\rho_\\psi^{5/3}\\le Ca^{-7}.$$ In particular, the two estimates needed in the atomic application are $$\\label{a:eq:screen-refined}\n \\mathbb E\\left[D(\\sigma-\\rho)+\\int W_-\\sigma\\right]\\le Ca^{-7+.02},\n \\qquad \\int_{B(y,4a)}\\rho_\\psi^{5/3}\\le Ca^{-7}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-a-cor-screen-refined",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"m:lem:screen-density\" label=\"m:lem:screen-density\"></span>There is a universal $a_0>0$ with the following property. If $a=a_y<a_0$ and $\\psi$ is a $\\delta$-state with $\\delta\\le a^{-7+0.02}$, choose the patch of Lemma <a href=\"#m:lem:screen-patch\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"m:lem:screen-patch\">[m:lem:screen-patch]</a> with $b=a^{1+0.2}$. Its densities obey $$\\label{m:eq:screen-fine-error}\n \\mathbb E\\left[D(\\sigma-\\rho)+\\mathcal L+\\int W_-\\sigma\\right]\n       \\le Ca^{-7+0.02},\\qquad\n \\mathbb E\\int\\sigma^{5/3}\\le Ca^{-7}.$$ The raw one-body density satisfies $$\\label{m:eq:screen-density}\n \\int_{B(y,4a)}\\rho_\\psi^{5/3}\\le Ca^{-7}.$$ In particular, the two estimates needed in the atomic application are $$\\label{a:eq:screen-refined}\n \\mathbb E\\left[D(\\sigma-\\rho)+\\int W_-\\sigma\\right]\\le Ca^{-7+.02},\n \\qquad \\int_{B(y,4a)}\\rho_\\psi^{5/3}\\le Ca^{-7}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"

      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"m:lem:screen-density\" label=\"m:lem:screen-density\"></span>There is a universal $a_0>0$ with the following property. Its densities obey $$\\label{m:eq:screen-fine-error}\n \\mathbb E\\left[D(\\sigma-\\rho)+\\mathcal L+\\int W_-\\sigma\\right]\n       \\le Ca^{-7+0.02},\\qquad\n \\mathbb E\\int\\sigma^{5/3}\\le Ca^{-7}.$$ The raw one-body density satisfies $$\\label{m:eq:screen-density}\n \\int_{B(y,4a)}\\rho_\\psi^{5/3}\\le Ca^{-7}.$$ In particular, the two estimates needed in the atomic application are $$\\label{a:eq:screen-refined}\n \\mathbb E\\left[D(\\sigma-\\rho)+\\int W_-\\sigma\\right]\\le Ca^{-7+.02},\n \\qquad \\int_{B(y,4a)}\\rho_\\psi^{5/3}\\le Ca^{-7}.$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-a-lem-screen-oscillation",
        "exact_quote": "For every patch in Lemma <a href=\"#a:lem:screen-patch\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:lem:screen-patch\">18</a> with $\\nu(B(y,20a))=0$, and every $0<s'\\le a/2$, almost surely in its data, $$\\label{a:eq:screen-oscillation}\n \\sup_{x\\in B(y,s')}|W(x)-W(y)|\n       \\le C\\frac{s'}a\\bigl(a^{-4}+W(y)_-\\bigr).$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-a-cor-screen-annular",
        "exact_quote": "In the atomic distance geometry, for every $0<\\lambda<\\Lambda<\\infty$ and $u>0$, every normalized $\\delta$-state of finite particle number satisfies $$\\label{a:eq:screen-annular}\n \\left\\|\\#\\{i:\\lambda u<|x_i|<\\Lambda u\\}\\right\\|_2\n \\le C_{\\lambda,\\Lambda}\n       \\bigl(\\max(u^{-3},1)+\\sqrt{\\delta u}\\bigr).$$ All constants are independent of $Z$ and of the particle number.",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-a-cor-screen-annular",
          "exact_quote": "In the atomic distance geometry, for every $0<\\lambda<\\Lambda<\\infty$ and $u>0$, every normalized $\\delta$-state of finite particle number satisfies $$\\label{a:eq:screen-annular}\n \\left\\|\\#\\{i:\\lambda u<|x_i|<\\Lambda u\\}\\right\\|_2\n \\le C_{\\lambda,\\Lambda}\n       \\bigl(\\max(u^{-3},1)+\\sqrt{\\delta u}\\bigr).$$ All constants are independent of $Z$ and of the particle number.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "In the atomic distance geometry, for every $0<\\lambda<\\Lambda<\\infty$ and $u>0$, every normalized $\\delta$-state of finite particle number satisfies $$\\label{a:eq:screen-annular}\n \\left\\|\\#\\{i:\\lambda u<|x_i|<\\Lambda u\\}\\right\\|_2\n \\le C_{\\lambda,\\Lambda}\n       \\bigl(\\max(u^{-3},1)+\\sqrt{\\delta u}\\bigr).$$ All constants are independent of $Z$ and of the particle number.",
      "explanation": "A comparable-cell cover controls annular particle counts for arbitrary finite offsets, uniformly in charge and particle number.",

      "hypotheses": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-obs-data",
        "exact_quote": "There are jointly Borel versions of $$\\mu_j(y)=\\mathbb E_P\\!\\left[\\sum_{i=1}^n\\mathcal K_{x_i}(y)\n                                      \\mid\\mathcal F_j\\right],\n \\qquad H_j(y)=V_s(y)-(K*\\mu_j)(y),$$ with the following properties. At every positive-density data tuple, $\\mu_j\\ge0$, $\\int\\mu_j=n$, and $\\mu_j$ is smooth in its spatial variable. Every spatial derivative has a deterministic bound for the fixed system. The potential $K*\\mu_j$ is $C^1$, with bounded value and gradient, and $$\\label{eq:obs-poisson}\n \\Delta H_j=-4\\pi\\nu_s+4\\pi\\mu_j$$ in distributions. In particular, the always valid regularity statement is that $H_j-V_s$ is $C^1$ and bounded with bounded gradient. If $V_s$ and its gradient are bounded and continuous, these conclusions hold for $H_j$ itself. Conditional averaging satisfies $$\\label{eq:obs-tower}\n \\mathbb E_P[\\mu_j(y)\\mid\\mathcal F_{j+1}]=\\mu_{j+1}(y),\\qquad\n \\mathbb E_P[H_j(y)\\mid\\mathcal F_{j+1}]=H_{j+1}(y)$$ where $V_s(y)$ is finite. The same towers hold for nonnegative spatial integrals, and for integrable signed observables. These statements include the trivial data at $j=m$. None of the fixed-system regularity bounds is asserted to be uniform in the nuclear system or in $t_{\\min}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-obs-tilt",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\Psi$ is an eigenstate of its sector Hamiltonian with energy $e_\\Psi$, where $0\\le e_\\Psi-E\\le D_0$. In particular, $e_\\Psi=E_n$ for a sector ground state. Suppose further that $$\\sum_{h=j}^{m-1}\\ell_h^{-2}\\le C_\\ell\\ell_j^{-2}$$ with a fixed numerical $C_\\ell$. This holds for the dyadic scales $\\ell_h=r_h^{1.01}$, $r_{h+1}=2r_h$, with $C_\\ell=(1-2^{-2.02})^{-1}$. If $A\\in\\mathcal F_j$ has probability $p=P(A)>0$, put $p_A(x)=P(A\\mid x)$ and $$\\Psi_A=\\sqrt{p_A(x)/p}\\,\\Psi.$$ Then $\\Psi_A$ is a normalized antisymmetric form-domain vector, its raw position-and-spin law is the raw marginal of $P$ conditioned on $A$, and $$\\label{eq:obs-tilt}\n q_n[\\Psi_A]\\le e_\\Psi+C\\ell_j^{-2}\\log^5(e/p)\n       \\le E+D_0+C\\ell_j^{-2}\\log^5(e/p).$$ The constant depends only on $C_\\ell$ and the chosen noise law, not on $n$, the number of arrays, or the nuclear system. The same assertion holds for an event of one unordered observation at any width $\\ell$, using $\\ell$ in place of $\\ell_j$. No upper bound on the particle number in terms of the nuclear charge is needed.",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-obs-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a point $y$ where $V_s(y)$ is finite and a length $a>0$. Let $A\\in\\mathcal F_j$ have positive $P$-probability, and let $Q$ be $P$ conditioned on $A$, extended by fresh localization labels. Its raw marginal is the law of $\\Psi_A$ from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:obs-tilt\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:obs-tilt\">26</a>. Let $X$ denote the fresh patch data, and let $\\rho_X^c$ be the conditional density of core particles under this tilted localization law. Partition the remaining raw particles into retained and deleted particles. Let $$\\sigma=\\sum_{i\\ {\\rm retained}}\\vartheta_b(\\cdot-x_i),\\qquad\n h_X=V_s-K*\\rho_X^c-K*\\rho_X,\\qquad W_X=V_c+h_X,$$ where $\\vartheta_b$ is the radial probability kernel of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:loc-cubes\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:loc-cubes\">15</a>, supported in $B(0,\\sqrt3b)$, and $\\rho_X\\ge0$ is a measurably chosen patch comparison density. Assume $\\rho_X$ and $\\sigma$ belong to $L^{5/3}$ and have compact support for almost every patch datum. Put $t=t_y$, $\\tau=t/a$, and $\\beta=b/a$, and suppose $0<t,b\\le a/10$. Assume the following estimates, with fixed constants:*\n\n1.  *The master width $z\\mapsto t_z$ is Lipschitz with constant at most $1/2$. The raw one-particle density $\\rho_{\\Psi_A}$ satisfies $$\\int_{B(y,4a)}\\rho_{\\Psi_A}^{5/3}\\le Ca^{-7}.$$*\n\n2.  *Deleted particles have $|x_i-y|\\ge ca$, and their count obeys $\\mathbb E_Q n_{\\rm del}\\le Ca^{-3}\\sqrt\\beta$.*\n\n3.  *For some $\\varepsilon_a\\ge0$, $$\\mathbb E_Q D(\\rho_X-\\sigma)\\le Ca^{-7}\\varepsilon_a^2.$$*\n\n4.  *On $B(y,t)$ either the source-free bound $\\rho_X\\le Ca^{-6}$ holds, or $$\\rho_X=k(W_X)_+^{3/2},\\qquad\n     W_X\\le V_{\\rm loc}+Ca^{-4},\\qquad\n     V_{\\rm loc}(z)=\\sum_\\alpha\\frac{q_\\alpha}{|z-R_\\alpha|},\n     \\qquad\\sum_\\alpha q_\\alpha\\le Ca^{-3},$$ with $q_\\alpha\\ge0$. The latter alternative permits arbitrary locations of the local nuclei.*\n\n\n\n*Then the two expectation towers are $$\\label{eq:obs-two-towers}\n \\mathbb E_Q(K*\\mu_j)(y)=\\mathbb E_Q P^{\\rm ms}(y),\\qquad\n \\mathbb E_Q(K*\\rho_X^c)(y)=\\mathbb E_Q P^c(y),$$ where $P^{\\rm ms}$ is the potential of the raw master packets and $P^c$ is the raw core potential. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:obs-transfer}\n \\begin{split}\n |\\mathbb E_Q(H_j-h_X)(y)|\\le Ca^{-4}\\big[&\n    \\tau^{1/5}+\\beta^{1/5}+\\sqrt\\beta\n      +\\varepsilon_a\\tau^{-1/2}\\\\\n    &+R(\\tau)+(\\tau+\\sqrt3\\beta)^{1/5}\\big],\n \\end{split}$$ where $R(\\tau)=\\tau^2$ in the source-free alternative and $R(\\tau)=\\tau^2+\\tau^{1/2}$ in the nuclear alternative. Thus the common upper bound $R(\\tau)\\le C\\tau^{1/2}$ is always available. All constants are independent of the number of particles; they depend only on the constants in the displayed hypotheses.",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-obs-transfer",

      "explanation": "Separate expectation towers under the original observation posterior and the fresh tilted patch posterior yield an averaged field transfer. The estimate requires all displayed density, deletion, Coulomb-error and kernel hypotheses and does not identify posteriors pathwise.",
```
