    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-maximum",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal O\\subset\\mathbb R^3$ be open, let $g$ be locally bounded, and let $F(y,t)$ be measurable in $y$, continuous in $t$, and bounded on compact spatial sets and bounded ranges of $t$. If finitely many functions $v_i\\in C(\\mathcal O)\\cap H^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\mathcal O)$ satisfy $$\\Delta v_i\\ge g+F(y,v_i)$$ in distributions, their maximum satisfies the same inequality with its own value in the reaction term. The assertion also holds for functions $v_i=V+a_i$ with continuous locally $H^1$ offsets $a_i$, when all inequalities have the common singular term $-4\\pi\\nu$ and the reaction vanishes in a neighborhood of $\\operatorname{supp}\\nu$.",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-maximum",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal O\\subset\\mathbb R^3$ be open, let $g$ be locally bounded, and let $F(y,t)$ be measurable in $y$, continuous in $t$, and bounded on compact spatial sets and bounded ranges of $t$. If finitely many functions $v_i\\in C(\\mathcal O)\\cap H^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\mathcal O)$ satisfy $$\\Delta v_i\\ge g+F(y,v_i)$$ in distributions, their maximum satisfies the same inequality with its own value in the reaction term. The assertion also holds for functions $v_i=V+a_i$ with continuous locally $H^1$ offsets $a_i$, when all inequalities have the common singular term $-4\\pi\\nu$ and the reaction vanishes in a neighborhood of $\\operatorname{supp}\\nu$.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\mathcal O\\subset\\mathbb R^3$ be open, let $g$ be locally bounded, and let $F(y,t)$ be measurable in $y$, continuous in $t$, and bounded on compact spatial sets and bounded ranges of $t$.         "exact_quote": "There are universal constants $t_0,C>0$ with the following property. Let $O\\in\\mathbb R^3$, $t\\ge t_0$ and $T=2^Jt$, where $J\\ge1$ is an integer.         "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $\\zeta$ be as above, with total mass $Q>0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-barrier-mixture",
        "exact_quote": "For one point charge $Q>0$ at $R$, the field in Lemma <a href=\"#m:lem:barrier-global-tf\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"m:lem:barrier-global-tf\">31</a> satisfies $$\\label{m:eq:single-field-lower}\n W(x)\\ge c\\min\\left(\\frac{Q}{|x-R|},|x-R|^{-4}\\right).$$ For a single point charge or radial smooth charge of mass $Q$, its screening potential is bounded by $CQ^{4/3}$.*\n\n*More generally, select source components of masses $Q_i$, truncate them to masses $q_i\\le Q_i$, and put $q=\\sum_iq_i>0$. Let $W_i$ be the single-source field of mass $q$ at the $i$th center with that source’s normalized profile. If $W$ is the full-source field, then $$\\label{m:eq:tf-mixture}\n \\sum_i\\frac{q_i}{q}W_i\\le W.$$",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-barrier-kernel",
        "exact_quote": "Uniformly as $r_*\\downarrow0$, a center contributing to $Rf(y)$ satisfies $|z-y|\\le2t_y$ and $t_z/t_y=1+O(r_*^w)$. Moreover, $$\\label{m:eq:master-kernel-bounds}\n R1(y)=1+O(r_*^w),\\qquad\n \\|\\nabla_z\\mathcal K_z(y)\\|_\\infty\\le Ct_y^{-4},\\qquad\n \\|\\nabla_z\\mathcal K_z(y)\\|_2\\le Ct_y^{-5/2}.$$ The norms in these gradient estimates are taken in the center variable $z$. The operator $R$ is bounded on $L^{5/3}$ and preserves integrals. The correction $$\\label{m:eq:potential-correction}\n q_0=K*(\\rho_0-R\\rho_0)$$ is nonnegative, continuous, belongs to $\\dot H^1$, and tends to zero at infinity. It satisfies $$\\label{m:eq:potential-correction-bound}\n 0\\le q_0(y)\\le Ca_y^{-4}\\tau_y^{1/2}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },

The operator $R$ is bounded on $L^{5/3}$ and preserves integrals. The correction $$\\label{m:eq:potential-correction}\n q_0=K*(\\rho_0-R\\rho_0)$$ is nonnegative, continuous, belongs to $\\dot H^1$, and tends to zero at infinity. It satisfies $$\\label{m:eq:potential-correction-bound}\n 0\\le q_0(y)\\le Ca_y^{-4}\\tau_y^{1/2}.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-barrier-subsolution",
        "exact_quote": "There are fixed positive constants $\\alpha,\\epsilon_0,C'_0,c_b,c'$ with the following properties for all sufficiently small $r_*>0$. For $0<r\\le r_*$ define $$\\label{m:eq:barrier-definition}\n \\Gamma_r=\\epsilon_0W_s+C'_0r^{4\\alpha}W_s^{1+\\alpha},\\qquad\n B_r=W_0+q_0-\\Gamma_r,\\qquad b_r=B_r-V_c,\n \\qquad h_b=h_0-c_ba^{-4}.$$ The offsets $b_r$ are continuous and locally $H^1$ and decay at infinity. On $\\{a\\ge r\\}$ their Laplacian densities satisfy $$\\label{m:eq:barrier-subsolution}\n \\Delta b_r\\ge-4\\pi\\nu_s+f_y(b_r).$$ More precisely, this inequality holds almost everywhere there between the locally integrable densities representing the two sides; on every open set contained in this region it is also a distributional inequality. On the same region, $$\\label{m:eq:barrier-bounds}\n B_r\\ge0.9W_0>0,\\qquad\n h_b+c'a^{-4}\\le b_r\\le Ca^{-4}.$$ For every contributing center $z$ in <a href=\"#m:eq:offset-reaction\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:offset-reaction\">[m:eq:offset-reaction]</a>, $$\\label{m:eq:reaction-positive-margin}\n V_c(z)+h_b(y)\\ge ca_y^{-4}\n \\quad\\text{almost everywhere in }z.$$ All these constants are independent of $r,r_*$ and the nuclear system.",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-barrier-subsolution",
          "exact_quote": "There are fixed positive constants $\\alpha,\\epsilon_0,C'_0,c_b,c'$ with the following properties for all sufficiently small $r_*>0$. For $0<r\\le r_*$ define $$\\label{m:eq:barrier-definition}\n \\Gamma_r=\\epsilon_0W_s+C'_0r^{4\\alpha}W_s^{1+\\alpha},\\qquad\n B_r=W_0+q_0-\\Gamma_r,\\qquad b_r=B_r-V_c,\n \\qquad h_b=h_0-c_ba^{-4}.$$ The offsets $b_r$ are continuous and locally $H^1$ and decay at infinity. On $\\{a\\ge r\\}$ their Laplacian densities satisfy $$\\label{m:eq:barrier-subsolution}\n \\Delta b_r\\ge-4\\pi\\nu_s+f_y(b_r).$$ More precisely, this inequality holds almost everywhere there between the locally integrable densities representing the two sides; on every open set contained in this region it is also a distributional inequality. On the same region, $$\\label{m:eq:barrier-bounds}\n B_r\\ge0.9W_0>0,\\qquad\n h_b+c'a^{-4}\\le b_r\\le Ca^{-4}.$$ For every contributing center $z$ in <a href=\"#m:eq:offset-reaction\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:offset-reaction\">[m:eq:offset-reaction]</a>, $$\\label{m:eq:reaction-positive-margin}\n V_c(z)+h_b(y)\\ge ca_y^{-4}\n \\quad\\text{almost everywhere in }z.$$ All these constants are independent of $r,r_*$ and the nuclear system.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-cond-cap",
        "exact_quote": "There are universal thresholds $C'_{\\rm cap},C_{\\rm cap}$ with the following properties. Define $$\\begin{split}\n Q\\text{ is bad}&\\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n       \\sup_QH_j>C'_{\\rm cap}a_Q^{-4},\\\\\n B_Q&=\\mathbf 1_{\\{Q\\text{ bad}\\}}\\sup_Q(H_j)_+,\n \\qquad \\Pi=\\sum_QB_Q\\chi_Q,\\qquad\n \\mathcal E=\\bigcup_{Q\\text{ bad}}Q'.\n \\end{split}$$ Then $H_j-\\Pi\\le C_{\\rm cap}a^{-4}$ on the participating band. For each fixed $m\\ge1$ and $A'>0$, $$\\label{m:eq:cond-cap-moments}\n \\mathbb P(Q\\text{ bad})+a_Q^{4m}\\mathbb EB_Q^m\n       \\le C_{L_1,m,A'}r^{A'}.$$ At each deterministic point, $\\mathbb P(y\\in\\mathcal E)$ has the same arbitrarily high polynomial bound. The cube families are finite, and $$\\label{m:eq:cond-band-volume}\n \\sum_Q|Q'|\\le C_{L_1}Mr^3.$$ All these random objects are jointly measurable in the data and the spatial variable. For the fixed system and scale, their sizes and supports have deterministic bounds.",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-cond-inverse",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $j<n$, and put $r=r_j$ and $R_+=2r$. For each deterministic $y$ with $r\\le a_y\\le2L_1R_+$, the failure probability of each implication below is less than $r^{40}$: $$\\label{m:eq:cond-inverse}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\mu_j(y)\\ge T_y(h_b(y))\n   &\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ H_j(y)\\ge I_y(\\mu_j(y))-\\delta_0a_y^{-4},\\\\\n \\mu_j(y)\\le T_y(h_{\\rm top}(y))\n   &\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ H_j(y)\\le I_y(\\mu_j(y))+\\delta_0a_y^{-4}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ The constants and the smallness threshold for $r_*$ are independent of the system and the number of scales.",

      },

      "exact_statement": "Fix $j<n$, and put $r=r_j$ and $R_+=2r$.         "exact_quote": "Fix $j<n$, put $r=r_j$ and $R_+=2r$, and let $r\\le a_y\\le2L_1R_+$. Under the original joint law $P=\\mathbb P$, $$\\label{m:eq:cond-field-moments}\n \\|\\mu_j(y)\\|_2\\le Ct_y^{-3}a_y^{-3},\\qquad\n \\|f_y(H_j(y))\\|_2\n        \\le Ca_y^{-6}(1+\\tau_y^{-3/2}).$$ Consequently, if $F_y$ is either failure event from Lemma <a href=\"#m:lem:cond-inverse\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"m:lem:cond-inverse\">40</a>, then $$\\label{m:eq:cond-integrated-cost}\n \\int_{\\{r\\le a_y\\le2L_1R_+\\}}\n \\mathbb E\\big[\\mathbf 1_{F_y}(\\mu_j(y)+f_y(H_j(y)))\\big]\\,\\mathrm dy\n       \\le CM r^{20-3-3w}.$$ The same bound holds with $F_y$ replaced by $\\{y\\in\\mathcal E\\}$, and the expected integral over all of $\\mathbb R^3$ of $r^{-2}\\sum_QB_Q\\mathbf 1_{Q'}$ is bounded by the right side as well.",

      "exact_statement": "Fix $j<n$, put $r=r_j$ and $R_+=2r$, and let $r\\le a_y\\le2L_1R_+$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-m-prop-outward-step",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $u,d_j$ satisfy the inductive conditions above, including <a href=\"#m:eq:outward-invariant\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:outward-invariant\">[m:eq:outward-invariant]</a>–<a href=\"#m:eq:outward-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:outward-bounds\">[m:eq:outward-bounds]</a>, at scale $r=r_j$. There are an offset $u'$ and a nonnegative error density $\\widetilde d$, measurable with respect to the same data $\\mathcal F_j$, that satisfy these inequalities at scale $R_+$ with $\\mu_j$ still in the core term. One may take $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{m:eq:outward-error}\n \\widetilde d={}&d_j+C r^{-2}\\sum_Q B_Q\\mathbf 1_{Q'}\\notag\\\\\n &+\\mathbf 1_{\\{r\\le a<R_+\\}}\\mu_j\n                       \\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal E\\cup\\mathcal B^{\\mathrm h}}\\notag\\\\\n &+\\mathbf 1_{\\{R_+\\le a\\le2L_1R_+\\}}T_y(H)\n                       \\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal E\\cup\\mathcal B^{\\mathrm l}},\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $C$ is a universal constant large enough to dominate the penalty Laplacian.",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-m-prop-outward-step",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that $u,d_j$ satisfy the inductive conditions above, including <a href=\"#m:eq:outward-invariant\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:outward-invariant\">[m:eq:outward-invariant]</a>–<a href=\"#m:eq:outward-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:outward-bounds\">[m:eq:outward-bounds]</a>, at scale $r=r_j$. There are an offset $u'$ and a nonnegative error density $\\widetilde d$, measurable with respect to the same data $\\mathcal F_j$, that satisfy these inequalities at scale $R_+$ with $\\mu_j$ still in the core term. One may take $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{m:eq:outward-error}\n \\widetilde d={}&d_j+C r^{-2}\\sum_Q B_Q\\mathbf 1_{Q'}\\notag\\\\\n &+\\mathbf 1_{\\{r\\le a<R_+\\}}\\mu_j\n                       \\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal E\\cup\\mathcal B^{\\mathrm h}}\\notag\\\\\n &+\\mathbf 1_{\\{R_+\\le a\\le2L_1R_+\\}}T_y(H)\n                       \\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal E\\cup\\mathcal B^{\\mathrm l}},\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $C$ is a universal constant large enough to dominate the penalty Laplacian.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose that $u,d_j$ satisfy the inductive conditions above, including <a href=\"#m:eq:outward-invariant\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:outward-invariant\">[m:eq:outward-invariant]</a>–<a href=\"#m:eq:outward-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:outward-bounds\">[m:eq:outward-bounds]</a>, at scale $r=r_j$. There are an offset $u'$ and a nonnegative error density $\\widetilde d$, measurable with respect to the same data $\\mathcal F_j$, that satisfy these inequalities at scale $R_+$ with $\\mu_j$ still in the core term. One may take $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{m:eq:outward-error}\n \\widetilde d={}&d_j+C r^{-2}\\sum_Q B_Q\\mathbf 1_{Q'}\\notag\\\\\n &+\\mathbf 1_{\\{r\\le a<R_+\\}}\\mu_j\n                       \\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal E\\cup\\mathcal B^{\\mathrm h}}\\notag\\\\\n &+\\mathbf 1_{\\{R_+\\le a\\le2L_1R_+\\}}T_y(H)\n                       \\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal E\\cup\\mathcal B^{\\mathrm l}},\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $C$ is a universal constant large enough to dominate the penalty Laplacian.",
      "explanation": "Unequally shifted maxima replace the reaction by the actual conditional density on the next layer. Explicit nonnegative errors pay failed inverse comparisons and the cap penalty.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-outward-average",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-outward-error",
        "exact_quote": "Starting with $u=b_{r_0}$ and $d_0=0$, the preceding construction reaches scale $r_n=r_*$ and produces a deterministic error density with $$\\label{m:eq:outward-total-error}\n \\int d_n\\le C M r_*^{17-3w}=o(M)\n \\qquad\\text{along the contradiction sequence}.$$ The constant is independent of the number of scales and of the nuclear configuration.",

          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-a-prop-cond-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "There is a universal constant $C_{\\rm cap}$ with the following property. Fix $L_1\\ge2$, $0<h_l<h_h<\\infty$, and a sufficiently small $\\xi>0$; it suffices to require $\\xi<h_l/16$. There exists $s_*=s_*(D_0,L_1,h_l,h_h,\\xi)>0$ such that, whenever $0<s\\le s_*$, at each $j<m$, with $r=r_j$, there is an event $\\mathcal G_j\\in\\mathcal F_j$ satisfying $\\mathbb P(\\mathcal G_j^c)\\le Cr^{25}$ on which, simultaneously for $r\\le d_y\\le4L_1r$ and $h_l\\le h\\le h_h$, $$\\label{a:eq:cond-inverse}\n \\begin{aligned}\n d_y^6\\mu_j(y)>kh^{3/2}\n       &\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad d_y^4H_j(y)\\ge h-\\xi,\\\\\n d_y^6\\mu_j(y)<kh^{3/2}\n       &\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad d_y^4H_j(y)\\le h+\\xi.\n \\end{aligned}$$ On the same event, $H_j(y)\\le C_{\\rm cap}d_y^{-4}$ throughout that band. The constant $C$ may depend on the fixed parameters, whereas $C_{\\rm cap}$ does not depend on $D_0,L_1,h_l,h_h,\\xi$. All estimates are uniform in $Z$, the ground state, $j$, and $m$.",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

The constant $C$ may depend on the fixed parameters, whereas $C_{\\rm cap}$ does not depend on $D_0,L_1,h_l,h_h,\\xi$. All estimates are uniform in $Z$, the ground state, $j$, and $m$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-a-prop-prop-deficit",
        "exact_quote": "There is a universal constant $c_{\\mathrm{def}}>0$ with the following property. For every $D_0\\in[0,\\infty)$ there is $s_*(D_0)>0$ such that, for each $0<s\\le s_*(D_0)$, there is an integer $Z_*(D_0,s)$ for which every integer $Z\\ge Z_*(D_0,s)$ and every normalized sector ground state $\\Psi$ satisfying $$n\\le3Z,\\qquad q_n[\\Psi]=E_n\\le E+D_0$$ obey $$\\label{a:eq:prop-deficit}\n Z-\\mathbb E_\\Psi\\#\\{i:|x_i|<s/4\\}\n \\ge c_{\\mathrm{def}}s^{-3}>1.$$ All thresholds are independent of the sector and of the choice of its ground state. In particular, admissible fixed radii $s$ can be taken arbitrarily small.",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-a-prop-prop-deficit",

In particular, admissible fixed radii $s$ can be taken arbitrarily small.",

        }

In particular, admissible fixed radii $s$ can be taken arbitrarily small.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-atomic-intermediate",
        "exact_quote": "There are universal constants $\\varepsilon,B,C_{\\rm inv}>0$ and $L_1>2$ with the following property. Fix $D_0<\\infty$, then take $s>0$ sufficiently small and integer $Z$ sufficiently large as in Proposition <a href=\"#a:prop:prop-deficit\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:prop:prop-deficit\">46</a>. For every sector ground state in that proposition use the common observations and master packets <a href=\"#a:eq:cond-observations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:eq:cond-observations\">[a:eq:cond-observations]</a>–<a href=\"#a:eq:cond-master\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:eq:cond-master\">[a:eq:cond-master]</a>, with $r_0=\\varepsilon Z^{-1/3}$ and $s/2<r_m\\le s$. For each $0\\le j\\le m$ there exist jointly measurable $\\mathcal F_j$-measurable fields $u_j$ and densities $p_j^{\\rm err}$ with the following properties. Put $d=|y|$, $r=r_j$, and $f(t)=4\\pi k(t_+)^{3/2}$, and define $$b_r(y)=Bd^{-4}\\left(1-\\frac r{8d}\\right)\\quad(d\\ge r).$$ $$\\label{a:eq:prop-invariant}\n \\begin{split}\n \\Delta u_j&\\ge-4\\pi\\nu+4\\pi\\mathbf 1_{\\{d<r\\}}\\mu_j\n       -4\\pi p_j^{\\mathrm{err}}+\\mathbf 1_{\\{d\\ge r\\}}f(u_j),\\\\\n b_r\\le u_j&\\le C_{\\mathrm{inv}}d^{-4}\\quad(d\\ge r),\n \\qquad u_j=b_r\\quad(d>L_1r).\n \\end{split}$$ The differential inequality is global in distributions. The offsets $u_j-V$ are continuous and locally Lipschitz on all of $\\mathbb R^3$; $p_j^{\\rm err}\\ge0$ is bounded and supported in $|y|\\le r_j$. For each fixed system and step, the offsets have deterministic size and Lipschitz bounds on every compact set; the errors have deterministic size bounds. Universally, $$\\label{eq:atomic-prefix-error}\n \\mathbb E\\int p_j^{\\rm err}\n \\le C r_0^{32}+C\\sum_{i<j}r_i^{19/2-3w}\n \\le C\\left(r_0^{32}+r_j^{19/2-3w}\\right),\\qquad w=10^{-5}.$$ In particular this is at most $C(s^{32}+s^{19/2-3w})$. The expectations cannot in general be omitted for $j<m$; the terminal fields are deterministic. The same master kernels are used at every intermediate stopping index. The constants $\\varepsilon,B,C_{\\rm inv},L_1$ are independent of any additional inverse-comparison parameters. Such a comparison uses these same observations whenever its own smallness condition on $s$ is met.",
        "source_id": "PM-263.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-atomic-intermediate",
          "exact_quote": "There are universal constants $\\varepsilon,B,C_{\\rm inv}>0$ and $L_1>2$ with the following property. For every sector ground state in that proposition use the common observations and master packets <a href=\"#a:eq:cond-observations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:eq:cond-observations\">[a:eq:cond-observations]</a>–<a href=\"#a:eq:cond-master\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:eq:cond-master\">[a:eq:cond-master]</a>, with $r_0=\\varepsilon Z^{-1/3}$ and $s/2<r_m\\le s$. For each $0\\le j\\le m$ there exist jointly measurable $\\mathcal F_j$-measurable fields $u_j$ and densities $p_j^{\\rm err}$ with the following properties. Put $d=|y|$, $r=r_j$, and $f(t)=4\\pi k(t_+)^{3/2}$, and define $$b_r(y)=Bd^{-4}\\left(1-\\frac r{8d}\\right)\\quad(d\\ge r).$$ $$\\label{a:eq:prop-invariant}\n \\begin{split}\n \\Delta u_j&\\ge-4\\pi\\nu+4\\pi\\mathbf 1_{\\{d<r\\}}\\mu_j\n       -4\\pi p_j^{\\mathrm{err}}+\\mathbf 1_{\\{d\\ge r\\}}f(u_j),\\\\\n b_r\\le u_j&\\le C_{\\mathrm{inv}}d^{-4}\\quad(d\\ge r),\n \\qquad u_j=b_r\\quad(d>L_1r).\n \\end{split}$$ The differential inequality is global in distributions. The offsets $u_j-V$ are continuous and locally Lipschitz on all of $\\mathbb R^3$; $p_j^{\\rm err}\\ge0$ is bounded and supported in $|y|\\le r_j$. Universally, $$\\label{eq:atomic-prefix-error}\n \\mathbb E\\int p_j^{\\rm err}\n \\le C r_0^{32}+C\\sum_{i<j}r_i^{19/2-3w}\n \\le C\\left(r_0^{32}+r_j^{19/2-3w}\\right),\\qquad w=10^{-5}.$$ In particular this is at most $C(s^{32}+s^{19/2-3w})$.       "exact_statement": "There are universal constants $\\varepsilon,B,C_{\\rm inv}>0$ and $L_1>2$ with the following property. Fix $D_0<\\infty$, then take $s>0$ sufficiently small and integer $Z$ sufficiently large as in Proposition <a href=\"#a:prop:prop-deficit\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:prop:prop-deficit\">46</a>. For every sector ground state in that proposition use the common observations and master packets <a href=\"#a:eq:cond-observations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:eq:cond-observations\">[a:eq:cond-observations]</a>–<a href=\"#a:eq:cond-master\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:eq:cond-master\">[a:eq:cond-master]</a>, with $r_0=\\varepsilon Z^{-1/3}$ and $s/2<r_m\\le s$. For each $0\\le j\\le m$ there exist jointly measurable $\\mathcal F_j$-measurable fields $u_j$ and densities $p_j^{\\rm err}$ with the following properties. Put $d=|y|$, $r=r_j$, and $f(t)=4\\pi k(t_+)^{3/2}$, and define $$b_r(y)=Bd^{-4}\\left(1-\\frac r{8d}\\right)\\quad(d\\ge r).$$ $$\\label{a:eq:prop-invariant}\n \\begin{split}\n \\Delta u_j&\\ge-4\\pi\\nu+4\\pi\\mathbf 1_{\\{d<r\\}}\\mu_j\n       -4\\pi p_j^{\\mathrm{err}}+\\mathbf 1_{\\{d\\ge r\\}}f(u_j),\\\\\n b_r\\le u_j&\\le C_{\\mathrm{inv}}d^{-4}\\quad(d\\ge r),\n \\qquad u_j=b_r\\quad(d>L_1r).\n \\end{split}$$ The differential inequality is global in distributions. The offsets $u_j-V$ are continuous and locally Lipschitz on all of $\\mathbb R^3$; $p_j^{\\rm err}\\ge0$ is bounded and supported in $|y|\\le r_j$. For each fixed system and step, the offsets have deterministic size and Lipschitz bounds on every compact set; the errors have deterministic size bounds. Universally, $$\\label{eq:atomic-prefix-error}\n \\mathbb E\\int p_j^{\\rm err}\n \\le C r_0^{32}+C\\sum_{i<j}r_i^{19/2-3w}\n \\le C\\left(r_0^{32}+r_j^{19/2-3w}\\right),\\qquad w=10^{-5}.$$ In particular this is at most $C(s^{32}+s^{19/2-3w})$. The expectations cannot in general be omitted for $j<m$; the terminal fields are deterministic. The same master kernels are used at every intermediate stopping index. The constants $\\varepsilon,B,C_{\\rm inv},L_1$ are independent of any additional inverse-comparison parameters. Such a comparison uses these same observations whenever its own smallness condition on $s$ is met.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Nuclear measure, Coulomb kernel and external potential** ([source](#eq:hamiltonian)): Fix $M\ge1$, distinct positions $R_1,\ldots,R_M\in\mathbb R^3$, and real charges $z_1,\ldots,z_M\ge1$. Put $$Z=\sum_{j=1}^M z_j,\qquad \nu=\sum_{j=1}^M z_j\delta_{R_j}, \qquad K(x)=\|x\|^{-1},\qquad V=K*\nu.$$
- **Fermionic sector and spin convention** ([source](#eq:hamiltonian)): For $n\ge1$ let $\mathcal H_n=\bigwedge^nL^2(\mathbb R^3;\mathbb C^2)$, so that there are two electron spin states and antisymmetry exchanges spatial and spin variables together.
- **Strict binding** ([source](#eq:hamiltonian)): We say that sector $n$ *binds strictly* if $E_n<E_{n-1}$.
- **Outer half-electron radius** ([source](#eq:radius)): Define $$\label{eq:radius} R(\Psi_Z)=\inf\left\{r\ge0: \int_{\|x\|>r}\rho_{\Psi_Z}(x)\,dx\le\frac12\right\}.$$
- **Energy-offset state** ([source](#pm-h-9)): A *$\delta$-state* is any normalized $\psi\in\mathcal Q_n$ in a finite sector $n$ with $q_n[\psi]\le E+\delta$, where $\delta\ge0$.
- **Signed Coulomb energy** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For a compactly supported real $f\in L^{5/3}(\mathbb R^3)$, put $$D(f)=\frac12\int f(K*f).$$
- **First-out factors and final core factor** ([source](#pm-h-13)): Define the first-out factors and the final core factor by $$\label{eq:loc-first-out} p_h=s_h\prod_{l<h}c_l,\qquad c=\prod_hc_h, \qquad o=\Big(\sum_hp_h^2\Big)^{1/2}.$$
- **Radial fine-density kernel** ([source](#eq:loc-fine-kernel)): For $b>0$, define the radial probability kernel $$\label{eq:loc-fine-kernel} \vartheta_b(x)=\int_{\mathrm{SO}(3)} b^{-6}\prod_{j=1}^3\big(b-\|(Qx)_j\|\big)_+\,\mathrm dQ,$$ where Haar measure has mass one. It is bounded by $Cb^{-3}$ and supported in $\overline{B(0,\sqrt3b)}$.
- **Nuclear and atomic distance geometries** ([source](#m:eq:screen-scale)): Fix $A=40$. We use either of the following scale geometries; the choice is kept fixed throughout each application of this section. All balls in these definitions are open. $$\label{m:eq:screen-scale} \begin{aligned} &\text{nuclear scale:}\qquad L=10^6,\quad\mathcal Y=\mathbb R^3,\\ &\hspace{2em}a_y=\sup\{s>0:\nu(B(y,Ls))\le s^{-3}\};\\[.4ex] &\text{atomic distance scale:}\qquad L=10^5,\quad \nu=Z\delta_0,\\ &\hspace{2em}\mathcal Y=\mathbb R^3\setminus\{0\},\qquad a_y=\|y\|/L. \end{aligned}$$
- **Local count normalization** ([source](#m:eq:screen-count-normalization)): For the present $\delta$-state define $$\label{m:eq:screen-count-normalization} \begin{gathered} n_y=\#\{i:x_i\in B_y\},\qquad m_{0,y}=\max(a_y^{-3},1),\qquad m_y=m_{0,y}+\sqrt{\delta a_y},\qquad e_y=\frac{m_y^2}{a_y},\\ P=\max\left(1,\sup_{y\in\mathcal Y}\frac{\mathbb En_y^2}{m_y^2}\right). \end{gathered}$$ Initially $P$ is merely finite, since $n_y\le n$ and $m_y\ge1$. We shall prove that it is bounded by a universal constant. Notice that $$\label{m:eq:screen-normalization-inequalities} m_y\ge1,\qquad a_y m_y\ge1,\qquad a_y m_y^{2/3}\ge1, \qquad e_y\asymp\max(a_y^{-7},a_y^{-1},\delta).$$ These facts hold also when $a_y>1$. Define the raw far field $$\label{m:eq:screen-raw-field} J_y=\int_{\|v-y\|\ge Aa_y}\frac{d\nu(v)}{\|y-v\|} -\sum_{\|x_i-y\|\ge Aa_y}\frac1{\|y-x_i\|}.$$ The exclusion removes its singularities and, for each fixed system, $\|J_y\|\le (Z+n)/(Aa_y)$. In the atomic geometry the nuclear term is exactly $V(y)=Z/\|y\|$, since $A<L$.
- **Raw excluded far field** ([source](#pm-h-17)): Define the raw far field $$\label{m:eq:screen-raw-field} J_y=\int_{\|v-y\|\ge Aa_y}\frac{d\nu(v)}{\|y-v\|} -\sum_{\|x_i-y\|\ge Aa_y}\frac1{\|y-x_i\|}.$$
- **Conditional core screened field** ([source](#pm-h-13)): Write $e_X$ for its energy, $\rho_X^c$ for its one-body density, and $$\Phi_X=V-K*\rho_X^c.$$
- **Local Thomas–Fermi functional** ([source](#stmt-a-lem-screen-patch)): There is a unique minimizer $\rho=\rho_X\ge0$, extended by zero outside $\Omega$, of $$\label{m:eq:screen-tf-functional} \mathcal T_X(\rho)=c_{\mathrm{TF}}\int\rho^{5/3}-\int\Phi_X\rho+D(\rho).$$
- **Observation sigma-fields** ([source](#pm-h-29)): Set $$\mathcal F_j=\sigma(Y_j,\ldots,Y_{m-1}),\qquad \mathcal F_m=\{\varnothing,\Omega\}.$$
- **Molecular master averaging operator** ([source](#m:eq:master-kernel)): Define the smearing widths, center kernels, and averaging operator by $$\label{m:eq:master-kernel} t_z=c_1a_z\min(a_z,r_*)^w,\qquad \mathcal K_z(y)=g_{t_z}^2(y-z),\qquad Rf(y)=\int\mathcal K_z(y)f(z)\,dz, \qquad \tau_y=t_y/a_y.$$
- **Averaged offset reaction** ([source](#m:eq:offset-reaction)): Define the nonlinearity acting on an offset $h\in\mathbb R$ by $$\label{m:eq:offset-reaction} f_y(h)=c_{\mathrm F}\int\mathcal K_z(y)(V_c(z)+h)_+^p\,dz.$$
- **Molecular outward invariant** ([source](#m:eq:outward-invariant)): The invariant at scale $r=r_j$ is a continuous locally $H^1$ offset $u$ and a nonnegative bounded compactly supported error density $d_j$ such that $$\label{m:eq:outward-invariant} \Delta u\ge -4\pi\nu_s +4\pi\mathbf 1_{\{a<r\}}\mu_j-4\pi d_j +\mathbf 1_{\{a\ge r\}}f_y(u) \quad\text{in distributions},$$
- **Atomic intermediate invariant** ([source](#a:eq:prop-invariant)): $$\label{a:eq:prop-invariant} \begin{split} \Delta u_j&\ge-4\pi\nu+4\pi\mathbf 1_{\{d<r\}}\mu_j -4\pi p_j^{\mathrm{err}}+\mathbf 1_{\{d\ge r\}}f(u_j),\\ b_r\le u_j&\le C_{\mathrm{inv}}d^{-4}\quad(d\ge r), \qquad u_j=b_r\quad(d>L_1r). \end{split}$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-thomas-fermi-kinetic-coefficient | c_{\rm TF} | = | $\frac3{10}(3\pi^2)^{2/3}$ | Universal sharp semiclassical coefficient, distinct from the coarse kinetic constant. |
| q-thomas-fermi-density-coefficient | k | = | $\frac{2^{3/2}}{3\pi^2}$ | Universal density-field coefficient. |
| q-thomas-fermi-poisson-coefficient | c_F | = | $4\pi k$ | Universal coefficient in the stated normalization. |
| q-strict-molecular-sector-bound | n | ≤ | $Z+CM$ | The universal constant is independent of nuclear charges and positions; an explicit numerical value is ABSENT. |
| q-coarse-molecular-sector-bound | n | ≤ | $3Z$ | Explicit total-charge bound. |
| q-coarse-atomic-sector-bound | n | ≤ | $2Z+1$ | Explicit charge bound on strict atomic sectors. |
| q-radius-lower-bound | R(\Psi_Z) | ≥ | $c$ | Positive universal lower constant; explicit value is ABSENT. |
| q-radius-upper-bound | R(\Psi_Z) | ≤ | $C$ | Finite universal upper constant; explicit value is ABSENT. |
| q-ionization-lower-bound | I_1(Z) | ≥ | $c$ | Positive universal lower constant in the stated normalization; explicit value is ABSENT. |
| q-ionization-upper-bound | I_1(Z) | ≤ | $C$ | Finite universal upper constant; explicit value is ABSENT. |
| q-atomic-density-offset-bound | \int\rho_\psi^{5/3} | ≤ | $C(Z^{7/3}+\delta)$ | Universal coefficient; explicit value is ABSENT. |
| q-refined-comparison-offset-ceiling | \delta | ≤ | $a^{-7+0.02}$ | Admissible energy offset at a sufficiently small local scale. |
| q-refined-packet-width | b | = | $a^{1+0.2}$ | Explicit width as a function of the admissible local scale. |
| q-data-event-energy-bound | q_n[\Psi_A] | ≤ | $e_\Psi+C\ell_j^{-2}\log^5(e/p)$ | The constant depends on the width square-sum and noise law, without particle-number or array-count dependence. |
| q-dyadic-width-square-sum | C_\ell | = | $(1-2^{-2.02})^{-1}$ | Explicit geometric square-sum factor for dyadic widths. |
| q-molecular-exterior-count-bound | \mathbb E\#\{i:a_{x_i}\ge s\} | ≤ | $CMs^{-3}$ | Uniform in nuclear charges and separations; explicit coefficient is ABSENT. |
| q-global-thomas-fermi-density-mass | \int\rho | ≤ | $2Q$ | Explicit source-mass bound. |
| q-master-width-exponent | w | = | $10^{-5}$ | Fixed exponent in the master width and accumulated error estimates. |
| q-molecular-integrated-terminal-error | \int d_n | ≤ | $C M r_*^{17-3w}$ | Uniform in scale count and nuclear configuration; the coefficient is unspecified. |
| q-atomic-prefix-expected-error | \mathbb E\int p_j^{\rm err} | ≤ | $C\left(r_0^{32}+r_j^{19/2-3w}\right)$ | Expected prefix error; deterministic only after all observations are forgotten. |
| q-neutral-exterior-mass-bound | \int_{\|x\|>R}\rho_{\Psi_Z}(x)\,\,\mathrm dx | ≤ | $\frac C R$ | Universal coefficient and lower thresholds with explicit numerical values ABSENT. |

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "exact_quote": "There is a universal finite constant $C$ such that every nuclear system specified above and every integer $n\\ge1$ satisfy $$E_n<E_{n-1}\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad n\\le Z+CM.$$ Moreover, $E_n=E_{n-1}$ whenever $n>Z+CM$. For one nucleus this gives $n\\le Z+C$ under strict binding, for every real $Z\\ge1$. The same constant is independent of all charges and nuclear positions.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-obs-tilt",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\Psi$ is an eigenstate of its sector Hamiltonian with energy $e_\\Psi$, where $0\\le e_\\Psi-E\\le D_0$. In particular, $e_\\Psi=E_n$ for a sector ground state. Suppose further that $$\\sum_{h=j}^{m-1}\\ell_h^{-2}\\le C_\\ell\\ell_j^{-2}$$ with a fixed numerical $C_\\ell$. This holds for the dyadic scales $\\ell_h=r_h^{1.01}$, $r_{h+1}=2r_h$, with $C_\\ell=(1-2^{-2.02})^{-1}$. If $A\\in\\mathcal F_j$ has probability $p=P(A)>0$, put $p_A(x)=P(A\\mid x)$ and $$\\Psi_A=\\sqrt{p_A(x)/p}\\,\\Psi.$$ Then $\\Psi_A$ is a normalized antisymmetric form-domain vector, its raw position-and-spin law is the raw marginal of $P$ conditioned on $A$, and $$\\label{eq:obs-tilt}\n q_n[\\Psi_A]\\le e_\\Psi+C\\ell_j^{-2}\\log^5(e/p)\n       \\le E+D_0+C\\ell_j^{-2}\\log^5(e/p).$$ The constant depends only on $C_\\ell$ and the chosen noise law, not on $n$, the number of arrays, or the nuclear system. The same assertion holds for an event of one unordered observation at any width $\\ell$, using $\\ell$ in place of $\\ell_j$. No upper bound on the particle number in terms of the nuclear charge is needed.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "q-data-event-energy-bound",
      "kind": "bound",
      "prefactor": null,
      "quantity": "q_n[\\Psi_A]",
      "regime": {
        "ambient": "An eigenstate tilted by an unordered observation event.",
        "hypotheses": [
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-lem-obs-tilt",
            "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\Psi$ is an eigenstate of its sector Hamiltonian with energy $e_\\Psi$, where $0\\le e_\\Psi-E\\le D_0$.",

    {

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-void-count",
          "exact_quote": "There is a universal constant $C$ such that, for $0<s\\le 1$, $$\\label{m:eq:void-count}\n \\mathbb E\\#\\{i:a_{x_i}\\ge s\\}\\le CMs^{-3}.$$ The constant is independent of the charges and the distances between nuclei.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "q-molecular-exterior-count-bound",
      "kind": "bound",
      "prefactor": null,
      "quantity": "\\mathbb E\\#\\{i:a_{x_i}\\ge s\\}",
      "regime": {
        "ambient": "Largest strictly binding molecular sector at the global minimum.",
        "hypotheses": [
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-void-count",
            "exact_quote": "for $0<s\\le 1$,",
            "source_id": "PM-263.1"
          },
          {
            "anchor": "m:sec:void",
            "exact_quote": "### Electron counts in large nuclear voids The local count bound of Lemma <a href=\"#m:lem:screen-count\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"m:lem:screen-count\">19</a> controls the electrons in each annulus whose radius is comparable to its distance from the nuclei. Summing that bound over arbitrarily many annuli would lose a factor equal to the number of scales. We first remove this loss in a nuclear void. A geometric decomposition will then reduce the exterior region of an arbitrary molecule to only $O(M)$ such voids. Throughout this section $\\Psi$ is the normalized eigenstate in the largest strictly bound sector, and $E=E_N=\\inf_{j\\ge0}E_j$, as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:sector-global-minimum\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:sector-global-minimum\">6</a>. Expectations without an additional subscript refer to its position law, with spins summed. Constants may depend on the fixed numbers $A=40$ and $L=10^6$, but on no feature of the molecule. <div id=\"m:lem:void-shell\" class=\"lemma\"> **Lemma 29** (A large nuclear void). *There are universal constants $t_0,C>0$ with the following property. Let $O\\in\\mathbb R^3$, $t\\ge t_0$ and $T=2^Jt$, where $J\\ge1$ is an integer. Suppose that at least one nucleus lies in $B(O,t/100)$ and that every nucleus satisfies $$|R_m-O|<t/100\\qquad\\text{or}\\qquad |R_m-O|>100T.$$ Then $$\\label{m:eq:void-annulus-count} \\left\\|\\#\\{i:t\\le |x_i-O|<T\\}\\right\\|_{L^2(\\Psi)}\\le C.$$* </div> <details id=\"proof-stmt-m-lem-void-shell\"><summary>Proof of stmt-m-lem-void-shell</summary> *Proof.* Translate $O$ to the origin. Put $u_i=|x_i|$, $s_k=2^kt$ and $$n_k=\\#\\{i:s_k\\le u_i<2s_k\\},\\qquad L_{\\mathrm{ann}}=\\sum_{k=0}^{J-1}n_k.$$ For $s_k/4\\le |v|\\le8s_k$, the nuclear assumptions give $c s_k\\le d(v)\\le C s_k$, where $d(v)$ is the distance to the nearest nucleus. Indeed, the inner nucleus supplies the upper bound; the inner and outer alternatives supply the lower bound. Choose $t_0$ so large that $d(v)/L>1$ on all these annuli. Lemma <a href=\"#m:lem:screen-scale\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"m:lem:screen-scale\">16</a> then gives $a_v=d(v)/L\\asymp s_k/L$. Each enlarged annulus is covered by a universally bounded number of local balls $B(v,a_v)$ with comparable scales. For example, take a maximal set of centers in the annulus separated by $c s_k/L$, with $c$ small; the disjoint balls of half that radius give the bound on their number. Lemma <a href=\"#m:lem:screen-count\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"m:lem:screen-count\">19</a>, applied to any $\\delta$-state $\\omega$ in the fixed sector $N$, consequently yields $$\\label{m:eq:void-local-shell} \\|n_k\\|_{L^2(\\omega)} +\\|n_{\\mathrm{neigh},k}\\|_{L^2(\\omega)} \\le C(1+\\sqrt{\\delta s_k}), \\qquad n_{\\mathrm{neigh},k}=\\#\\{i:s_k/2\\le u_i\\le4s_k\\}.$$ This proves the result when $J$ is bounded. We may therefore assume that $J$ exceeds a fixed sufficiently large integer. *A weighted eigenstate inequality.* Choose $0\\le\\chi\\le1$ supported in $(32t,T/32)$, equal to one on $[64t,T/64]$, and satisfying $|\\chi'(u)|\\le C/u$, $|\\chi''(u)|\\le C/u^2$. Such a function is obtained by multiplying a fixed cutoff at scale $t$ by a fixed cutoff at scale $T$. Set $w(u)=u\\chi(u)^2$. It is a bounded smooth multiplier that vanishes near the origin. For each weighted index, integration by parts gives $$\\begin{aligned} \\operatorname{Re}\\langle w(u_i)\\Psi,-\\tfrac12\\Delta_i\\Psi\\rangle &=\\tfrac12\\int w(u_i)|\\nabla_i\\Psi|^2 -\\tfrac14\\int \\Delta_iw(u_i)|\\Psi|^2\\\\ &\\ge-C\\mathbb E\\frac{\\mathbf 1_{\\{t\\le u_i<T\\}}}{u_i}. \\end{aligned}$$ These identities hold first after form-domain approximation and then by continuity, since $w$ and its derivatives are bounded for fixed $t,T$. Summing the last bound costs at most $C\\sum_k s_k^{-1}\\mathbb En_k\\le C/t\\le C$ by <a href=\"#m:eq:void-local-shell\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:void-local-shell\">[m:eq:void-local-shell]</a> with $\\delta=0$. After fixing $x_i$ and its spin, the remaining Hamiltonian has energy at least $E_{N-1}\\ge E$. Its contribution against the nonnegative weight $w(u_i)$ is therefore nonnegative. Testing the eigen-equation with $\\sum_iw(u_i)\\Psi$ shows that weighted repulsion minus attraction is at most $C$. For an electron in a shell meeting the support of $\\chi$, keep only its repulsion against electrons at radius $<t$, at radius $\\ge T$, and in shells $l\\le k-5$. Dropping the other repulsions preserves the upper bound. Write $$P_k=\\sum_{\\substack{0\\le l<J\\\\l\\le k-5}}n_l, \\qquad q=\\nu(B(0,t))-\\#\\{i:u_i<t\\},$$ and define the exterior signed potential at the origin by $$B=\\int_{|R|>100T}\\frac{d\\nu(R)}{|R|} -\\sum_{u_i\\ge T}\\frac1{u_i}.$$ For each such $k$, use a cut that is full out on $[s_k/2,4s_k]$, supported in $[s_k/4,8s_k]$, and has gradients bounded by $C/s_k$. Let $X_k$ be its out data as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:loc-identity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:loc-identity\">13</a>; a bar will mean conditional expectation given $X_k$ in this paragraph and below. Set $$\\varphi(v)=V(v)-\\sum_{u_i<t}\\frac1{|v-x_i|} -\\sum_{u_i\\ge T}\\frac1{|v-x_i|}, \\qquad \\psi_k(v)=\\overline{\\varphi(v) -\\sum_{l\\le k-5}\\sum_{i\\in l}\\frac1{|v-x_i|}}.$$ Every supported shell satisfies $32t\\le s_k\\le T/64$. All sources defining $\\psi_k$ are outside $\\{s_k/4<|v|<8s_k\\}$: prefix electrons have radius $<s_k/16$, inner electrons have radius $<t\\le s_k/32$, and exterior electrons have radius $\\ge T\\ge64s_k$. Thus $\\psi_k$ has a continuous harmonic version on this annulus. Newton’s sphere-average formula gives $$\\label{m:eq:void-sphere-mean} \\langle\\psi_k\\rangle_u =\\frac{\\bar q-\\bar P_k}{u}+\\bar B, \\qquad s_k/4<u<8s_k,$$ where $\\langle F\\rangle_u=(4\\pi u^2)^{-1}\\int_{|v|=u}F(v)\\,dS(v)$. The shell-$k$ positions are all recorded in $X_k$. We may therefore condition the selected interaction terms while retaining their weights. The preceding eigenstate inequality becomes $$\\label{m:eq:void-weighted-field} -\\mathbb E\\sum_k\\sum_{i\\in k}u_i\\chi(u_i)^2\\psi_k(x_i)\\le C,$$ where $k$ ranges over the supported shells. Conditional harmonic versions and sphere averages are justified directly by the positive distance to these sources and their fixed finite total masses. *A size-biased law for one shell.* Fix a supported $k$. Choose a smooth function $b_k$, equal to one on $[s_k,2s_k]$, supported in $(s_k/2,4s_k)$, with $0\\le b_k\\le1$ and $|b_k'|\\le C/s_k$. Put $$S_k=\\sum_i b_k(u_i)^2,\\qquad m_k=\\mathbb ES_k.$$ Then $n_k\\le S_k\\le n_{\\mathrm{neigh},k}$. If $m_k=0$, every shell-$k$ contribution vanishes and can be omitted. Otherwise define $$\\Psi_k=\\sqrt{S_k/m_k}\\,\\Psi,$$ and denote its expectation and $L^2$ norm by $\\mathbb E_k$ and $\\|\\cdot\\|_{2,k}$. The square root of a sum of squares is Lipschitz, and, off its zero set, $$|\\nabla\\sqrt{S_k}|^2 =\\frac{\\sum_i b_k(u_i)^2|\\nabla b_k(u_i)|^2}{S_k} \\le Cs_k^{-2}\\mathbf 1_{\\{n_{\\mathrm{neigh},k}>0\\}} \\le Cs_k^{-2}n_{\\mathrm{neigh},k}.$$ Its gradient is zero almost everywhere on its zero set. The multiplier identity in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:sector-multiplier\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:sector-multiplier\">7</a> shows that $\\Psi_k$ is a $\\delta_k$-state, with $$\\label{m:eq:void-tilt-energy} \\delta_k\\le \\frac{C\\mathbb En_{\\mathrm{neigh},k}}{s_k^2m_k}.$$ The random variable $S_k$ is $X_k$-measurable, because its support is inside the full-cut zone. The joint position and cut-label law changes by the factor $S_k/m_k$. Its conditional position law given $X_k$ is therefore unchanged on $S_k>0$. In particular all the barred fields and counts above have the same conditional versions under this new law. This permits us to use the local estimates for $\\Psi_k$ without changing the fields in <a href=\"#m:eq:void-weighted-field\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:void-weighted-field\">[m:eq:void-weighted-field]</a>. *A positive bound and a signed harmonic comparison.* On the broad annulus of harmonicity, the base and prefix sources lie outside the exclusion defining $J_v$. Its radius $Aa_v$ is a fixed small fraction of $|v|$, since $A/L$ is small. The additional electrons subtracted by $J_v$ give the upper bound $$\\label{m:eq:void-positive-comparison} \\psi_k(v)\\le\\overline{J_v} +C\\sum_{l\\ge k-4}\\frac{\\bar n_l}{\\max(s_l,s_k)}.$$ For comparable shells the exclusion supplies the denominator $c s_k$; for sufficiently larger shells radial separation supplies $c s_l$. These are precisely the electrons absent from the definition of $\\psi_k$. The cut defining $X_k$ is covered by a universally bounded number of cells at scales comparable to $s_k/L$. Its gradients are $C/s_k$ and $a_v\\ge1$ there, so it meets the initial-cut hypothesis of Lemma <a href=\"#m:lem:screen-positive-field\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"m:lem:screen-positive-field\">[m:lem:screen-positive-field]</a>. For targets in the same broad annulus, that lemma and <a href=\"#m:eq:void-local-shell\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:void-local-shell\">[m:eq:void-local-shell]</a>, now applied under $\\Psi_k$, give $$\\|(\\psi_k(v))_+\\|_{2,k} \\le \\frac{C(1+\\sqrt{\\delta_k s_k})}{s_k}.$$ Conditional Jensen’s inequality bounds $\\|\\bar n_l\\|_{2,k}$ by $\\|n_l\\|_{2,k}$. Thus the added count terms are bounded by the convergent series $$C\\sum_{l\\ge k-4}\\frac{1+\\sqrt{\\delta_k s_l}}{s_l} \\le \\frac{C(1+\\sqrt{\\delta_k s_k})}{s_k}.$$ Positive parts of harmonic functions are subharmonic. Covering the compact annulus $\\{s_k/2\\le|v|\\le3s_k\\}$ by a fixed number of interior balls in the broad annulus, and using the ball submean inequality and Minkowski’s inequality, consequently gives $$\\label{m:eq:void-positive-supremum} A_k=s_k\\sup_{s_k/2\\le|v|\\le3s_k}(\\psi_k(v))_+, \\qquad \\|A_k\\|_{2,k}\\le C(1+\\sqrt{\\delta_k s_k}).$$ The function $A_k/s_k-\\psi_k$ is nonnegative and harmonic in the interior of this latter annulus. Its value at any point of a sphere $s_k\\le u\\le2s_k$ is at least $c$ times its sphere mean, for one universal $c>0$. Indeed, after scaling, a bounded chain of interior balls joins any two points of these spheres; on a ball, this comparison follows by comparing its Poisson kernels on a smaller concentric ball. Applying this comparison and <a href=\"#m:eq:void-sphere-mean\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:void-sphere-mean\">[m:eq:void-sphere-mean]</a> gives $$\\label{m:eq:void-harnack} u\\psi_k(v)\\le C A_k+c(\\bar q+u\\bar B-\\bar P_k), \\qquad |v|=u\\in[s_k,2s_k].$$ The negative prefix term retains a fixed positive coefficient. *The endpoint fields.* Put $s_-=2t$ and $s_+=T/2$. For a supported shell and $u\\in[s_k,2s_k]$, $$\\bar q+u\\bar B =(1-\\vartheta(u))(\\bar q+s_-\\bar B) +\\vartheta(u)(\\bar q+s_+\\bar B), \\quad \\vartheta(u)=\\frac{u-s_-}{s_+-s_-}, \\quad 0\\le\\vartheta(u)\\le C s_k/T.$$ At either endpoint all base sources are outside the $J_v$ exclusion. Sphere averaging $\\bar\\varphi$ and adding back the annular electrons therefore yields $$\\label{m:eq:void-endpoint-field} \\bar q+s\\bar B\\le s\\langle(\\overline{J_v})_+\\rangle_s +Cs\\sum_l\\frac{\\bar n_l}{\\max(s_l,s)}, \\qquad s\\in\\{s_-,s_+\\}.$$ Indeed, the omitted contribution of an annular electron is nonnegative and at most its full Coulomb kernel. The latter has sphere average $1/\\max(s,u_i)\\le1/\\max(s,s_l)$ for an electron in shell $l$. For these targets $a_v\\asymp s/L$. The initial cut is still at scale $s_k/L$, so the energy parameter in Lemma <a href=\"#m:lem:screen-positive-field\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"m:lem:screen-positive-field\">[m:lem:screen-positive-field]</a> satisfies $e_*\\le C(\\min(s,s_k)^{-1}+\\delta_k)$. The precise consequence is $$\\label{m:eq:void-endpoint-norm} \\left\\|s\\langle(\\overline{J_v})_+\\rangle_s\\right\\|_{2,k} \\le C\\sqrt{\\frac{s}{\\min(s,s_k)}+\\delta_k s}.$$ At $s=s_-$ this is at most $C(1+\\sqrt{\\delta_k s_k})$. At $s=s_+$ the interpolation weight gives instead $$C\\frac{s_k}{T}\\sqrt{\\frac{T}{s_k}+\\delta_k T} \\le C\\left(\\sqrt{\\frac{s_k}{T}} +\\sqrt{\\delta_k s_k}\\sqrt{\\frac{s_k}{T}}\\right) \\le C(1+\\sqrt{\\delta_k s_k}).$$ The inner endpoint count term in <a href=\"#m:eq:void-endpoint-field\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:void-endpoint-field\">[m:eq:void-endpoint-field]</a> has norm at most $$C\\sum_l2^{-l}(1+\\sqrt{\\delta_k2^lt}) \\le C(1+\\sqrt{\\delta_k t}).$$ For the outer endpoint, $s_+/\\max(s_l,s_+)\\le1$; we retain its contribution as $C(s_k/T)\\bar L_{\\mathrm{ann}}$. Thus <a href=\"#m:eq:void-harnack\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:void-harnack\">[m:eq:void-harnack]</a> supplies a nonnegative, $X_k$-measurable random variable $D_k$, the same for every electron in shell $k$, such that $$\\label{m:eq:void-final-field-bound} u_i\\psi_k(x_i)\\le D_k+C\\frac{s_k}{T}\\bar L_{\\mathrm{ann}}-c\\bar P_k, \\qquad \\|D_k\\|_{2,k}\\le C(1+\\sqrt{\\delta_k s_k}).$$ *Summation over shells.* Changing to the size-biased law and using <a href=\"#m:eq:void-tilt-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:void-tilt-energy\">[m:eq:void-tilt-energy]</a>, $s_k\\ge1$, and $m_k\\le\\mathbb En_{\\mathrm{neigh},k}$, gives $$\\begin{aligned} \\mathbb E[n_kD_k]&\\le m_k\\mathbb E_kD_k \\le C m_k(1+\\sqrt{\\delta_k s_k})\\notag\\\\ &\\le C\\left(m_k+ \\sqrt{m_k\\mathbb En_{\\mathrm{neigh},k}/s_k}\\right) \\le C\\mathbb En_{\\mathrm{neigh},k}. \\label{m:eq:void-local-error-sum} \\end{aligned}$$ The neighbor annuli for supported $k$ lie inside $\\{t\\le|x|<T\\}$ and have bounded overlap, so these costs sum to $C\\mathbb EL_{\\mathrm{ann}}$. For the outer term, $n_k$ is $X_k$-measurable and the tower property gives $$\\begin{aligned} \\sum_k\\frac{s_k}{T}\\mathbb E[n_k\\bar L_{\\mathrm{ann}}] &=\\sum_k\\frac{s_k}{T}\\mathbb E[n_kL_{\\mathrm{ann}}]\\\\ &\\le\\|L_{\\mathrm{ann}}\\|_2 \\sum_k\\frac{s_k}{T}\\|n_k\\|_2 \\le C\\|L_{\\mathrm{ann}}\\|_2. \\end{aligned}$$ Here the unconditioned norms use <a href=\"#m:eq:void-local-shell\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:void-local-shell\">[m:eq:void-local-shell]</a> with $\\delta=0$, and $\\sum_k s_k/T\\le1$. Insert <a href=\"#m:eq:void-final-field-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:void-final-field-bound\">[m:eq:void-final-field-bound]</a> into <a href=\"#m:eq:void-weighted-field\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:void-weighted-field\">[m:eq:void-weighted-field]</a>. The weights $W_k=\\sum_{i\\in k}\\chi(u_i)^2$ are $X_k$-measurable and satisfy $0\\le W_k\\le n_k$. Removing the bar on $P_k$ by the tower property, we obtain $$\\label{m:eq:void-prefix-products} \\mathbb E\\sum_kW_kP_k\\le C(1+\\|L_{\\mathrm{ann}}\\|_2).$$ Let $I_{\\mathrm{mid}}$ consist of the shells entirely contained in $[64t,T/64]$, and set $L_{\\mathrm{mid}}=\\sum_{k\\in I_{\\mathrm{mid}}}n_k$. On these shells $W_k=n_k$. The sum on the left of <a href=\"#m:eq:void-prefix-products\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:void-prefix-products\">[m:eq:void-prefix-products]</a> therefore includes all products $n_kn_l$ with $k,l\\in I_{\\mathrm{mid}}$ and $l\\le k-5$. For every omitted close pair $|k-l|<5$, including $k=l$, use the tilt at $k$ and <a href=\"#m:eq:void-local-shell\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:void-local-shell\">[m:eq:void-local-shell]</a> to obtain $$\\mathbb En_kn_l\\le m_k\\mathbb E_kn_l \\le C m_k(1+\\sqrt{\\delta_k s_l}) \\le C\\mathbb En_{\\mathrm{neigh},k}.$$ The last step uses $s_l\\asymp s_k$ and the same calculation as <a href=\"#m:eq:void-local-error-sum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:void-local-error-sum\">[m:eq:void-local-error-sum]</a>. Each $k$ has only a bounded number of such neighbors. Thus the close-pair costs sum to $C\\mathbb EL_{\\mathrm{ann}}$, rather than a cost proportional to $J$. Expanding $L_{\\mathrm{mid}}^2$ now gives $$\\mathbb EL_{\\mathrm{mid}}^2\\le C(1+\\|L_{\\mathrm{ann}}\\|_2).$$ Only a fixed number of endpoint shells were omitted, so $\\|L_{\\mathrm{ann}}-L_{\\mathrm{mid}}\\|_2\\le C$ by <a href=\"#m:eq:void-local-shell\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:void-local-shell\">[m:eq:void-local-shell]</a>. Consequently $X=\\|L_{\\mathrm{ann}}\\|_2$ satisfies $X^2\\le C(1+X)$, proving the lemma. ◻ </details> <div id=\"m:lem:void-count\" class=\"lemma\"> **Lemma 30** (Exterior count). *There is a universal constant $C$ such that, for $0<s\\le 1$, $$\\label{m:eq:void-count} \\mathbb E\\#\\{i:a_{x_i}\\ge s\\}\\le CMs^{-3}.$$ The constant is independent of the charges and the distances between nuclei.* </div> <details id=\"proof-stmt-m-lem-void-count\"><summary>Proof of stmt-m-lem-void-count</summary> *Proof.* Write $\\mathcal R=\\{R_1,\\ldots,R_M\\}$ and $d(x)=\\operatorname{dist}(x,\\mathcal R)$. We use $d(x)\\le L a_x$ from Lemma <a href=\"#m:lem:screen-scale\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"m:lem:screen-scale\">16</a>. For a Borel set $B$, write $n(B)=\\#\\{i:x_i\\in B\\}$. The local count bound <a href=\"#m:eq:screen-count\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:screen-count\">[m:eq:screen-count]</a>, applied with $\\delta=0$, gives $$\\label{m:eq:void-net-local-count} \\mathbb En(B(y,a_y)) \\le C\\max\\{a_y^{-3},1\\}.$$ We first prove that $$\\label{m:eq:void-net-far-count} \\mathbb En(\\{d\\ge U\\})\\le CM$$ for a sufficiently large universal constant $U$. A direct summation of local bounds over all large distance scales would give an infinite series. We instead group scales into a bounded number of annuli to which Lemma <a href=\"#m:lem:void-shell\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"m:lem:void-shell\">29</a> applies.",

        {
          "anchor": "eq:atomic-prefix-error",
          "exact_quote": "There are universal constants $\\varepsilon,B,C_{\\rm inv}>0$ and $L_1>2$ with the following property. Fix $D_0<\\infty$, then take $s>0$ sufficiently small and integer $Z$ sufficiently large as in Proposition <a href=\"#a:prop:prop-deficit\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:prop:prop-deficit\">46</a>. For every sector ground state in that proposition use the common observations and master packets <a href=\"#a:eq:cond-observations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:eq:cond-observations\">[a:eq:cond-observations]</a>–<a href=\"#a:eq:cond-master\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:eq:cond-master\">[a:eq:cond-master]</a>, with $r_0=\\varepsilon Z^{-1/3}$ and $s/2<r_m\\le s$. For each $0\\le j\\le m$ there exist jointly measurable $\\mathcal F_j$-measurable fields $u_j$ and densities $p_j^{\\rm err}$ with the following properties. Put $d=|y|$, $r=r_j$, and $f(t)=4\\pi k(t_+)^{3/2}$, and define $$b_r(y)=Bd^{-4}\\left(1-\\frac r{8d}\\right)\\quad(d\\ge r).$$ $$\\label{a:eq:prop-invariant}\n \\begin{split}\n \\Delta u_j&\\ge-4\\pi\\nu+4\\pi\\mathbf 1_{\\{d<r\\}}\\mu_j\n       -4\\pi p_j^{\\mathrm{err}}+\\mathbf 1_{\\{d\\ge r\\}}f(u_j),\\\\\n b_r\\le u_j&\\le C_{\\mathrm{inv}}d^{-4}\\quad(d\\ge r),\n \\qquad u_j=b_r\\quad(d>L_1r).\n \\end{split}$$ The differential inequality is global in distributions. The offsets $u_j-V$ are continuous and locally Lipschitz on all of $\\mathbb R^3$; $p_j^{\\rm err}\\ge0$ is bounded and supported in $|y|\\le r_j$. For each fixed system and step, the offsets have deterministic size and Lipschitz bounds on every compact set; the errors have deterministic size bounds. Universally, $$\\label{eq:atomic-prefix-error}\n \\mathbb E\\int p_j^{\\rm err}\n \\le C r_0^{32}+C\\sum_{i<j}r_i^{19/2-3w}\n \\le C\\left(r_0^{32}+r_j^{19/2-3w}\\right),\\qquad w=10^{-5}.$$ In particular this is at most $C(s^{32}+s^{19/2-3w})$. The expectations cannot in general be omitted for $j<m$; the terminal fields are deterministic. The same master kernels are used at every intermediate stopping index. The constants $\\varepsilon,B,C_{\\rm inv},L_1$ are independent of any additional inverse-comparison parameters. Such a comparison uses these same observations whenever its own smallness condition on $s$ is met.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],

For every sector ground state in that proposition use the common observations and master packets <a href=\"#a:eq:cond-observations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:eq:cond-observations\">[a:eq:cond-observations]</a>–<a href=\"#a:eq:cond-master\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:eq:cond-master\">[a:eq:cond-master]</a>, with $r_0=\\varepsilon Z^{-1/3}$ and $s/2<r_m\\le s$. <a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-sector-compactness** [claim](#stmt-lem-sector-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sector-compactness): Distant wedge trials prove monotonicity; spatial square partitions, tightness and local compactness prove attainment under a strict gap. -> Sector monotonicity and normalized strict-sector minimizers.
- **step-coarse-molecular-number** [claim](#stmt-lem-sector-crude) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sector-crude): Regularize the reciprocal nuclear potential, test the weak eigen-equation, and use pairwise triangle and weighted Cauchy–Schwarz bounds before removing regularization. -> Coarse charge-linear strict-sector bound.
- **step-global-sector-minimum** [claim](#stmt-lem-sector-global-minimum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sector-global-minimum): Select the largest strict drop from the finite nonempty strict-sector set and apply monotonicity beyond it. -> An attained all-sector minimum.
- **step-multiplier-identity** [claim](#stmt-lem-sector-multiplier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sector-multiplier): Test the eigen-equation with the squared symmetric multiplier and cancel potentials and mixed gradients. -> Exact multiplier energy cost.
- **step-atomic-binding** [claim](#stmt-lem-atomic-binding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-atomic-binding): Inductively wedge a cutoff core with a distant radial orbital; the spherical mean exposes positive unscreened nuclear charge. -> Binding and compactness through the neutral sector.
- **step-atomic-global-sector** [claim](#stmt-lem-atomic-sector) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-atomic-sector): A regularized radial reciprocal multiplier and the triangle inequality bound strict atomic sectors; monotonicity identifies the global minimum. -> Refined atomic sector bound and attained global minimum.
- **step-coulomb-duality** [claim](#stmt-lem-loc-coulomb) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-loc-coulomb): Use Riesz representation and harmonic uniqueness for Coulomb duality, then spherical averaging for radial smearing. -> Coulomb norm identity, test duality and smearing inequalities.
- **step-coarse-kinetic** [claim](#stmt-lem-loc-kinetic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-loc-kinetic): Antisymmetric occupation bounds and Fourier spectral splitting give a coarse kinetic density estimate. -> Coarse kinetic inequality and mixture occupation control.
- **step-atomic-global-density** [claim](#stmt-lem-atomic-global-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-atomic-global-density): Drop repulsion, split nuclear attraction at the bulk radius, and absorb its inner part with Young's inequality. -> Global raw-density integrability bound.
- **step-conditional-cut-identity** [claim](#stmt-lem-loc-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-loc-identity): Expand sequential square partitions, normalize recorded-coordinate slices, and retain the exact conditional core–out interaction. -> Exact conditional localization, out kinetic estimate and nested cut-data towers.
- **step-hole-trials** [claim](#stmt-lem-loc-hole-trial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-loc-hole-trial): Build coherent packet density matrices, truncate their spectral expansions and average Bernoulli Slater occupations; wedge with the separated core. -> Upper quantum comparison with the sharp kinetic coefficient.
- **step-neumann-fine-density** [claim](#stmt-lem-loc-cubes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-loc-cubes): Count spin-degenerate Neumann cube levels, average grid translations and rotations, then subtract radial self energies. -> Matching lower kinetic and pair comparisons.
- **step-nuclear-scale** [claim](#stmt-m-lem-screen-scale) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-screen-scale): Use downward closure of admissible radii and ball inclusions to prove scale bounds and Lipschitzness, then a local mesh cover. -> Comparable-cell geometry and local nuclear mass control.
- **step-positive-field-bootstrap** [claim](#stmt-a-lem-screen-provisional) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-lem-screen-provisional): A hole trial penalizes a large positive conditional field; repeated deterministic cuts would force field growth faster than the elementary nuclear cap permits. -> Conditional positive-field norm bound with a provisional count factor.
- **step-interior-patch-comparison** [claim](#stmt-a-lem-screen-patch) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-lem-screen-patch): Select a shell by averaging, minimize the local convex functional, compare coherent upper and Neumann lower energies, and prove an interior supersolution cap. -> Measurable patch minimizer, discrepancy budget and interior field bounds.
- **step-close-count-bootstrap** [claim](#stmt-m-lem-screen-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-screen-count): Test the fine-density discrepancy against a smooth local cutoff and improve any excessively large provisional supremum ratio. -> Universal local count second moments.
- **step-uniform-local-fields** [claim](#stmt-a-prop-screen-local) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-prop-screen-local): Substitute the closed count bound into the positive-field estimate and compare covering-cell energy units. -> Uniform count and conditional positive-field estimates.
- **step-refined-local-density** [claim](#stmt-a-cor-screen-refined) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-cor-screen-refined): Choose the stated fine-width and absorption powers, bound each error term, and apply the coarse kinetic estimate where the cut is full out. -> Refined Coulomb and raw-density bounds.
- **step-near-coulomb** [claim](#stmt-auto-corollary-1) / [proof](#proof-stmt-auto-corollary-1): Apply Hölder's inequality and direct radial kernel integration to the raw local density. -> Expected near-Coulomb bound.
- **step-source-free-oscillation** [claim](#stmt-a-lem-screen-oscillation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-lem-screen-oscillation): Subtract a bounded Newton particular solution, then apply a positive harmonic gradient estimate to the remaining offset. -> Oscillation control including negative central fields.
- **step-annular-counts** [claim](#stmt-a-cor-screen-annular) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-cor-screen-annular): Dilate a finite comparable-cell annular cover and sum local count norms by Minkowski's inequality. -> Atomic annular count norms at arbitrary finite offsets.
- **step-observation-versions** [claim](#stmt-lem-obs-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-obs-data): Use permutation-summed likelihood ratios for explicit conditional laws; differentiate the smooth packets and integrate the conditional ratios for towers. -> Jointly measurable densities, field regularity, Poisson identities and towers.
- **step-event-energy** [claim](#stmt-lem-obs-tilt) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-obs-tilt): Bound directional likelihood scores with independent symmetric noise, control the square-root likelihood gradient and apply the eigenstate multiplier identity. -> Dimension-free observation event energy cost.
- **step-field-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-obs-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-obs-transfer): Use separate posterior towers, decompose raw and packet potentials, and control signed density errors with truncated Coulomb tests. -> Averaged transfer estimate without pathwise posterior identification.
- **step-weak-maxima** [claim](#stmt-lem-maximum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-maximum): Test competing inequalities with complementary Lipschitz approximations of the activity indicator and discard the favorable gradient term. -> Weak differential inequality for finite maxima.
- **step-void-shell** [claim](#stmt-m-lem-void-shell) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-void-shell): Combine weighted eigenstate testing, shell-biased laws, conditional harmonic comparisons and prefix-count products to control a long empty annulus. -> Void count norm independent of shell count.
- **step-molecular-exterior-count** [claim](#stmt-m-lem-void-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-void-count): Charge crowded scale pairs to removed nuclear representatives and group isolated runs into voids; sum the remaining inverse-cube distance costs. -> Global exterior expected count proportional to nuclear count.
- **step-global-tf** [claim](#stmt-m-lem-barrier-global-tf) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-barrier-global-tf): Apply the direct method on the Coulomb-space positive cone, derive the Euler equation, establish finite mass and use positive-part tests for nonnegativity. -> Unique global Thomas–Fermi minimizer and decaying positive field.
- **step-point-mixtures** [claim](#stmt-m-lem-barrier-mixture) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-barrier-mixture): Bound single-source screening by a near–far split, compare with an exterior power subsolution, and use convexity for truncated-source mixtures. -> Single-source lower field bounds and mixture comparison.
- **step-reference-fields** [claim](#stmt-m-lem-barrier-fields) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-barrier-fields): Select local source mass, apply mixture comparisons, control offsets with blowup supersolutions and choose the smooth-source strength in the stated order. -> Positive original and smooth reference fields with offset and gradient caps.
- **step-offset-oscillation** [claim](#stmt-m-lem-barrier-oscillation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-barrier-oscillation): Cancel nuclear singularities, estimate the Newton particular solution of the offset Laplacian and control the harmonic remainder by Harnack estimates. -> Continuous nuclear offsets and local oscillation estimates.
- **step-master-kernel** [claim](#stmt-m-lem-barrier-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-barrier-kernel): Use Lipschitz width comparability for center-kernel derivatives and mass preservation; radial smearing controls the potential correction. -> Master averaging bounds and a small nonnegative potential correction.
- **step-damped-barriers** [claim](#stmt-m-lem-barrier-subsolution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-barrier-subsolution): Choose damping powers and coefficients before the terminal smallness threshold; compare the damping Laplacian with the averaged reaction decrement. -> Subsolution barriers with exterior and reaction margins.
- **step-doubling-gap** [claim](#stmt-m-lem-barrier-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-barrier-gap): Subtract the damping definitions at consecutive scales and use the smooth reference lower bound. -> Uniform transition-band gap under scale doubling.
- **step-observed-positive-tail** [claim](#stmt-m-lem-cond-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-cond-tail): Tilt an excessive observed-field event, use the original density tower and bound its far field through the general-offset local estimates. -> Stretched-exponential positive-field tail.
- **step-spatial-cap** [claim](#stmt-m-lem-cond-cap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-cond-cap): Apply a submean estimate after a quadratic correction, integrate the positive-field tail and cover the nuclear-scale band by enlarged grid cubes. -> Measurable cap penalty with moment and volume bounds.
- **step-molecular-inverse** [claim](#stmt-m-lem-cond-inverse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-cond-inverse): Tilt a proposed failure, match the observation and fine densities, invert the patch reaction on Coulomb-good data, pay negative exceptional fields, and transfer expectations. -> Pointwise molecular one-sided inverse comparisons.
- **step-exceptional-moments** [claim](#stmt-m-lem-cond-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-cond-moments): Use conditional Jensen for density moments, tail integration for reaction moments and Cauchy–Schwarz against each failure probability before spatial integration. -> Integrated exceptional density, reaction and penalty costs.
- **step-molecular-outward-step** [claim](#stmt-m-prop-outward-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-prop-outward-step): Glue unequal shifted offsets with the next barrier on agreeing open overlaps; near-active branches use inverse comparisons and failed sets are paid explicitly. -> Next-layer invariant with the same retained observations.
- **step-molecular-average** [claim](#stmt-m-lem-outward-average) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-outward-average): Integrate the weak inequality under the positive-parent likelihood law, apply density towers and Jensen's inequality, and preserve deterministic local regularity bounds. -> Next-scale invariant after forgetting an observation array.
- **step-molecular-error-sum** [claim](#stmt-m-lem-outward-error) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-outward-error): Initialize below the minimum nuclear scale, integrate the nonnegative increments and sum their positive scale powers geometrically. -> Deterministic terminal error negligible relative to nuclear count.
- **step-excess-charge-conclusion** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-lem-outward-error-2): Cancel nuclear singularities in a compact-source potential comparison, take a far spherical mean and combine terminal exterior mass with the error bound to contradict unbounded excess per nucleus. -> Uniform excess-charge theorem and adjacent-energy equality beyond its bound.
- **step-atomic-simultaneous-inverse** [claim](#stmt-a-prop-cond-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-prop-cond-comparison): Control observed annular counts and positive fields, exclude point-height failures through refined patches and separate towers, then use spatial and height nets. -> Simultaneous annular inverse comparisons and a universal cap.
- **step-atomic-prefix-subsolutions** [claim](#stmt-prop-atomic-intermediate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-atomic-intermediate): Initialize with a truncated interior potential, glue shifted fields to explicit atomic barriers, pay bad-band source loss, and average each observation array. -> Intermediate atomic subsolutions with expected prefix error bounds.
- **step-atomic-deficit** [claim](#stmt-a-prop-prop-deficit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-atomic-intermediate): At the terminal index compare with a compact signed source potential, use the positive barrier's spherical mean and convert packet mass to raw interior count. -> Uniform positive deficit inside admissible fixed small balls.
- **step-inner-harmonic-fields** [claim](#stmt-a-lem-tail-harmonic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-lem-tail-harmonic): Add back excluded exterior electrons shell by shell, apply arbitrary-offset local count and field bounds, then use harmonic submeans for the spatial supremum. -> Conditional harmonic supremum estimate with a convergent offset-dependent series.
- **step-deficit-upper** [claim](#stmt-a-lem-tail-deficit-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-lem-tail-deficit-upper): Bound the spherical mean of the unconditional inner field by its positive harmonic supremum. -> Offset-dependent upper bound on a fixed-radius deficit.
- **step-neutral-offset** [claim](#stmt-a-prop-tail-offset) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-prop-tail-offset): Delete exterior particles from a global atomic minimizer; observed deficit tails give a favorable core-sector probability, and summable screened shell attraction bounds its energy cost. -> Uniform singly ionized and neutral energy offsets.
- **step-positive-ionization-gap** [claim](#stmt-a-prop-tail-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-prop-tail-gap): Average separated insertion holes over a distant center annulus, recover the unit residual charge, and use a complementary multiplier to bound the discarded attractive field. -> Uniform positive ionization gap at sufficiently large charge.
- **step-all-charge-ionization** [claim](#stmt-cor-first-ionization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-prop-tail-gap-2): Combine the uniform offset upper bound with the positive gap and extend through finitely many smaller integer charges using strict binding and finite sector energies. -> Two-sided first-ionization bounds for all positive integer charges.
- **step-neutral-tail-mass** [claim](#stmt-a-prop-tail-mass) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-prop-tail-mass): Test the neutral eigen-equation with a shell mass, bias the law by that data-measurable mass, preserve the conditional field and sum reciprocal dyadic shell counts. -> Uniform reciprocal exterior mass decay.
- **step-half-electron-radius** [claim](#stmt-thm-radius) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-prop-tail-mass-2): Use the neutral deficit for a lower radius and the exterior tail for an upper radius; compactness and kinetic density bounds handle the finitely many remaining charges uniformly over their ground-state spaces. -> Universal two-sided half-electron radius bound.

It satisfies $N\\leq3Z$, has a normalized minimizer $\\Psi$, and $$\\label{eq:sector-global-minimum}\n E:=E_N=\\inf_{n\\geq0}E_n.$$ Any bound on this $N$ bounds every strictly bound sector of the same system.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-global-sector-minimum",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-sector-multiplier",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Psi\\in\\mathcal Q_n$ be a normalized eigenstate with eigenvalue $e_\\Psi$. If $F$ is a bounded real Lipschitz function of the spatial configuration, invariant under particle permutations, then $$\\label{eq:sector-multiplier}\n q_n[F\\Psi]-e_\\Psi\\|F\\Psi\\|_2^2\n =\\frac12\\mathbb E_\\Psi|\\nabla F|^2.$$ Here $\\nabla$ denotes the full spatial gradient in $\\mathbb R^{3n}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-multiplier-identity",
      "inputs": [

      ],
      "method": "Test the eigen-equation with the squared symmetric multiplier and cancel potentials and mixed gradients.",
      "output": "Exact multiplier energy cost.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-atomic-binding",

Each of these sectors has a ground state, and every normalized minimizing sequence has an $L^2$ convergent subsequence with normalized ground-state limit. The multiplier identity of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:sector-multiplier\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:sector-multiplier\">7</a> holds for every such ground state with $e_\\Psi=E_n$.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-atomic-sector",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-loc-coulomb",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-loc-coulomb",
          "exact_quote": "For compactly supported real $f\\in L^{5/3}(\\mathbb R^3)$, its Coulomb potential is continuous and belongs to $\\dot H^1$, and $$\\label{eq:loc-coulomb-duality}\n D(f)=\\frac1{8\\pi}\\|\\nabla(K*f)\\|_2^2,\n \\qquad\n \\left|\\int fv\\right|\\leq C\\sqrt{D(f)}\\|\\nabla v\\|_2\n \\quad(v\\in\\dot H^1).$$ The potential and energy assertions also hold for a nonnegative $f\\in L^{5/3}$ whose distribution belongs to $(\\dot H^1)^*$, with $K*f$ defined by its nonnegative convolution. Its dual pairing with any $v\\in\\dot H^1$ is the absolutely convergent integral $\\int fv$.*\n\n*If $\\eta$ is a radial nonnegative probability density supported in $B(0,b)$, then $K*\\eta\\leq K$, with equality outside that ball. In particular, $D(\\eta*\\rho)\\leq D(\\rho)$ for every nonnegative compactly supported $\\rho\\in L^{5/3}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-coulomb-duality",
      "inputs": [
        "Homogeneous Sobolev space",
        "Admissible signed or positive density"
      ],
      "method": "Use Riesz representation and harmonic uniqueness for Coulomb duality, then spherical averaging for radial smearing.",
      "output": "Coulomb norm identity, test duality and smearing inequalities.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-loc-coulomb"
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-loc-kinetic",

The one-body density matrix of an antisymmetric $k$-particle vector $f$ is bounded above by $\\|f\\|_2^2$ times the identity. The same bound holds after integration in auxiliary variables, and such matrices may be summed whenever the corresponding squared norms sum to one.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-loc-identity",
          "exact_quote": "With the preceding definitions, set $$T_o=\\frac12\\sum_I\\sum_{i\\in I}\\|\\nabla_i\\psi_I\\|_2^2.$$ There is a nonnegative localization error satisfying $$\\label{eq:loc-error}\n \\mathrm{IMS}\\leq\\frac12\\mathbb E_\\psi\\sum_i\\sum_h\n       \\big(|\\nabla s_h|^2+|\\nabla c_h|^2\\big)(x_i)$$ such that $$\\label{eq:loc-cut}\n q_n[\\psi]+\\mathrm{IMS}\n =\\mathbb Ee_X+T_o-\\mathbb E\\sum_{i\\ \\mathrm{out}}\\Phi_X(x_i)\n                   +\\mathbb E\\sum_{i<j\\ \\mathrm{out}}K(x_i-x_j).$$ The out kinetic energy satisfies $$T_o\\geq c\\int(o^2\\rho_\\psi)^{5/3},$$ where $\\rho_\\psi$ is the raw one-body density. Conditional quantities have versions jointly measurable in the data and the field evaluation point. Appending deterministic cuts admits a coupling in which these decorated data are nested, and hence conditional expectations of raw quantities satisfy the tower property.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-conditional-cut-identity",
      "inputs": [
        "Deterministic spatial cuts",
        "Antisymmetric state",
        "Occupation bounds"
      ],
      "method": "Expand sequential square partitions, normalize recorded-coordinate slices, and retain the exact conditional core–out interaction.",
      "output": "Exact conditional localization, out kinetic estimate and nested cut-data towers.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-loc-identity"
    },

          "exact_quote": "Fix a conditional core slice with finite energy $e_X$ and field $\\Phi_X$ as above. Let $\\rho\\geq0$ belong to $L^{5/3}$ and have compact support. Suppose its packet region $\\operatorname{supp}\\rho+\\overline{B(0,b)}$ has positive distance from the spatial support allowed to the core particles. Then $$\\label{eq:loc-trial}\n E\\leq e_X+c_{\\mathrm{TF}}\\int\\rho^{5/3}\n       +Cb^{-2}\\int\\rho\n       -\\int\\Phi_X(g_b^2*\\rho)+D(\\rho).$$ The constant depends only on the fixed function $g$. The vacuum trial also gives $E\\leq e_X$.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"

          "exact_quote": "In the setting of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:loc-identity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:loc-identity\">13</a>, let $n_o$ be the out particle count. Choose, measurably, any subset of the out particles and set $$\\sigma(x)=\\sum_{i\\ \\mathrm{retained}}\\vartheta_b(x-x_i).$$ Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n T_o&\\geq\\mathbb E\\left[c_{\\mathrm{TF}}\\int\\sigma^{5/3}\n               -Cb^{-2}(n_o^{4/3}+n_o)\\right],\n \\label{eq:loc-fine-kinetic}\\\\\n \\sum_{i<j\\ \\mathrm{out}}K(x_i-x_j)\n   &\\geq D(\\sigma)-Cn_o/b\n \\quad\\text{almost surely}.\n \\label{eq:loc-fine-pair}\n\\end{aligned}$$",

For every fixed $c<L/2$, $B(y,ca_y)$ is covered by a bounded number of balls $B_z$ with $a_z\\asymp a_y$. The covering bound depends only on $c$ and $L$.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-a-lem-screen-provisional",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"m:lem:screen-positive-field\" label=\"m:lem:screen-positive-field\"></span>Fix $C_0\\ge1$. Suppose the initial sequence is absent, or its total out support is covered by at most $C_0$ balls $B_z$ and the sum of its cuts’ squared gradients is bounded by $$\\sum_z D_z^2a_z^{-2}\\mathbf 1_{B_z},\n \\qquad \\frac{D_z^2}{a_zm_z}\\le C_0\\sqrt P.$$ If $X$ denotes its data (trivial for an absent sequence), and $e_*$ is the maximum of $e_y$ and the energies $e_z$ of these covering cells, then $$\\label{m:eq:screen-positive}\n \\big\\|(\\mathbb E[J_y\\mid X])_+\\big\\|_2\n       \\le C(C_0)\\sqrt{\\frac{P e_*}{a_y}}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],

      "inputs": [
