      "method": "A hole trial penalizes a large positive conditional field; repeated deterministic cuts would force field growth faster than the elementary nuclear cap permits.",
      "output": "Conditional positive-field norm bound with a provisional count factor.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-a-lem-screen-provisional"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-a-lem-screen-patch",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-positive-field-bootstrap",
        "step-hole-trials",
        "step-neumann-fine-density",
        "step-coulomb-duality"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-a-lem-screen-patch",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"m:lem:screen-patch\" label=\"m:lem:screen-patch\"></span>Fix $y\\in\\mathcal Y$, write $a=a_y$, $m=m_y$, $e=e_y$, and choose $0<\\beta<1/100$ such that $$\\label{m:eq:screen-patch-condition}\n \\frac{\\beta^{-2}}{am}\\le\\sqrt P.$$ Set $b=\\beta a$. There is a deterministic $t_0\\in[5a,6a]$ and a cut full out on $B(y,t_0)$, supported in $B(y,t_0+b)$, with gradients at most $C/b$. Let $X$ be its out data, $\\rho_X^c$ the conditional core density, $\\Phi_X=V-K*\\rho_X^c$, and $\\Omega=B(y,t_0-4b)$. There is a unique minimizer $\\rho=\\rho_X\\ge0$, extended by zero outside $\\Omega$, of $$\\label{m:eq:screen-tf-functional}\n \\mathcal T_X(\\rho)=c_{\\mathrm{TF}}\\int\\rho^{5/3}-\\int\\Phi_X\\rho+D(\\rho).$$ It is measurable in $X$ and, with $W=\\Phi_X-K*\\rho$, satisfies $$\\label{m:eq:screen-tf-equation}\n \\rho=kW_+^{3/2}\\quad\\hbox{in }\\Omega.$$ Retain the out particles with $|x_i-y|<t_0-7b$ and put $\\sigma=\\sum_{i\\ \\mathrm{retained}}\\vartheta_b(\\cdot-x_i)$, using the radial fine kernel from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:loc-cubes\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:loc-cubes\">15</a>. Write $$\\label{m:eq:screen-remainder}\n \\mathcal L=c_{\\mathrm{TF}}\\int\\left(\\sigma^{5/3}-\\rho^{5/3}\n              -\\frac53\\rho^{2/3}(\\sigma-\\rho)\\right).$$ For every sufficiently small $\\epsilon>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{m:eq:screen-comparison}\n &\\mathbb E\\left[D(\\sigma-\\rho)+\\mathcal L+\\int W_-\\sigma\\right]\\\\\n &\\quad\\le C\\left[\\delta+(\\epsilon+\\sqrt\\beta)Pe\n       +\\epsilon^{-3/2}\\beta^{1/2}a^{-7}\n       +b^{-1}\\sqrt Pm\n       +b^{-2}\\left(P^{2/3}m^{4/3}+\\sqrt Pm\\right)\\right].\\notag\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $\\mathbb E\\int\\sigma^{5/3}$ is bounded by $CPe$ plus a constant times this right-hand side, and the out kinetic energy obeys $$\\label{m:eq:screen-out-kinetic}\n T_o\\le C\\left(Pe+b^{-2}\\sqrt Pm\\right).$$ Finally, independently of the data, $$\\label{m:eq:screen-interior-upper}\n W\\le V_{\\rm loc}+Ca^{-4}\\quad\\hbox{on }B(y,3a),\\qquad\n \\int_{B(y,2a)}\\rho\\le Ca^{-3},$$ where $V_{\\rm loc}$ is the potential of nuclei in $B(y,20a)$. If this local nuclear measure is zero, as it always is in the atomic distance geometry, the same construction has the sharper bound $$\\label{a:eq:screen-patch}\n \\mathbb E\\left[D(\\sigma-\\rho)+\\mathcal L+\\int W_-\\sigma\\right]\n \\le C\\left[\\delta+\\sqrt\\beta Pe+b^{-1}\\sqrt Pm\n       +b^{-2}\\left(P^{2/3}m^{4/3}+\\sqrt Pm\\right)\\right],$$ and its interior bounds include $$\\label{a:eq:screen-interior}\n W\\le Ca^{-4},\\qquad \\rho\\le Ca^{-6}\n       \\quad\\hbox{on }B(y,3a),\\qquad\n \\int_{B(y,2a)}\\rho\\le Ca^{-3}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-interior-patch-comparison",
      "inputs": [
        "Positive-field bound",
        "Hole trials",
        "Fine-density lower bounds",
        "Coulomb duality"

      "method": "Select a shell by averaging, minimize the local convex functional, compare coherent upper and Neumann lower energies, and prove an interior supersolution cap.",
      "output": "Measurable patch minimizer, discrepancy budget and interior field bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-a-lem-screen-patch"

          "exact_quote": "In either geometry, every normalized $\\delta$-state in a finite sector satisfies $P\\le C$. Fix $y\\in\\mathcal Y$. Suppose the initial finite sequence of cuts has total out support covered by at most $C_0$ cells $B_z$, with $C_0^{-1}\\le a_z/a_y\\le C_0$, and satisfies the gradient hypothesis of Lemma <a href=\"#m:lem:screen-positive-field\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"m:lem:screen-positive-field\">[m:lem:screen-positive-field]</a>. The absent-cut case is allowed. Then $$\\label{a:eq:screen-local}\n P\\le C,\\qquad\n \\big\\|(\\mathbb E[J_y\\mid X])_+\\big\\|_2\n             \\le C(C_0)\\frac{m_y}{a_y}.$$ Neither constant depends on the nuclear system, particle number, state, target, or $\\delta$.",

          "anchor": "stmt-a-cor-screen-refined",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"m:lem:screen-density\" label=\"m:lem:screen-density\"></span>There is a universal $a_0>0$ with the following property. If $a=a_y<a_0$ and $\\psi$ is a $\\delta$-state with $\\delta\\le a^{-7+0.02}$, choose the patch of Lemma <a href=\"#m:lem:screen-patch\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"m:lem:screen-patch\">[m:lem:screen-patch]</a> with $b=a^{1+0.2}$. Its densities obey $$\\label{m:eq:screen-fine-error}\n \\mathbb E\\left[D(\\sigma-\\rho)+\\mathcal L+\\int W_-\\sigma\\right]\n       \\le Ca^{-7+0.02},\\qquad\n \\mathbb E\\int\\sigma^{5/3}\\le Ca^{-7}.$$ The raw one-body density satisfies $$\\label{m:eq:screen-density}\n \\int_{B(y,4a)}\\rho_\\psi^{5/3}\\le Ca^{-7}.$$ In particular, the two estimates needed in the atomic application are $$\\label{a:eq:screen-refined}\n \\mathbb E\\left[D(\\sigma-\\rho)+\\int W_-\\sigma\\right]\\le Ca^{-7+.02},\n \\qquad \\int_{B(y,4a)}\\rho_\\psi^{5/3}\\le Ca^{-7}.$$",

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-a-lem-screen-oscillation",
          "exact_quote": "For every patch in Lemma <a href=\"#a:lem:screen-patch\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:lem:screen-patch\">18</a> with $\\nu(B(y,20a))=0$, and every $0<s'\\le a/2$, almost surely in its data, $$\\label{a:eq:screen-oscillation}\n \\sup_{x\\in B(y,s')}|W(x)-W(y)|\n       \\le C\\frac{s'}a\\bigl(a^{-4}+W(y)_-\\bigr).$$",

          "anchor": "stmt-a-cor-screen-annular",
          "exact_quote": "In the atomic distance geometry, for every $0<\\lambda<\\Lambda<\\infty$ and $u>0$, every normalized $\\delta$-state of finite particle number satisfies $$\\label{a:eq:screen-annular}\n \\left\\|\\#\\{i:\\lambda u<|x_i|<\\Lambda u\\}\\right\\|_2\n \\le C_{\\lambda,\\Lambda}\n       \\bigl(\\max(u^{-3},1)+\\sqrt{\\delta u}\\bigr).$$ All constants are independent of $Z$ and of the particle number.",

        }

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-obs-data",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-obs-data",
          "exact_quote": "There are jointly Borel versions of $$\\mu_j(y)=\\mathbb E_P\\!\\left[\\sum_{i=1}^n\\mathcal K_{x_i}(y)\n                                      \\mid\\mathcal F_j\\right],\n \\qquad H_j(y)=V_s(y)-(K*\\mu_j)(y),$$ with the following properties. At every positive-density data tuple, $\\mu_j\\ge0$, $\\int\\mu_j=n$, and $\\mu_j$ is smooth in its spatial variable. Every spatial derivative has a deterministic bound for the fixed system. The potential $K*\\mu_j$ is $C^1$, with bounded value and gradient, and $$\\label{eq:obs-poisson}\n \\Delta H_j=-4\\pi\\nu_s+4\\pi\\mu_j$$ in distributions. In particular, the always valid regularity statement is that $H_j-V_s$ is $C^1$ and bounded with bounded gradient. If $V_s$ and its gradient are bounded and continuous, these conclusions hold for $H_j$ itself. Conditional averaging satisfies $$\\label{eq:obs-tower}\n \\mathbb E_P[\\mu_j(y)\\mid\\mathcal F_{j+1}]=\\mu_{j+1}(y),\\qquad\n \\mathbb E_P[H_j(y)\\mid\\mathcal F_{j+1}]=H_{j+1}(y)$$ where $V_s(y)$ is finite. The same towers hold for nonnegative spatial integrals, and for integrable signed observables. These statements include the trivial data at $j=m$. None of the fixed-system regularity bounds is asserted to be uniform in the nuclear system or in $t_{\\min}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-observation-versions",

        "Finite unordered noisy arrays",
        "Positive minimum packet width"
      ],
      "method": "Use permutation-summed likelihood ratios for explicit conditional laws; differentiate the smooth packets and integrate the conditional ratios for towers.",
      "output": "Jointly measurable densities, field regularity, Poisson identities and towers.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-obs-data"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-obs-tilt",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-obs-tilt",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\Psi$ is an eigenstate of its sector Hamiltonian with energy $e_\\Psi$, where $0\\le e_\\Psi-E\\le D_0$. In particular, $e_\\Psi=E_n$ for a sector ground state. Suppose further that $$\\sum_{h=j}^{m-1}\\ell_h^{-2}\\le C_\\ell\\ell_j^{-2}$$ with a fixed numerical $C_\\ell$. This holds for the dyadic scales $\\ell_h=r_h^{1.01}$, $r_{h+1}=2r_h$, with $C_\\ell=(1-2^{-2.02})^{-1}$. If $A\\in\\mathcal F_j$ has probability $p=P(A)>0$, put $p_A(x)=P(A\\mid x)$ and $$\\Psi_A=\\sqrt{p_A(x)/p}\\,\\Psi.$$ Then $\\Psi_A$ is a normalized antisymmetric form-domain vector, its raw position-and-spin law is the raw marginal of $P$ conditioned on $A$, and $$\\label{eq:obs-tilt}\n q_n[\\Psi_A]\\le e_\\Psi+C\\ell_j^{-2}\\log^5(e/p)\n       \\le E+D_0+C\\ell_j^{-2}\\log^5(e/p).$$ The constant depends only on $C_\\ell$ and the chosen noise law, not on $n$, the number of arrays, or the nuclear system. The same assertion holds for an event of one unordered observation at any width $\\ell$, using $\\ell$ in place of $\\ell_j$. No upper bound on the particle number in terms of the nuclear charge is needed.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-obs-transfer",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-obs-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a point $y$ where $V_s(y)$ is finite and a length $a>0$. Let $A\\in\\mathcal F_j$ have positive $P$-probability, and let $Q$ be $P$ conditioned on $A$, extended by fresh localization labels. Its raw marginal is the law of $\\Psi_A$ from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:obs-tilt\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:obs-tilt\">26</a>. Let $X$ denote the fresh patch data, and let $\\rho_X^c$ be the conditional density of core particles under this tilted localization law. Partition the remaining raw particles into retained and deleted particles. Let $$\\sigma=\\sum_{i\\ {\\rm retained}}\\vartheta_b(\\cdot-x_i),\\qquad\n h_X=V_s-K*\\rho_X^c-K*\\rho_X,\\qquad W_X=V_c+h_X,$$ where $\\vartheta_b$ is the radial probability kernel of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:loc-cubes\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:loc-cubes\">15</a>, supported in $B(0,\\sqrt3b)$, and $\\rho_X\\ge0$ is a measurably chosen patch comparison density. Assume $\\rho_X$ and $\\sigma$ belong to $L^{5/3}$ and have compact support for almost every patch datum. Put $t=t_y$, $\\tau=t/a$, and $\\beta=b/a$, and suppose $0<t,b\\le a/10$. Assume the following estimates, with fixed constants:*\n\n1.  *The master width $z\\mapsto t_z$ is Lipschitz with constant at most $1/2$. The raw one-particle density $\\rho_{\\Psi_A}$ satisfies $$\\int_{B(y,4a)}\\rho_{\\Psi_A}^{5/3}\\le Ca^{-7}.$$*\n\n2.  *Deleted particles have $|x_i-y|\\ge ca$, and their count obeys $\\mathbb E_Q n_{\\rm del}\\le Ca^{-3}\\sqrt\\beta$.*\n\n3.  *For some $\\varepsilon_a\\ge0$, $$\\mathbb E_Q D(\\rho_X-\\sigma)\\le Ca^{-7}\\varepsilon_a^2.$$*\n\n4.  *On $B(y,t)$ either the source-free bound $\\rho_X\\le Ca^{-6}$ holds, or $$\\rho_X=k(W_X)_+^{3/2},\\qquad\n     W_X\\le V_{\\rm loc}+Ca^{-4},\\qquad\n     V_{\\rm loc}(z)=\\sum_\\alpha\\frac{q_\\alpha}{|z-R_\\alpha|},\n     \\qquad\\sum_\\alpha q_\\alpha\\le Ca^{-3},$$ with $q_\\alpha\\ge0$. The latter alternative permits arbitrary locations of the local nuclei.*\n\n\n\n*Then the two expectation towers are $$\\label{eq:obs-two-towers}\n \\mathbb E_Q(K*\\mu_j)(y)=\\mathbb E_Q P^{\\rm ms}(y),\\qquad\n \\mathbb E_Q(K*\\rho_X^c)(y)=\\mathbb E_Q P^c(y),$$ where $P^{\\rm ms}$ is the potential of the raw master packets and $P^c$ is the raw core potential. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:obs-transfer}\n \\begin{split}\n |\\mathbb E_Q(H_j-h_X)(y)|\\le Ca^{-4}\\big[&\n    \\tau^{1/5}+\\beta^{1/5}+\\sqrt\\beta\n      +\\varepsilon_a\\tau^{-1/2}\\\\\n    &+R(\\tau)+(\\tau+\\sqrt3\\beta)^{1/5}\\big],\n \\end{split}$$ where $R(\\tau)=\\tau^2$ in the source-free alternative and $R(\\tau)=\\tau^2+\\tau^{1/2}$ in the nuclear alternative. Thus the common upper bound $R(\\tau)\\le C\\tau^{1/2}$ is always available. All constants are independent of the number of particles; they depend only on the constants in the displayed hypotheses.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],

      "output": "Averaged transfer estimate without pathwise posterior identification.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-obs-transfer"

If finitely many functions $v_i\\in C(\\mathcal O)\\cap H^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\mathcal O)$ satisfy $$\\Delta v_i\\ge g+F(y,v_i)$$ in distributions, their maximum satisfies the same inequality with its own value in the reaction term. The assertion also holds for functions $v_i=V+a_i$ with continuous locally $H^1$ offsets $a_i$, when all inequalities have the common singular term $-4\\pi\\nu$ and the reaction vanishes in a neighborhood of $\\operatorname{supp}\\nu$.",

          "exact_quote": "Let $\\zeta$ be as above, with total mass $Q>0$. The functional $$\\mathcal T_\\zeta(\\rho)\n   =c_{\\mathrm{TF}}\\int\\rho^{5/3}-\\int V_\\zeta\\rho+D(\\rho),\n       \\qquad \\rho\\in\\mathcal X_+,$$ has a unique minimizer. Its field $W=V_\\zeta-K*\\rho$ satisfies $$\\label{m:eq:global-tf-equation}\n \\rho=kW_+^p,\\qquad\n \\Delta W=-4\\pi\\zeta+c_{\\mathrm F}W_+^p,\\qquad\n W\\ge0,\\qquad \\int\\rho\\le2Q.$$ The screening potential $K*\\rho$ is continuous and tends to zero at infinity.           "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-barrier-mixture",

Let $W_i$ be the single-source field of mass $q$ at the $i$th center with that source’s normalized profile. If $W$ is the full-source field, then $$\\label{m:eq:tf-mixture}\n \\sum_i\\frac{q_i}{q}W_i\\le W.$$",

          "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-barrier-kernel",
          "exact_quote": "Uniformly as $r_*\\downarrow0$, a center contributing to $Rf(y)$ satisfies $|z-y|\\le2t_y$ and $t_z/t_y=1+O(r_*^w)$.           "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-barrier-subsolution",
          "exact_quote": "There are fixed positive constants $\\alpha,\\epsilon_0,C'_0,c_b,c'$ with the following properties for all sufficiently small $r_*>0$. For $0<r\\le r_*$ define $$\\label{m:eq:barrier-definition}\n \\Gamma_r=\\epsilon_0W_s+C'_0r^{4\\alpha}W_s^{1+\\alpha},\\qquad\n B_r=W_0+q_0-\\Gamma_r,\\qquad b_r=B_r-V_c,\n \\qquad h_b=h_0-c_ba^{-4}.$$ The offsets $b_r$ are continuous and locally $H^1$ and decay at infinity. On $\\{a\\ge r\\}$ their Laplacian densities satisfy $$\\label{m:eq:barrier-subsolution}\n \\Delta b_r\\ge-4\\pi\\nu_s+f_y(b_r).$$ More precisely, this inequality holds almost everywhere there between the locally integrable densities representing the two sides; on every open set contained in this region it is also a distributional inequality. On the same region, $$\\label{m:eq:barrier-bounds}\n B_r\\ge0.9W_0>0,\\qquad\n h_b+c'a^{-4}\\le b_r\\le Ca^{-4}.$$ For every contributing center $z$ in <a href=\"#m:eq:offset-reaction\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:offset-reaction\">[m:eq:offset-reaction]</a>, $$\\label{m:eq:reaction-positive-margin}\n V_c(z)+h_b(y)\\ge ca_y^{-4}\n \\quad\\text{almost everywhere in }z.$$ All these constants are independent of $r,r_*$ and the nuclear system.",

          "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-cond-cap",
          "exact_quote": "There are universal thresholds $C'_{\\rm cap},C_{\\rm cap}$ with the following properties. Define $$\\begin{split}\n Q\\text{ is bad}&\\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n       \\sup_QH_j>C'_{\\rm cap}a_Q^{-4},\\\\\n B_Q&=\\mathbf 1_{\\{Q\\text{ bad}\\}}\\sup_Q(H_j)_+,\n \\qquad \\Pi=\\sum_QB_Q\\chi_Q,\\qquad\n \\mathcal E=\\bigcup_{Q\\text{ bad}}Q'.\n \\end{split}$$ Then $H_j-\\Pi\\le C_{\\rm cap}a^{-4}$ on the participating band. For each fixed $m\\ge1$ and $A'>0$, $$\\label{m:eq:cond-cap-moments}\n \\mathbb P(Q\\text{ bad})+a_Q^{4m}\\mathbb EB_Q^m\n       \\le C_{L_1,m,A'}r^{A'}.$$ At each deterministic point, $\\mathbb P(y\\in\\mathcal E)$ has the same arbitrarily high polynomial bound. The cube families are finite, and $$\\label{m:eq:cond-band-volume}\n \\sum_Q|Q'|\\le C_{L_1}Mr^3.$$ All these random objects are jointly measurable in the data and the spatial variable. For the fixed system and scale, their sizes and supports have deterministic bounds.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-cond-inverse",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $j<n$, and put $r=r_j$ and $R_+=2r$. For each deterministic $y$ with $r\\le a_y\\le2L_1R_+$, the failure probability of each implication below is less than $r^{40}$: $$\\label{m:eq:cond-inverse}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\mu_j(y)\\ge T_y(h_b(y))\n   &\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ H_j(y)\\ge I_y(\\mu_j(y))-\\delta_0a_y^{-4},\\\\\n \\mu_j(y)\\le T_y(h_{\\rm top}(y))\n   &\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ H_j(y)\\le I_y(\\mu_j(y))+\\delta_0a_y^{-4}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ The constants and the smallness threshold for $r_*$ are independent of the system and the number of scales.",

          "anchor": "stmt-m-lem-cond-moments",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $j<n$, put $r=r_j$ and $R_+=2r$, and let $r\\le a_y\\le2L_1R_+$. Under the original joint law $P=\\mathbb P$, $$\\label{m:eq:cond-field-moments}\n \\|\\mu_j(y)\\|_2\\le Ct_y^{-3}a_y^{-3},\\qquad\n \\|f_y(H_j(y))\\|_2\n        \\le Ca_y^{-6}(1+\\tau_y^{-3/2}).$$ Consequently, if $F_y$ is either failure event from Lemma <a href=\"#m:lem:cond-inverse\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"m:lem:cond-inverse\">40</a>, then $$\\label{m:eq:cond-integrated-cost}\n \\int_{\\{r\\le a_y\\le2L_1R_+\\}}\n \\mathbb E\\big[\\mathbf 1_{F_y}(\\mu_j(y)+f_y(H_j(y)))\\big]\\,\\mathrm dy\n       \\le CM r^{20-3-3w}.$$ The same bound holds with $F_y$ replaced by $\\{y\\in\\mathcal E\\}$, and the expected integral over all of $\\mathbb R^3$ of $r^{-2}\\sum_QB_Q\\mathbf 1_{Q'}$ is bounded by the right side as well.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }

      "output": "Integrated exceptional density, reaction and penalty costs.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-m-prop-outward-step",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that $u,d_j$ satisfy the inductive conditions above, including <a href=\"#m:eq:outward-invariant\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:outward-invariant\">[m:eq:outward-invariant]</a>–<a href=\"#m:eq:outward-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"m:eq:outward-bounds\">[m:eq:outward-bounds]</a>, at scale $r=r_j$. There are an offset $u'$ and a nonnegative error density $\\widetilde d$, measurable with respect to the same data $\\mathcal F_j$, that satisfy these inequalities at scale $R_+$ with $\\mu_j$ still in the core term. One may take $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{m:eq:outward-error}\n \\widetilde d={}&d_j+C r^{-2}\\sum_Q B_Q\\mathbf 1_{Q'}\\notag\\\\\n &+\\mathbf 1_{\\{r\\le a<R_+\\}}\\mu_j\n                       \\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal E\\cup\\mathcal B^{\\mathrm h}}\\notag\\\\\n &+\\mathbf 1_{\\{R_+\\le a\\le2L_1R_+\\}}T_y(H)\n                       \\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal E\\cup\\mathcal B^{\\mathrm l}},\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $C$ is a universal constant large enough to dominate the penalty Laplacian.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],

      "output": "Next-layer invariant with the same retained observations.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-m-prop-outward-step"

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-a-prop-cond-comparison",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-a-prop-cond-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "There is a universal constant $C_{\\rm cap}$ with the following property. Fix $L_1\\ge2$, $0<h_l<h_h<\\infty$, and a sufficiently small $\\xi>0$; it suffices to require $\\xi<h_l/16$. There exists $s_*=s_*(D_0,L_1,h_l,h_h,\\xi)>0$ such that, whenever $0<s\\le s_*$, at each $j<m$, with $r=r_j$, there is an event $\\mathcal G_j\\in\\mathcal F_j$ satisfying $\\mathbb P(\\mathcal G_j^c)\\le Cr^{25}$ on which, simultaneously for $r\\le d_y\\le4L_1r$ and $h_l\\le h\\le h_h$, $$\\label{a:eq:cond-inverse}\n \\begin{aligned}\n d_y^6\\mu_j(y)>kh^{3/2}\n       &\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad d_y^4H_j(y)\\ge h-\\xi,\\\\\n d_y^6\\mu_j(y)<kh^{3/2}\n       &\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad d_y^4H_j(y)\\le h+\\xi.\n \\end{aligned}$$ On the same event, $H_j(y)\\le C_{\\rm cap}d_y^{-4}$ throughout that band. The constant $C$ may depend on the fixed parameters, whereas $C_{\\rm cap}$ does not depend on $D_0,L_1,h_l,h_h,\\xi$. All estimates are uniform in $Z$, the ground state, $j$, and $m$.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }

      "method": "Control observed annular counts and positive fields, exclude point-height failures through refined patches and separate towers, then use spatial and height nets.",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-atomic-intermediate",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-atomic-global-density",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-atomic-intermediate",
          "exact_quote": "There are universal constants $\\varepsilon,B,C_{\\rm inv}>0$ and $L_1>2$ with the following property. Fix $D_0<\\infty$, then take $s>0$ sufficiently small and integer $Z$ sufficiently large as in Proposition <a href=\"#a:prop:prop-deficit\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"a:prop:prop-deficit\">46</a>. For every sector ground state in that proposition use the common observations and master packets <a href=\"#a:eq:cond-observations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:eq:cond-observations\">[a:eq:cond-observations]</a>–<a href=\"#a:eq:cond-master\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:eq:cond-master\">[a:eq:cond-master]</a>, with $r_0=\\varepsilon Z^{-1/3}$ and $s/2<r_m\\le s$. For each $0\\le j\\le m$ there exist jointly measurable $\\mathcal F_j$-measurable fields $u_j$ and densities $p_j^{\\rm err}$ with the following properties. Put $d=|y|$, $r=r_j$, and $f(t)=4\\pi k(t_+)^{3/2}$, and define $$b_r(y)=Bd^{-4}\\left(1-\\frac r{8d}\\right)\\quad(d\\ge r).$$ $$\\label{a:eq:prop-invariant}\n \\begin{split}\n \\Delta u_j&\\ge-4\\pi\\nu+4\\pi\\mathbf 1_{\\{d<r\\}}\\mu_j\n       -4\\pi p_j^{\\mathrm{err}}+\\mathbf 1_{\\{d\\ge r\\}}f(u_j),\\\\\n b_r\\le u_j&\\le C_{\\mathrm{inv}}d^{-4}\\quad(d\\ge r),\n \\qquad u_j=b_r\\quad(d>L_1r).\n \\end{split}$$ The differential inequality is global in distributions. The offsets $u_j-V$ are continuous and locally Lipschitz on all of $\\mathbb R^3$; $p_j^{\\rm err}\\ge0$ is bounded and supported in $|y|\\le r_j$. For each fixed system and step, the offsets have deterministic size and Lipschitz bounds on every compact set; the errors have deterministic size bounds. Universally, $$\\label{eq:atomic-prefix-error}\n \\mathbb E\\int p_j^{\\rm err}\n \\le C r_0^{32}+C\\sum_{i<j}r_i^{19/2-3w}\n \\le C\\left(r_0^{32}+r_j^{19/2-3w}\\right),\\qquad w=10^{-5}.$$ In particular this is at most $C(s^{32}+s^{19/2-3w})$. The expectations cannot in general be omitted for $j<m$; the terminal fields are deterministic. The same master kernels are used at every intermediate stopping index. The constants $\\varepsilon,B,C_{\\rm inv},L_1$ are independent of any additional inverse-comparison parameters. Such a comparison uses these same observations whenever its own smallness condition on $s$ is met.",
          "source_id": "PM-263.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-atomic-prefix-subsolutions",

In particular, admissible fixed radii $s$ can be taken arbitrarily small.",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-a-lem-tail-harmonic",

          "anchor": "stmt-a-lem-tail-harmonic",
          "exact_quote": "Fix positive constants $\\alpha<\\alpha'<\\beta'<\\beta$ and $\\alpha_0<\\beta_0$. Let $u>0$, $0<h\\le\\alpha u/2$, and let $\\psi$ be a normalized $\\delta$-state with $n\\le3Z$, where $\\delta<\\infty$ is arbitrary. Let $Y$ be the data from a cut whose out support lies in $\\{\\alpha_0u<|x|<\\beta_0u\\}$ and whose squared gradient cost is at most $C u^{-2}$. The cut may also be absent, in which case $Y$ is trivial. There is a jointly measurable version $$H_Y(x)=\\mathbb E_\\psi[B_h(x)\\mid Y]$$ which is harmonic on $\\{\\alpha u<|x|<\\beta u\\}$ for almost every datum. Put $$M(Y)=\\sup_{\\alpha'u\\le|x|\\le\\beta'u}(H_Y(x))_+.$$ Then $$\\label{a:eq:tail-harmonic-bound}\n \\|M\\|_2\\le C\\left\\{\n \\frac{\\max(u^{-3},1)+\\sqrt{\\delta u}}{u}\n +\\sum_{j=0}^\\infty\n \\frac{\\max((2^jh)^{-3},1)+\\sqrt{\\delta\\,2^jh}}\n      {\\max(u,2^jh)}\\right\\}.$$ The constant depends only on the fixed annular ratios and cut-gradient constant, in addition to the fixed screening constants $L,A$. In particular it does not depend on $n,Z,\\delta,h,u$.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-strict-binding-excess**: Strict adjacent binding implies a universal excess-charge bound proportional to the number of fixed nuclei. Beyond that bound, adjacent sector energies agree; the bound is independent of nuclear charges and positions.
- **invariant-neutral-outer-radius**: Every normalized neutral atomic ground state has an outer half-electron radius bounded above and below by positive universal constants. No uniqueness or spherical symmetry is required.
- **invariant-first-ionization-gap**: The neutral atom's first ionization energy stays between positive universal constants across all positive integer nuclear charges.
- **invariant-averaged-transfer-laws**: The observed field and the fresh patch field are compared after averaging under the event law. Their conditional densities arise from different conditioning procedures, and the transfer does not identify their values on individual configurations.
- **invariant-neutral-exterior-mass**: The exterior electronic mass of every normalized neutral ground state obeys a reciprocal-radius upper bound at sufficiently large radius and nuclear charge.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-threshold-state-exclusion**: Treat equality of adjacent sector energies as proof that no ground state exists in the higher sector. -> A state at the binding threshold is not excluded by equality of the energies.
- **anti-explicit-numerical-cutoff**: Use the universal excess-charge constant as a supplied numerical cutoff for a finite computation. -> The conclusion asserts existence of a universal finite constant without supplying a numerical value or a certified finite-dimensional approximation.
- **anti-statistics-extension**: Apply the molecular conclusion to particles without the stated fermionic antisymmetry or spin convention. -> The occupation bound and kinetic density control used in the proof are tied to antisymmetric electronic states.
- **anti-moving-nuclear-model**: Apply the bounds directly to a model with dynamical nuclei. -> Nuclear motion adds terms and degrees of freedom absent from the specified electronic Hamiltonian.
- **anti-radius-as-grid-certificate**: Treat the half-electron radius bound as a certified computational boundary enclosing the complete electronic state. -> The defining radius allows a fixed exterior electronic mass and gives no discretization or energy error certificate.
- **anti-posterior-pathwise-identification**: Substitute the fresh patch field for the observed field on each sampled configuration. -> The transfer uses separate expectation towers under the original observation law and the fresh tilted localization law.
- **anti-growing-offset-comparison**: Use the fixed-offset atomic inverse comparison uniformly while the baseline energy offset grows. -> The smallness threshold depends on the fixed baseline offset and the chosen comparison parameters.
- **anti-prefix-error-pathwise-bound**: Replace the expected intermediate atomic error integral by a deterministic bound before all observations are forgotten. -> Intermediate fields retain observation randomness, and their error estimate is in expectation.
- **anti-general-ionization-extension**: Apply the uniform first-ionization bound unchanged to removal of an increasing number of electrons. -> The corollary concerns the first removal, while the companion asymptotic problem has a different removal regime.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results concern continuum Coulomb Hamiltonians, variational ground states, and universal constants established by analytic arguments. The global comparison proof states: "A bounded minimizing sequence has weakly convergent subsequences in these two reflexive spaces." The excess-charge proof concludes: "This proves the existence of one universal finite constant bounding the excess per nucleus in every strictly bound sector." These arguments do not supply a finite constructive algorithm for the asserted binding, radius, or ionization bounds. Finite enumeration or numerical discretization would test a separate approximation and would not certify the paper's continuum conclusions.

A numerical domain-selection tool needs additional information. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-fixed-nuclear-coulomb-model** (formal_scope): The conclusions concern fixed distinct nuclear positions and real charges at least one in the stated nonrelativistic Coulomb model. Extensions to moving nuclei, different kinetic operators or modified interaction kernels are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Applying the bounds directly to dynamical nuclei.', 'Transferring the conclusions to a relativistic, magnetic or modified-interaction Hamiltonian without a separate proof.']
- **limit-fermionic-spin-convention** (hypothesis): The electronic sectors use joint spatial and spin antisymmetry with the specified spin multiplicity. The occupation and kinetic bounds cannot be transferred to other statistics merely by preserving the Coulomb potential. Blocked use: ['Using the molecular excess bound for bosonic states.', 'Changing the spin convention while retaining the same kinetic and density coefficients.']
- **limit-strict-binding-versus-threshold** (edge_case): Equality of adjacent sector energies excludes a strict drop but does not exclude a ground state at threshold. Blocked use: ['Concluding that a higher sector has no eigenstate solely from adjacent-energy equality.']
- **limit-universal-constants-not-numerical-cutoffs** (nonconstructive): The main results assert universal finite constants without supplying their numerical values. A certified finite-dimensional approximation, computational stopping rule and complexity bound are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Selecting a numerical excess-charge cutoff from an unspecified universal coefficient.', 'Treating the continuum theorem as a finite spatial basis truncation certificate.']
- **limit-neutral-integer-charge-observables** (hypothesis): The radius and first-ionization conclusions concern neutral atoms with positive integer nuclear charge. Their statements do not extend these observables to arbitrary molecular ground states or arbitrary nonneutral sectors. Blocked use: ['Applying the neutral half-electron radius theorem to a molecular system.', 'Using the neutral first-ionization corollary for a general ionic sector or noninteger neutral electron number.']
- **limit-radius-is-exterior-mass-observable** (formal_scope): The radius measures a fixed expected exterior electronic mass, rather than compact support of the state or a fixed fraction of the atomic density. Blocked use: ['Interpreting the radius as a boundary beyond which the density vanishes.', 'Inferring an energy approximation certificate from the radius bound.', 'Replacing the fixed exterior-mass threshold by a fixed fraction without a new argument.']
- **limit-all-sector-energy-baseline** (hypothesis): Local insertion allows variable expected trial mass because every actual integer-sector trial is bounded below by the minimum over all particle sectors. A fixed-sector minimum alone does not justify this comparison. Blocked use: ['Replacing the all-sector minimum by a prescribed sector energy while retaining unrestricted insertion trials.']
- **limit-symmetric-eigenstate-multiplier** (hypothesis): The exact multiplier energy identity requires an eigenstate and a bounded real Lipschitz multiplier invariant under particle permutations. Blocked use: ['Applying the identity unchanged to an arbitrary approximate state.', 'Using a nonsymmetric multiplier that destroys the stated electronic sector.']
- **limit-separated-hole-trial** (hypothesis): Coherent insertion requires a nonnegative compactly supported admissible trial density and positive spatial separation between its packet region and the allowed core support. Blocked use: ['Wedge insertion with overlapping core and packet supports while retaining the separated-group energy formula.', 'Using a trial density outside the stated integrability class.']
- **limit-coarse-versus-sharp-kinetic-coefficient** (approximation): The positive coarse kinetic constant is not identified with the sharp Thomas–Fermi coefficient. Matching sharp coefficients requires the coherent upper and Neumann lower comparisons. Blocked use: ['Substituting the Thomas–Fermi coefficient for the unspecified coarse kinetic constant.', 'Dropping the fine-density remainder terms while claiming an exact quantum density law.']
- **limit-distinct-screening-geometries** (hypothesis): The nuclear scale and atomic distance scale serve different applications. The atomic distance scale does not inherit the nuclear scale's global lower length bound. Blocked use: ['Using the nuclear lower scale bound in atomic patches below the bulk length.', 'Applying source-free atomic patch estimates to molecular patches containing nuclei.']
- **limit-admissible-initial-cuts** (hypothesis): Conditional positive-field bounds require the stated finite covering and squared-gradient conditions for the initial cuts; comparable covering scales are additionally required for the simplified local field bound. Blocked use: ['Applying the conditional field estimate to unrestricted cuts with uncontrolled localization cost.', 'Replacing the largest covering-cell energy unit by the target energy unit without scale comparability.']
- **limit-refined-offset-ceiling** (hypothesis): The refined local comparison has a small-scale energy-offset ceiling. General finite-offset count estimates do not remove that extra restriction. Blocked use: ['Applying the fine Coulomb discrepancy estimate at an arbitrary finite offset.', 'Using the refined patch outside its prescribed scale and width regime.']
- **limit-observation-event-contract** (hypothesis): The observation-event energy estimate requires the specified noise likelihood, an eigenstate baseline, a positive-probability data event and the inverse-width square-sum condition. Its offset retains the original sector energy above the all-sector minimum. Blocked use: ['Conditioning on a zero-probability event.', 'Dropping the baseline sector offset from the tilted energy budget.', 'Assuming an arbitrary observation noise law has the same dimension-independent cost.']
- **limit-observed-field-regularity** (formal_scope): Smooth conditional densities do not remove singularities in the retained nuclear potential. Regularity bounds for a fixed system are not asserted uniformly in the nuclear system or the minimum smoothing width. Blocked use: ['Treating the atomic observed field as globally bounded and continuously differentiable at the nucleus.', 'Promoting fixed-system derivative bounds to uniform bounds as smoothing widths vanish.']
- **limit-posterior-transfer-only-after-averaging** (hypothesis): Observed and fresh patch fields use different conditional laws. Their comparison is in expectation under the specified event law and requires all displayed kernel, density, deletion and Coulomb-error hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Identifying the observed and fresh patch posterior fields on individual configurations.', 'Applying the transfer estimate after checking only the Coulomb discrepancy budget.']
- **limit-negative-field-exceptional-cost** (hypothesis): A small exceptional probability alone does not control the mean of arbitrarily negative patch fields. Passing the lower field estimate to expectation also uses retained local mass, oscillation and the negative-field density penalty. Blocked use: ['Discarding exceptional patch data solely on the basis of its small probability.', 'Assuming a deterministic lower cap on the patch field.']
- **limit-weak-maxima-on-open-neighborhoods** (hypothesis): The maximum rule requires continuous locally Sobolev branches and the stated locally bounded reaction. In the singular version, common nuclear terms must cancel and the reaction must vanish near their support. Blocked use: ['Differentiating across an arbitrary activity set as though each branch inequality were a classical pointwise identity.', 'Applying the singular maximum rule without checking its reaction condition.']
- **limit-molecular-pointwise-exceptions** (formal_scope): Molecular inverse comparisons control failure separately at each deterministic spatial point. The propagation argument integrates exceptional costs rather than asserting a simultaneous good event throughout space. Blocked use: ['Replacing pointwise molecular failure probabilities by a global spatial success event.', 'Removing the exceptional error density from the outward invariant.']
- **limit-fixed-offset-order-of-choices** (hypothesis): The atomic inverse comparison fixes a finite baseline offset and comparison parameters before choosing a sufficiently small outer scale and sufficiently large nuclear charge. It is not uniform at arbitrary growing offsets. Blocked use: ['Keeping the outer scale fixed while allowing the baseline offset to grow arbitrarily.', 'Choosing incompatible master smoothing for comparisons that must share conditional fields.']
- **limit-prefix-errors-remain-random** (formal_scope): Intermediate atomic error control is in expectation before the last observations are forgotten. The terminal fields are deterministic, but intermediate fields generally are not. Blocked use: ['Replacing an expected prefix error integral by a deterministic bound.', 'Changing the master kernels when stopping at an intermediate index.']
- **limit-shell-bias-preserves-field-conditionally** (hypothesis): The shell-weighted change of law preserves the surviving conditional field because the weight is measurable in the revealed cut data. The zero-weight case is handled without forming a normalized biased state. Blocked use: ['Reweighting by an unrevealed configuration observable while retaining the original conditional core field.', 'Dividing by a vanishing expected shell mass.']
- **limit-large-charge-tail-versus-all-charge-radius** (formal_scope): The reciprocal exterior-mass estimate has large-charge and large-radius thresholds. The all-charge radius theorem separately uses compactness and density estimates for the remaining charges. Blocked use: ['Applying the exterior-mass estimate below its stated thresholds.', 'Assuming the all-charge radius theorem supplies a numerical reciprocal tail coefficient for every charge.']
- **limit-first-removal-not-general-ionization** (formal_scope): The uniform energy corollary concerns the first electron removal from a neutral atom. The companion growing-removal asymptotic regime requires additional arguments. Blocked use: ['Applying the same universal first-removal bounds unchanged to an increasing number of removed electrons.']

If $V_s$ and its gradient are bounded and continuous, these conclusions hold for $H_j$ itself. Conditional averaging satisfies $$\\label{eq:obs-tower}\n \\mathbb E_P[\\mu_j(y)\\mid\\mathcal F_{j+1}]=\\mu_{j+1}(y),\\qquad\n \\mathbb E_P[H_j(y)\\mid\\mathcal F_{j+1}]=H_{j+1}(y)$$ where $V_s(y)$ is finite. The same towers hold for nonnegative spatial integrals, and for integrable signed observables. These statements include the trivial data at $j=m$.           "exact_quote": "<span id=\"m:lem:screen-patch\" label=\"m:lem:screen-patch\"></span>Fix $y\\in\\mathcal Y$, write $a=a_y$, $m=m_y$, $e=e_y$, and choose $0<\\beta<1/100$ such that $$\\label{m:eq:screen-patch-condition}\n \\frac{\\beta^{-2}}{am}\\le\\sqrt P.$$ Set $b=\\beta a$. Write $$\\label{m:eq:screen-remainder}\n \\mathcal L=c_{\\mathrm{TF}}\\int\\left(\\sigma^{5/3}-\\rho^{5/3}\n              -\\frac53\\rho^{2/3}(\\sigma-\\rho)\\right).$$ For every sufficiently small $\\epsilon>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{m:eq:screen-comparison}\n &\\mathbb E\\left[D(\\sigma-\\rho)+\\mathcal L+\\int W_-\\sigma\\right]\\\\\n &\\quad\\le C\\left[\\delta+(\\epsilon+\\sqrt\\beta)Pe\n       +\\epsilon^{-3/2}\\beta^{1/2}a^{-7}\n       +b^{-1}\\sqrt Pm\n       +b^{-2}\\left(P^{2/3}m^{4/3}+\\sqrt Pm\\right)\\right].\\notag\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, $\\mathbb E\\int\\sigma^{5/3}$ is bounded by $CPe$ plus a constant times this right-hand side, and the out kinetic energy obeys $$\\label{m:eq:screen-out-kinetic}\n T_o\\le C\\left(Pe+b^{-2}\\sqrt Pm\\right).$$ Finally, independently of the data, $$\\label{m:eq:screen-interior-upper}\n W\\le V_{\\rm loc}+Ca^{-4}\\quad\\hbox{on }B(y,3a),\\qquad\n \\int_{B(y,2a)}\\rho\\le Ca^{-3},$$ where $V_{\\rm loc}$ is the potential of nuclei in $B(y,20a)$. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that a molecule with <i>M</i> fixed nuclei of real charges at least one and total nuclear charge <i>Z</i> strictly binds at most $`Z+CM`$ electrons, where <i>C</i> is universal. The result holds for the full nonrelativistic Coulomb Hamiltonian with two spin states and arbitrary distinct nuclear positions. For neutral atoms with integer nuclear charge <i>Z</i> ≥ 1, the first ionization energy and, for every ground state, the radius outside which one half of an electron remains are each bounded above and below by positive universal constants.
