     \le C\|\nabla V_s\|_2\sqrt{D(\rho)},\qquad
 \left|\int V_c\rho\right|
     \le\|V_c\|_{5/2}\|\rho\|_{5/3}.$$ For the fixed source and smearing widths, both displayed potential norms are finite. Young’s inequality proves coercivity in both spaces defining $\mathcal X_+$. A bounded minimizing sequence has weakly convergent subsequences in these two reflexive spaces. Their limits agree as distributions, since both converge against compact smooth tests; nonnegativity passes to this limit. The two linear terms are weakly continuous in their respective spaces, and both positive terms are weakly lower semicontinuous. This proves existence. The strictly convex kinetic term proves uniqueness.

Nonnegative smooth compact variations give $$d:=\tfrac53c_{\mathrm{TF}}\rho^{2/3}-V_\zeta+K*\rho\ge0
 \quad\text{almost everywhere}.$$ For bounded smooth compactly supported $\varphi$, the variations $(1+t\varphi)\rho$ are admissible for sufficiently small positive and negative $t$. Indeed $|\varphi\rho|\le\|\varphi\|_\infty\rho$, and positivity of the Coulomb kernel bounds its Coulomb energy. The nonnegative pairing property in Lemma <a href="#lem:loc-coulomb" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:loc-coulomb">10</a> therefore justifies differentiating these variations and gives $\int d\varphi\rho=0$. Hence $d\rho=0$, proving the Euler equation in <a href="#m:eq:global-tf-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:global-tf-equation">[m:eq:global-tf-equation]</a> and its distributional Laplacian.

To establish finite mass without assuming it, let the source components have centers $R_m$ and masses $Q_m$, and put $$U(x)=\sum_m\frac{Q_m}{|x-R_m|},\qquad w(x)=U(x)^{-1}.$$ Newton’s theorem gives $V_\zeta\le U$. Where $\rho>0$, the Euler equation gives $K*\rho\le V_\zeta$. For any ball $B$, multiply this inequality by $1_Bw\rho$ and restrict the screening integral below to $B$. Write $q_m(x)=w(x)Q_m/|x-R_m|$, so that $\sum_mq_m=1$. The triangle inequality gives $$\frac{w(x)+w(y)}{|x-y|}
     \ge\sum_m\frac{q_m(x)q_m(y)}{Q_m}.$$ The resulting symmetrization and Cauchy–Schwarz yield, with $m_B=\int_B\rho$, $$m_B\ge\frac12\sum_m\frac{(\int_Bq_m\rho)^2}{Q_m}
       \ge\frac{m_B^2}{2Q}.$$ All integrals in this calculation are local and finite; in particular $w$ is bounded on $B$, and the local Coulomb energy is finite. Increasing $B$ proves $\int\rho\le2Q$.

For completeness, $L^1\cap L^{5/3}$ implies continuity and decay of the screening potential. The kernel restricted to a fixed ball belongs to $L^{5/2}$, so its pairing with translates of $\rho$ is continuous. The complementary kernel contributes at most $\|\rho\|_1/R$ when the cutoff radius is $R$. For fixed $R$, the local $L^{5/3}$ norm of $\rho$ on a ball whose center tends to infinity tends to zero. First letting the center tend to infinity and then $R\to\infty$ proves decay.

Finally, $(W+\varepsilon)_-$ is compactly supported away from point charges for every $\varepsilon>0$: $W\to0$ at infinity, and its positive Coulomb singularities dominate the continuous screening potential. On the support of this test $\rho=0$, and hence $\Delta W\le0$. Testing the equation gives $\int|\nabla(W+\varepsilon)_-|^2\le0$. The test is justified by compact $H^1$ approximation away from the singularities. Letting $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ proves $W\ge0$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-m-lem-barrier-mixture"></a>
<div id="m:lem:barrier-mixture" class="lemma">

<a id="m:eq:single-field-lower"></a>
**Lemma 32** (Point fields and mixtures). *For one point charge $Q>0$ at $R$, the field in Lemma <a href="#m:lem:barrier-global-tf" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:barrier-global-tf">31</a> satisfies $$\label{m:eq:single-field-lower}
 W(x)\ge c\min\left(\frac{Q}{|x-R|},|x-R|^{-4}\right).$$ For a single point charge or radial smooth charge of mass $Q$, its screening potential is bounded by $CQ^{4/3}$.*

*More generally, select source components of masses $Q_i$, truncate them to masses $q_i\le Q_i$, and put $q=\sum_iq_i>0$. Let $W_i$ be the single-source field of mass $q$ at the $i$th center with that source’s normalized profile. If $W$ is the full-source field, then <a id="m:eq:tf-mixture"></a>$$\label{m:eq:tf-mixture}
 \sum_i\frac{q_i}{q}W_i\le W.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-m-lem-barrier-mixture"><summary>Proof of stmt-m-lem-barrier-mixture</summary>

<a id="m:eq:local-power-potential"></a>
*Proof.* For a single source, Newton’s theorem and the Euler equation give $\rho(z)\le k(Q/|z-R|)^{3/2}$. For every $x,R$ and $s>0$, $$\label{m:eq:local-power-potential}
 \int_{B(x,s)}\frac{dz}{|x-z|\,|z-R|^{3/2}}
       \le Cs^{1/2}.$$ To see the uniformity in $R$, apply Hölder with exponent $q_1\in(3/2,2)$ to the second factor and its conjugate $q_1'\in(2,3)$ to the first. The integrals of both radial powers over $B(x,s)$ are bounded by their integrals over centered balls of the same radius. Their powers of $s$ combine to $s^{1/2}$. Split $K*\rho(x)$ at $s=Q^{-1/3}$. The near part is at most $CQ^{3/2}s^{1/2}$ by <a href="#m:eq:local-power-potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:local-power-potential">[m:eq:local-power-potential]</a>, and the far part at most $2Q/s$ by Lemma <a href="#m:lem:barrier-global-tf" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:barrier-global-tf">31</a>. Both are $CQ^{4/3}$.

For a point charge this gives $W\ge Q/(2d)$ for $d=|x-R|\le cQ^{-1/3}$. Outside that sphere set $v=\lambda d^{-4}$. For sufficiently small universal $\lambda>0$, $$\Delta v=12\lambda d^{-6}\ge c_{\mathrm F}v^{3/2},$$ and its boundary value is below the preceding lower bound. On the exterior domain, testing the equation for $v-W$ with $(v-W-\varepsilon)_+$ gives a nonpositive gradient square bounded below by zero. This test has compact support since both fields decay. Thus $v\le W$. Together with the inner estimate this proves <a href="#m:eq:single-field-lower" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:single-field-lower">[m:eq:single-field-lower]</a>.

For the mixture $F=\sum_i(q_i/q)W_i$, convexity gives $$\Delta F\ge-4\pi\zeta_{\rm truncated}+c_{\mathrm F}F^p
            \ge-4\pi\zeta+c_{\mathrm F}F^p.$$ Compare with the equation for $W$ and test with $(F-W-\varepsilon)_+$. The support is compact. Near a point charge whose mass was strictly decreased, $F-W$ tends to $-\infty$, so the test vanishes there. If the masses agree, the singular terms cancel and the difference is locally $H^1$. The remaining density terms are locally in $L^{5/3}$, so compact $H^1$ approximation is valid. Monotonicity of the power gives $F\le W$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-40"></a>
#### Fields adapted to the nuclear scale

Fix a sufficiently small constant $c_1\in(0,1/100)$. Smear the $m$th nucleus with the radial probability $g_{c_1a_{R_m}}^2$ and denote the resulting density by $\nu_s$. Put <a id="m:eq:nuclear-splitting"></a>$$\label{m:eq:nuclear-splitting}
 V_s=K*\nu_s,\qquad V_c=V-V_s\ge0.$$ The subscript $c$ records the part confined near the nuclei. A nucleus contributing to $V_c(x)$ lies within $2c_1a_x$ of $x$. Its scale is comparable to $a_x$, and the total contributing charge is at most $a_x^{-3}$. Therefore <a id="m:eq:smoothed-source-bounds"></a>$$\label{m:eq:smoothed-source-bounds}
 \nu_s(x)\le Ca_x^{-6},\qquad V_c\in L^{5/2}_c(\mathbb R^3).$$ The smooth potentials of nuclei within any of the fixed local multiples of $a_x$ used below sum to at most $Ca_x^{-4}$. Indeed each such width is comparable to $a_x$, each unit-mass smooth potential is at most $C/a_x$, and the total local charge is at most $a_x^{-3}$.

Let $W_0,\rho_0$ be the field and density for the original nuclear measure $\nu$. Its continuous offset is $$h_0=W_0-V_c=V_s-K*\rho_0.$$ For a fixed positive $\theta$, to be chosen below, let $W_s$ be the field for the smooth source $\theta\nu_s$.

<a id="stmt-m-lem-barrier-fields"></a>
<div id="m:lem:barrier-fields" class="lemma">

<a id="m:eq:reference-field-bounds"></a>
**Lemma 33** (Two reference fields). *There are fixed constants $c_0>0$, $\theta>0$, $c_\theta>0$, and $C<\infty$ such that, off the nuclei, $$\label{m:eq:reference-field-bounds}
 W_0(x)\ge c_0a_x^{-4},\qquad |h_0(x)|\le Ca_x^{-4},$$ and everywhere <a id="m:eq:smooth-reference-bounds"></a>$$\label{m:eq:smooth-reference-bounds}
 c_\theta a_x^{-4}\le W_s(x)\le Ca_x^{-4},\qquad
 |\nabla W_s(x)|\le Ca_x^{-5},\qquad W_s(x)\le1.01W_0(x).$$ Here $h_0$ is continuous, and $W_s$ is locally $C^1$. Both fields decay at infinity.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-m-lem-barrier-fields"><summary>Proof of stmt-m-lem-barrier-fields</summary>

*Proof.* By the scale definition, $\nu(B(x,2La_x))>(2a_x)^{-3}$. Truncate charges in this ball to total $q=(2a_x)^{-3}$ and apply Lemma <a href="#m:lem:barrier-mixture" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:barrier-mixture">32</a>. Their distances from $x$ are at most $2La_x$, so <a href="#m:eq:single-field-lower" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:single-field-lower">[m:eq:single-field-lower]</a> gives the first bound in <a href="#m:eq:reference-field-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:reference-field-bounds">[m:eq:reference-field-bounds]</a> with a fixed $c_0>0$.

If some charges contribute to $V_c(x)$, denote their total by $q\le a_x^{-3}$. The screening estimate for each single field of charge $q$, followed by the mixture comparison, gives $$W_0(x)\ge\sum_{\rm contributors}\frac{z_m}{|x-R_m|}-Cq^{4/3}
          \ge V_c(x)-Ca_x^{-4}.$$ If there are no contributors, the same conclusion follows from $W_0\ge0$.

For the upper bound, set $a=a_x$, $s=4a$, and let $V_{\rm loc}$ be the potential of nuclei within $20a$ of $x$. On $B(x,s)$ the function $$V_{\rm loc}(z)+\frac{C s^4}{(s^2-|z-x|^2)^4}$$ is a supersolution of the field equation when $C$ is large: the Laplacian of the second term is at most $80Cs^6/(s^2-|z-x|^2)^6$, whereas its $p$th power has the same spatial dependence and coefficient $C^{3/2}$. The boundary blowup dominates $W_0-V_{\rm loc}$. The positive-difference test is legitimate because the point singularities cancel. Thus $W_0(x)\le V_{\rm loc}(x)+Ca^{-4}$. Subtracting $V_c(x)$ and using the smooth local potential bound proves the upper offset estimate. The expression defining $h_0$ proves its continuity.

For $W_s$, the same bump without $V_{\rm loc}$ is a supersolution after its coefficient is enlarged to absorb the locally bounded source $4\pi\theta\nu_s$. This proves $W_s\le Ca^{-4}$. Its Laplacian is bounded by $Ca^{-6}$ on a local ball. Subtract the Newton potential of this source restricted to that ball. Its gradient is bounded by $Ca^{-5}$, since the integral of $|z|^{-2}$ on a ball of radius $a$ is $Ca$. The remaining harmonic function is bounded by $Ca^{-4}$ on a smaller ball; its interior gradient bound proves the stated estimate and local $C^1$ regularity.

For the lower bound, select and truncate the same charges as in the first paragraph, before multiplying them by $\theta$. Their smearing widths are at most $3c_1a$, because their centers are within $2La$. A single such blob of charge $\theta(2a)^{-3}$ has bare potential at $x$ at least $c_L\theta a^{-4}$: every source point is within $(2L+3c_1)a$ of $x$. Its screening potential is at most $C\theta^{4/3}a^{-4}$. Choose $\theta$ sufficiently small and apply the mixture comparison to obtain $W_s\ge c_\theta a^{-4}$.

Decrease $\theta$ further if necessary. The source bound and the lower bound for $W_0$ imply $$4\pi\theta\nu_s
   \le c_{\mathrm F}\bigl(1.01^{3/2}-1.01\bigr)W_0^{3/2}.$$ Consequently $1.01W_0$ is a supersolution for the field of $\theta\nu_s$; its extra negative point sources have the required sign. The positive-difference test for $W_s-1.01W_0$, supported away from those point singularities, proves the final comparison. Decay follows from Lemma <a href="#m:lem:barrier-global-tf" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:barrier-global-tf">31</a>. ◻

</details>

The reference fields now have positive lower bounds at every scale. To compare them with the local quantum constructions, we also need to control variation over a much smaller ball. The offset is the right quantity for this purpose: subtracting $V_c$ removes every nuclear singularity from the function being compared.

<a id="stmt-m-lem-barrier-oscillation"></a>
<div id="m:lem:barrier-oscillation" class="lemma">

<a id="m:eq:offset-oscillation"></a>
**Lemma 34** (Oscillation of offsets). *Fix $y$ and write $a=a_y$. Let $h=h_0$, or let $h=W-V_c$ for an interior Thomas–Fermi patch centered at $y$ satisfying the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#m:lem:screen-patch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:screen-patch">[m:lem:screen-patch]</a>. Then $h$ has a continuous representative. For $0<s\le a/10$ and $|z-y|\le s$, $$\label{m:eq:offset-oscillation}
 |h(z)-h(y)|\le Ca^{-4}(s/a)^{1/2}+C(s/a)h(y)_-.$$ For $h=h_0$ the final term can be omitted.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-m-lem-barrier-oscillation"><summary>Proof of stmt-m-lem-barrier-oscillation</summary>

*Proof.* In either case the local upper field estimate in <a href="#m:eq:screen-interior-upper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:screen-interior-upper">[m:eq:screen-interior-upper]</a> or Lemma <a href="#m:lem:barrier-fields" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:barrier-fields">33</a> gives $h\le Ca^{-4}$ on a fixed ball about $y$. The patch core is absent there. The Euler equation, the bound on $\nu_s$, and the weighted power inequality give <a id="m:eq:offset-laplacian-bound"></a>$$\label{m:eq:offset-laplacian-bound}
 |\Delta h(z)|\le Ca^{-6}
     +Ca^{-3/2}\sum_{\rm loc}z_m|z-R_m|^{-3/2}.$$ Here all centers in the sum belong to one fixed local ball and their total charge is at most $a^{-3}$. The same estimate holds for $h_0$ by Lemma <a href="#m:lem:barrier-fields" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:barrier-fields">33</a>.

Take the Newton particular solution for this Laplacian restricted to a ball of radius $a$. Equation <a href="#m:eq:local-power-potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:local-power-potential">[m:eq:local-power-potential]</a> bounds it by $Ca^{-4}$ on a smaller ball. Its variation over distance $s$ is at most $Ca^{-4}(s/a)^{1/2}$. In detail, the part within distance $Cs$ of either evaluation point is at most $$C\bigl(a^{-6}s^2+a^{-9/2}s^{1/2}\bigr).$$ On a dyadic ring of radius $l\ge Cs$, the difference of the two Coulomb kernels is at most $Cs/l^2$, and the source mass in that ring is at most $C(a^{-6}l^3+a^{-9/2}l^{3/2})$. Summing the rings up to radius $a$ gives the asserted variation.

The harmonic remainder is bounded above by $Ca^{-4}$. Harnack’s inequality and the harmonic gradient estimate, applied to this upper constant minus the remainder, bound its variation by $C(s/a)(a^{-4}+h(y)_-)$. Combining the estimates gives <a href="#m:eq:offset-oscillation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:offset-oscillation">[m:eq:offset-oscillation]</a>, as well as continuity. For $h_0$, Lemma <a href="#m:lem:barrier-fields" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:barrier-fields">33</a> bounds its negative part by $Ca^{-4}$, which is absorbed by the first term. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-41"></a>
#### Averaging at a smaller radius

<a id="m:eq:master-kernel"></a>
Fix $w=10^{-5}$, and use the small parameter $r_*$ fixed above. Define the smearing widths, center kernels, and averaging operator by $$\label{m:eq:master-kernel}
 t_z=c_1a_z\min(a_z,r_*)^w,\qquad
 \mathcal K_z(y)=g_{t_z}^2(y-z),\qquad
 Rf(y)=\int\mathcal K_z(y)f(z)\,dz,
 \qquad \tau_y=t_y/a_y.$$ Thus $R$ smooths a mass at its own center-dependent width. It preserves total mass, although it need not preserve the constant function exactly.

<a id="stmt-m-lem-barrier-kernel"></a>
<div id="m:lem:barrier-kernel" class="lemma">

<a id="m:eq:master-kernel-bounds"></a>
<a id="m:eq:potential-correction-bound"></a>
**Lemma 35** (Kernel and potential estimates). *Uniformly as $r_*\downarrow0$, a center contributing to $Rf(y)$ satisfies $|z-y|\le2t_y$ and $t_z/t_y=1+O(r_*^w)$. Moreover, $$\label{m:eq:master-kernel-bounds}
 R1(y)=1+O(r_*^w),\qquad
 \|\nabla_z\mathcal K_z(y)\|_\infty\le Ct_y^{-4},\qquad
 \|\nabla_z\mathcal K_z(y)\|_2\le Ct_y^{-5/2}.$$ The norms in these gradient estimates are taken in the center variable $z$. The operator $R$ is bounded on $L^{5/3}$ and preserves integrals. The correction <a id="m:eq:potential-correction"></a>$$\label{m:eq:potential-correction}
 q_0=K*(\rho_0-R\rho_0)$$ is nonnegative, continuous, belongs to $\dot H^1$, and tends to zero at infinity. It satisfies $$\label{m:eq:potential-correction-bound}
 0\le q_0(y)\le Ca_y^{-4}\tau_y^{1/2}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-m-lem-barrier-kernel"><summary>Proof of stmt-m-lem-barrier-kernel</summary>

*Proof.* The scale function is $1/L$-Lipschitz. The function $a\mapsto c_1a\min(a,r_*)^w$ has Lipschitz constant $O(r_*^w)$. Thus $|t_z-t_y|\le O(r_*^w)|z-y|$. If $|z-y|\le t_z$, this proves the support and comparability assertions. Replacing $t_z$ by $t_y$ changes the kernel by at most $Cr_*^wt_y^{-3}$ on a ball of radius $2t_y$, giving the first assertion of <a href="#m:eq:master-kernel-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:master-kernel-bounds">[m:eq:master-kernel-bounds]</a>. The chain rule for a Lipschitz width gives the gradient bounds, first pointwise almost everywhere and then in $L^2$ by the support bound. Since $\int\mathcal K_z(y)\,dy=1$, Fubini proves preservation of integrals. Weighted Jensen followed by Fubini, using the bound on $R1$, proves the $L^{5/3}$ estimate.

Newton’s theorem gives the nonnegative representation $$q_0(y)=\int\rho_0(z)
   \bigl[K(y-z)-(K*g_{t_z}^2)(y-z)\bigr]\,dz.$$ Only $|z-y|\le2t_y$ contributes. The local field bound gives $$\rho_0(z)\le Ca_y^{-6}
    +Ca_y^{-3/2}\sum_{\rm loc}z_m|z-R_m|^{-3/2}.$$ Equation <a href="#m:eq:local-power-potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:local-power-potential">[m:eq:local-power-potential]</a> therefore bounds the displayed integral by $C(a_y^{-6}t_y^2+a_y^{-9/2}t_y^{1/2})$, proving <a href="#m:eq:potential-correction-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:potential-correction-bound">[m:eq:potential-correction-bound]</a>. Both $\rho_0$ and $R\rho_0$ belong to $L^1\cap L^{5/3}$, hence also to $L^{6/5}$. Their potentials are continuous and decay by the proof of Lemma <a href="#m:lem:barrier-global-tf" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:barrier-global-tf">31</a>; the Sobolev inequality and the Coulomb energy identity put their difference in $\dot H^1$. ◻

</details>

<a id="m:eq:offset-reaction"></a>
Define the nonlinearity acting on an offset $h\in\mathbb R$ by $$\label{m:eq:offset-reaction}
 f_y(h)=c_{\mathrm F}\int\mathcal K_z(y)(V_c(z)+h)_+^p\,dz.$$ For each $y$, this is finite, convex, continuous, and nondecreasing in $h$. These properties follow from those of the positive power and the local integrability of $V_c^p$. We will use this averaged law in place of the unsmeared Thomas–Fermi equation.

<a id="pm-h-42"></a>
#### Barriers with a strict gap between scales

We have established the reference fields and the averaging estimates. The remaining task is to lower the reference field just enough that its averaged density pays for the Laplacian of the lowering term. The strict inequality between the coefficients below leaves room for all errors from averaging.

<a id="stmt-m-lem-barrier-subsolution"></a>
<div id="m:lem:barrier-subsolution" class="lemma">

<a id="m:eq:barrier-subsolution"></a>
<a id="m:eq:reaction-positive-margin"></a>
**Lemma 36** (Comparison barriers). *There are fixed positive constants $\alpha,\epsilon_0,C'_0,c_b,c'$ with the following properties for all sufficiently small $r_*>0$. For $0<r\le r_*$ define <a id="m:eq:barrier-definition"></a>$$\label{m:eq:barrier-definition}
 \Gamma_r=\epsilon_0W_s+C'_0r^{4\alpha}W_s^{1+\alpha},\qquad
 B_r=W_0+q_0-\Gamma_r,\qquad b_r=B_r-V_c,
 \qquad h_b=h_0-c_ba^{-4}.$$ The offsets $b_r$ are continuous and locally $H^1$ and decay at infinity. On $\{a\ge r\}$ their Laplacian densities satisfy $$\label{m:eq:barrier-subsolution}
 \Delta b_r\ge-4\pi\nu_s+f_y(b_r).$$ More precisely, this inequality holds almost everywhere there between the locally integrable densities representing the two sides; on every open set contained in this region it is also a distributional inequality. On the same region, <a id="m:eq:barrier-bounds"></a>$$\label{m:eq:barrier-bounds}
 B_r\ge0.9W_0>0,\qquad
 h_b+c'a^{-4}\le b_r\le Ca^{-4}.$$ For every contributing center $z$ in <a href="#m:eq:offset-reaction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:offset-reaction">[m:eq:offset-reaction]</a>, $$\label{m:eq:reaction-positive-margin}
 V_c(z)+h_b(y)\ge ca_y^{-4}
 \quad\text{almost everywhere in }z.$$ All these constants are independent of $r,r_*$ and the nuclear system.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-m-lem-barrier-subsolution"><summary>Proof of stmt-m-lem-barrier-subsolution</summary>

<a id="m:eq:damping-laplacian"></a>
*Proof.* First fix $\theta$ as in Lemma <a href="#m:lem:barrier-fields" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:barrier-fields">33</a>. That lemma implies $$D_*:=\sup_x\frac{|\nabla W_s(x)|^2}{W_s(x)^{5/2}}<\infty$$ with a fixed universal bound. Since $\Delta W_s\le c_{\mathrm F}W_s^{3/2}$, the chain rule gives $$\Delta(W_s^{1+\alpha})
 \le(1+\alpha)(c_{\mathrm F}+\alpha D_*)W_s^{\alpha+3/2}.$$ Choose $\alpha>0$ so small that $(1+\alpha)(1+\alpha D_*/c_{\mathrm F})\le1.1$. Consequently $$\label{m:eq:damping-laplacian}
 \Delta\Gamma_r\le1.1c_{\mathrm F}\sqrt{W_s}\,\Gamma_r.$$ All chain rules are local: $W_s$ is positive and locally $C^1$ with locally bounded Laplacian, so multiplication in the weak equation justifies them.

<a id="m:eq:damping-lower"></a>
Choose $c_b>0$ smaller than a sufficiently small fixed fraction of $c_0$. On $a\ge r$, $r^{4\alpha}W_s^\alpha\le C^\alpha$. We may therefore choose positive $\epsilon_0,C'_0$ so small that, throughout this region, $\Gamma_r$ is a tiny fixed fraction of $W_s$, is at most $0.1c_0a^{-4}$, and is at most $(c_b/2)a^{-4}$. At the same time, $$\label{m:eq:damping-lower}
 \Gamma_r\ge\epsilon_0c_\theta a^{-4}.$$ These choices precede every smallness requirement on $r_*$.

<a id="m:eq:smoothed-reference-equation"></a>
The equations for $h_0$ and $q_0$ give the exact cancellation $$\label{m:eq:smoothed-reference-equation}
 \Delta(h_0+q_0)=-4\pi\nu_s+c_{\mathrm F}R(W_0^p).$$ Fix $y$ with $a_y\ge r$. For a contributing center $z$, the offset oscillation estimate and <a href="#m:eq:potential-correction-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:potential-correction-bound">[m:eq:potential-correction-bound]</a> imply $$\begin{aligned}
 V_c(z)+b_r(y)
 &=W_0(z)+h_0(y)-h_0(z)+q_0(y)-\Gamma_r(y)\\
 &\le W_0(z)-\Gamma_r(y)+Ca_y^{-4}\tau_y^{1/2}.
\end{aligned}$$ By <a href="#m:eq:damping-lower" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:damping-lower">[m:eq:damping-lower]</a>, the final error is $o(\Gamma_r(y))$ uniformly as $r_*\to0$. Furthermore, $$W_0(z)\ge\frac{W_s(z)}{1.01}
      \ge\frac{1-o(1)}{1.01}W_s(y),$$ using the gradient and lower bounds for $W_s$ and $|z-y|\le2t_y$. The chosen small ratio $\Gamma_r/W_s$ and the derivative of the power therefore give $$W_0(z)^p-(V_c(z)+b_r(y))_+^p
       \ge 1.35\sqrt{W_s(y)}\,\Gamma_r(y)$$ for sufficiently small damping fractions and then sufficiently small $r_*$. Indeed the limiting coefficient is $p/\sqrt{1.01}>1.49$. After integration in $z$, the factor $R1(y)=1+o(1)$ leaves the lower bound $1.3\sqrt{W_s(y)}\Gamma_r(y)$. Combine this with <a href="#m:eq:smoothed-reference-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:smoothed-reference-equation">[m:eq:smoothed-reference-equation]</a> and <a href="#m:eq:damping-laplacian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:damping-laplacian">[m:eq:damping-laplacian]</a> to prove <a href="#m:eq:barrier-subsolution" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:barrier-subsolution">[m:eq:barrier-subsolution]</a>. All terms represent locally integrable densities, so the assertion includes points of a level set of $a$ up to a Lebesgue-null set; no regularity of those level sets is required.

Because $q_0\ge0$ and $\Gamma_r\le0.1W_0$, we have $B_r\ge0.9W_0$. The upper offset bound follows from $b_r\le h_0+q_0$. Also $$b_r-h_b=q_0-\Gamma_r+c_ba^{-4}\ge(c_b/2)a^{-4},$$ so one may take $c'=c_b/2$. On the kernel support, $$V_c(z)+h_b(y)
   =W_0(z)+h_0(y)-h_0(z)-c_ba_y^{-4}
   \ge ca_y^{-4},$$ by the lower bound for $W_0$, scale comparability, and the offset oscillation estimate, after decreasing $r_*$ further. This proves <a href="#m:eq:reaction-positive-margin" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:reaction-positive-margin">[m:eq:reaction-positive-margin]</a>.

Finally, $h_0,q_0$ are continuous and locally $H^1$, and the same is true of $\Gamma_r$. Their decay proves the remaining assertions. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-m-lem-barrier-gap"></a>
<div id="m:lem:barrier-gap" class="lemma">

<a id="m:eq:barrier-gap"></a>
**Lemma 37** (Gap under doubling). *Fix $\kappa\in(0,1/10)$. The barriers in Lemma <a href="#m:lem:barrier-subsolution" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:barrier-subsolution">36</a> have a fixed constant $\gamma>0$ such that, whenever $R_+=2r\le r_*$, $$\label{m:eq:barrier-gap}
 b_r-b_{R_+}\ge\gamma R_+^{-4}
 \qquad\text{on }\{R_+\le a\le(1+\kappa)R_+\}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-m-lem-barrier-gap"><summary>Proof of stmt-m-lem-barrier-gap</summary>

*Proof.* The difference is exactly $$b_r-b_{R_+}
   =C'_0(1-2^{-4\alpha})R_+^{4\alpha}W_s^{1+\alpha}.$$ The lower bound for $W_s$ gives the assertion with $$\gamma=C'_0(1-2^{-4\alpha})
       c_\theta^{1+\alpha}(1+\kappa)^{-4(1+\alpha)}>0.$$ ◻

</details>

The lower margin in <a href="#m:eq:reaction-positive-margin" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:reaction-positive-margin">[m:eq:reaction-positive-margin]</a> also controls inversion of the averaged law. If $\delta_0>0$ is a sufficiently small fixed constant, differentiation under the integral gives <a id="m:eq:barrier-reaction-derivative"></a>$$\label{m:eq:barrier-reaction-derivative}
 \frac{d}{dh}\frac{f_y(h)}{4\pi}\ge ca_y^{-2}
 \qquad\text{for }h\ge h_b(y)-\delta_0a_y^{-4}.$$ Indeed $(V_c(z)+h)_+^{p-1}\ge ca_y^{-2}$ on the kernel support, and $R1(y)$ is bounded below. This derivative bound and the gap <a href="#m:eq:barrier-gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:barrier-gap">[m:eq:barrier-gap]</a> are the two margins used in the conditional field comparisons and the subsequent passage from $r$ to $2r$.

<a id="m:sec:conditioning"></a>
### Noisy observations and conditional screening

We compare the conditional electron density with the nonlinear reaction law of Section <a href="#m:sec:barriers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:sec:barriers">7</a>. The observation noise makes a rare data event inexpensive to impose on the eigenstate. A local quantum comparison in the resulting state will then rule out each failure of the desired inverse relation. We use the master kernels and deterministic barriers of Section <a href="#m:sec:barriers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:sec:barriers">7</a>, with the small parameter now chosen from the proposed excess charge.

Suppose, towards a contradiction, that the asserted uniform excess-charge bound fails. By Lemma <a href="#lem:sector-global-minimum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:sector-global-minimum">6</a>, choose systems at their largest strictly bound sectors such that $$K_{\rm exc}:=N-Z>0,\qquad K_{\rm exc}/M\longrightarrow\infty.$$ Fix a universal constant $B_0>2$, to be enlarged in the final argument, and define <a id="m:eq:barrier-scales"></a>$$\label{m:eq:barrier-scales}
 r_*=(B_0M/K_{\rm exc})^{1/3}\longrightarrow0,\qquad
 r_0=2^{-n}r_*,\qquad r_j=2^jr_0\quad(0\le j\le n),$$ where the finite integer $n\ge1$ is chosen so that $r_0<Z^{-1/3}$. The crude bound $N\le3Z$ and $M\ge1$ imply $r_*>Z^{-1/3}$. All subsequent smallness assertions concern this sequence of systems; their thresholds will be universal and independent of $n$.

<a id="pm-h-44"></a>
#### The common observation construction in molecular geometry

Set $\ell_j=r_j^{1.01}$ for $j<n$. Use the unordered arrays and likelihood versions of Section <a href="#sec:observations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:observations">5</a>, with $N$ particles, indices $j,\ldots,n-1$, and $\mathcal F_n$ trivial. The master kernels are those of <a href="#m:eq:master-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:master-kernel">[m:eq:master-kernel]</a>. Define $$\mu_j(y)=\mathbb E\left[\sum_{i=1}^N\mathcal K_{x_i}(y)\mid\mathcal F_j\right],
 \qquad H_j=V_s-K*\mu_j.$$ Lemma <a href="#lem:obs-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:obs-data">25</a> gives the joint versions, fixed-system regularity, Poisson equation, and the tower property. Here the ground state attains $E$, so Lemma <a href="#lem:obs-tilt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:obs-tilt">26</a> gives offset $C\ell_j^{-2}\log^5(e/P_A)$ for every event of probability $P_A>0$.

<a id="pm-h-45"></a>
#### Upper tails and a spatial cap

The observations have two uses. First, their conditional electron density is a continuous field to which elliptic comparisons apply. Second, an event of these observations can be imposed at the small energy cost in Lemma <a href="#lem:obs-tilt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:obs-tilt">26</a>. We first use that cost to exclude large positive fields and excessive local observed counts.

For this subsection fix a numerical constant $L_1\ge1$. Its value will be chosen after the universal field caps have been obtained. At scale $r=r_j$, put $R_+=2r$. Constants in estimates of small probabilities may depend on $L_1$; the field thresholds below do not.

<a id="stmt-m-lem-cond-tail"></a>
<div id="m:lem:cond-tail" class="lemma">

**Lemma 38** (Positive conditional-field tail). *If $r/2\le a_y\le5L_1r$, then, for a universal $C$ and every $s>C$, <a id="m:eq:cond-tail"></a>$$\label{m:eq:cond-tail}
 \mathbb P\{H_j(y)>s a_y^{-4}\}
 \le e\exp\{-c_{L_1}(s-C)^{2/5}a_y^{-1+.004}\}.$$ All estimates hold for sufficiently small $r_*$, uniformly in $j<n$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-m-lem-cond-tail"><summary>Proof of stmt-m-lem-cond-tail</summary>

*Proof.* Write $a=a_y$ and tilt the event in question, if it has positive probability $P_A$. By the tower property, its average of $K*\mu_j$ is the tilted raw average of the potential of the master smears. Nuclei within a fixed multiple of $a$ contribute at most $Ca^{-4}$ to $V_s$. More distant electron smears have exactly their bare potential at $y$: a contributing smear near $y$ has center within $2t_y\ll a$. Dropping the remaining negative electron terms therefore gives $$\mathbb E[H_j(y)\mid A]\le Ca^{-4}+\mathbb E_{\psi_A}J_y.$$ Lemmas <a href="#m:lem:screen-positive-field" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:screen-positive-field">[m:lem:screen-positive-field]</a> and <a href="#m:lem:screen-count" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:screen-count">19</a>, with no initial cut, bound the last expression by $Ca^{-4}+C\sqrt{\delta/a}$. Indeed, for small $a$, their scale quantity is bounded by $C(a^{-7}+\delta)$. Lemma <a href="#lem:obs-tilt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:obs-tilt">26</a> and $\ell_j\ge c_{L_1}a^{1+.01}$ imply $$s-C\le C a^{7/2}\ell_j^{-1}\log^{5/2}(e/P_A)
       \le C_{L_1}a^{2.49}\log^{5/2}(e/P_A).$$ Solving for $P_A$ proves the assertion. The deterministic threshold comes solely from the universal local field estimates. ◻

</details>

Call $\{r\le a\le2L_1R_+\}$ the participating band. Use a grid of closed cubes $Q$ of side $r/100$ meeting this band. Let $Q'$ be concentric enlargements, chosen with bounded overlap to contain every ball of radius $r$ centered in $Q$, with an additional margin. The Lipschitz bound for $a$ ensures that $a_Q:=\inf_{Q'}a\ge r/2$ and that all points in $Q'$ fall in the range of Lemma <a href="#m:lem:cond-tail" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:cond-tail">38</a>. Choose smooth $0\le\chi_Q\le1$ equal to one on $Q$, supported in $Q'$, with $|\Delta\chi_Q|\le Cr^{-2}$.

<a id="stmt-m-lem-cond-cap"></a>
<div id="m:lem:cond-cap" class="lemma">

<a id="m:eq:cond-cap-moments"></a>
<a id="m:eq:cond-band-volume"></a>
**Lemma 39** (Spatial cap and its exceptional cost). *There are universal thresholds $C'_{\rm cap},C_{\rm cap}$ with the following properties. Define $$\begin{split}
 Q\text{ is bad}&\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
       \sup_QH_j>C'_{\rm cap}a_Q^{-4},\\
 B_Q&=\mathbf 1_{\{Q\text{ bad}\}}\sup_Q(H_j)_+,
 \qquad \Pi=\sum_QB_Q\chi_Q,\qquad
 \mathcal E=\bigcup_{Q\text{ bad}}Q'.
 \end{split}$$ Then $H_j-\Pi\le C_{\rm cap}a^{-4}$ on the participating band. For each fixed $m\ge1$ and $A'>0$, $$\label{m:eq:cond-cap-moments}
 \mathbb P(Q\text{ bad})+a_Q^{4m}\mathbb EB_Q^m
       \le C_{L_1,m,A'}r^{A'}.$$ At each deterministic point, $\mathbb P(y\in\mathcal E)$ has the same arbitrarily high polynomial bound. The cube families are finite, and $$\label{m:eq:cond-band-volume}
 \sum_Q|Q'|\le C_{L_1}Mr^3.$$ All these random objects are jointly measurable in the data and the spatial variable. For the fixed system and scale, their sizes and supports have deterministic bounds.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-m-lem-cond-cap"><summary>Proof of stmt-m-lem-cond-cap</summary>

*Proof.* Since $\Delta H_j\ge-Ca_Q^{-6}$ on $Q'$, adding a quadratic and applying the ball submean inequality gives, for any fixed large $C'$ and a universal $C''$, $$\sup_QH_j\le C''a_Q^{-4}
       +Cr^{-3}\int_{Q'}(H_j-C'a_Q^{-4})_+.$$ The same inequality follows for distributional Laplacians by mollification. Choose $C'$ above the threshold in Lemma <a href="#m:lem:cond-tail" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:cond-tail">38</a>. Because $a_z/a_Q$ is bounded and $a_z\le5L_1r$ on $Q'$, integration of that tail yields $$\mathbb E(H_j(z)-C'a_Q^{-4})_+^m
       \le C a_Q^{-4m}r^{A'}$$ for arbitrary fixed $m,A'$. Jensen’s inequality for the spatial average gives the same bound for the integral term. Taking $C'_{\rm cap}>C''+1$, Markov’s inequality gives the bad-cube probability. On a bad cube its supremum is bounded by the integral term plus $C''a_Q^{-4}$; the probability just proved controls the latter contribution to every moment. This proves <a href="#m:eq:cond-cap-moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-cap-moments">[m:eq:cond-cap-moments]</a>.

If a point lies in a bad cube, the corresponding term of $\Pi$ dominates $(H_j)_+$ there. Otherwise the good-cube bound gives the asserted cap, since $a_Q$ and the local scale are comparable by a universal factor. At a fixed point only a bounded number of enlarged cubes occur, proving the estimate for $\mathcal E$. Every participating or enlarged cube lies within distance $C_{L_1}r$ of some nucleus, by $d_y\le La_y$. Counting grid cubes in this union proves finiteness and <a href="#m:eq:cond-band-volume" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-band-volume">[m:eq:cond-band-volume]</a>. Cube suprema are measurable through a countable dense subset, because $H_j$ is continuous. The bounds from Lemma <a href="#lem:obs-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:obs-data">25</a> also give the last assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-46"></a>
#### The inverse comparison and the order of constants

We now fix the constants needed for propagation. Choose $C_{\rm inv}$ larger than the barrier upper bound in $a^{-4}$ units and than $C_{\rm cap}$. Choose $C_{\rm top}>C_{\rm inv}$ sufficiently large and put $h_{\rm top}(y)=C_{\rm top}a_y^{-4}$; this also exceeds $h_b$ with a fixed margin. If $\gamma$ is the constant in Lemma <a href="#m:lem:barrier-gap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:barrier-gap">37</a>, choose $0<\lambda_2<\lambda_1<\gamma/4$. Choose $L_1$ sufficiently large that, when $a>L_1R_+$, subtracting $\lambda_2R_+^{-4}$ from either upper cap places it strictly below $b_{R_+}$. This is possible since $|b_{R_+}|\le Ca^{-4}$ there. The preceding probability estimates now apply with this fixed $L_1$.

Choose a safety constant $e_0>0$ so small that $\eta=e_0R_+^{-4}$ is smaller than the barrier margin above $h_b$ throughout $R_+\le a\le2L_1R_+$ and than the shift gap. Finally choose $\delta_0>0$ small compared with that margin, $\lambda_2$, and $\lambda_1-\lambda_2$, so that <a id="m:eq:cond-shift-order"></a>$$\label{m:eq:cond-shift-order}
 16\delta_0+2e_0<\lambda_1-\lambda_2,\qquad
 \delta_0<\lambda_2,\qquad 2e_0<c'(2L_1)^{-4}.$$ These choices precede the final smallness requirement on $r_*$.

<a id="m:eq:cond-inverse-derivative"></a>
<a id="m:eq:cond-clipping"></a>
Define $$T_y(h)=\frac{f_y(h)}{4\pi},\qquad
 I_y(m)=\begin{cases}
 h_b(y),&m\le T_y(h_b(y)),\\
 T_y^{-1}(m),&T_y(h_b(y))<m<T_y(h_{\rm top}(y)),\\
 h_{\rm top}(y),&m\ge T_y(h_{\rm top}(y)).
 \end{cases}$$ The positivity on the master kernel established with the barriers implies $$\label{m:eq:cond-inverse-derivative}
 T_y'(h)\ge ca_y^{-2}
       \quad(h\ge h_b(y)-\delta_0a_y^{-4}).$$ Indeed, differentiating the power $3/2$ leaves a positive square root bounded below by $ca_y^{-2}$ on a kernel of integral $1+o(1)$. Thus the inverse on the indicated interval exists and is continuous. The clipped inverse satisfies $$\label{m:eq:cond-clipping}
 \begin{aligned}
 m\ge T_y(h_b)&\ \Longrightarrow\ T_y(I_y(m))\le m,\\
 m\le T_y(h_{\rm top})&\ \Longrightarrow\ T_y(I_y(m))\ge m.
 \end{aligned}$$

<a id="stmt-m-lem-cond-inverse"></a>
<div id="m:lem:cond-inverse" class="lemma">

<a id="m:eq:cond-inverse"></a>
**Lemma 40** (One-sided inverse comparisons). *Fix $j<n$, and put $r=r_j$ and $R_+=2r$. For each deterministic $y$ with $r\le a_y\le2L_1R_+$, the failure probability of each implication below is less than $r^{40}$: $$\label{m:eq:cond-inverse}
 \begin{aligned}
 \mu_j(y)\ge T_y(h_b(y))
   &\ \Longrightarrow\ H_j(y)\ge I_y(\mu_j(y))-\delta_0a_y^{-4},\\
 \mu_j(y)\le T_y(h_{\rm top}(y))
   &\ \Longrightarrow\ H_j(y)\le I_y(\mu_j(y))+\delta_0a_y^{-4}.
 \end{aligned}$$ The constants and the smallness threshold for $r_*$ are independent of the system and the number of scales.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-m-lem-cond-inverse"><summary>Proof of stmt-m-lem-cond-inverse</summary>

*Proof.* The argument has three steps. Observation matching first relates the conditional density to the particles retained by a fresh patch. The patch comparison then determines the local offset except for a small event, whose negative-field cost must also be controlled. Finally, two separate tower identities transfer the offset comparison back to the master field.

<a id="pm-h-47"></a>
###### A count-good event and the fresh patch.

Write $a=a_y$, $t=t_y$, $\tau=t/a$. Recall that $Rf(y)=\int\mathcal K_z(y)f(z)\,dz$: the averaging and the kernel gradient estimates below concern the center variable $z$. There is a universal $K_0$ such that <a id="m:eq:cond-observed-count"></a>$$\label{m:eq:cond-observed-count}
 \mathbb P\{\#\{i:Y_{ji}\in B(y,3a)\}>K_0a^{-3}\}<r^{42}.$$ Otherwise tilt this event. Lemma <a href="#lem:obs-tilt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:obs-tilt">26</a> gives $\delta\le Cr^{-2.02}\log^5(e/r^{42})\le a^{-7+.02}$ for small $r_*$. The last inequality is uniform: after multiplication by $a^{6.98}$ its left side is at most $C_{L_1}r^{4.96}\log^5(e/r^{42})$. Every compatible raw configuration has the same excessive count in $B(y,4a)$, because $\sqrt3\ell_j=o(a)$. A fixed cell cover and Lemma <a href="#m:lem:screen-count" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:screen-count">19</a> contradict this for large enough $K_0$.

<a id="m:eq:cond-matching"></a>
Call the complement of this excessive-count event count-good. On such an observation path, match its entries to any compatible raw configuration. The kernel Lipschitz bound gives $$\label{m:eq:cond-matching}
 \left|\sum_i\mathcal K_{x_i}(y)
             -\sum_i\mathcal K_{Y_{ji}}(y)\right|
       \le Ca^{-3}\ell_jt^{-4}.$$ Only observations in $B(y,3a)$ can contribute to this difference. The conditional ratios in Lemma <a href="#lem:obs-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:obs-data">25</a> are supported on compatible configurations. Averaging <a href="#m:eq:cond-matching" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-matching">[m:eq:cond-matching]</a> therefore gives the same bound with $\mu_j(y)$ replacing the raw sum.

If an implication in <a href="#m:eq:cond-inverse" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-inverse">[m:eq:cond-inverse]</a> fails with probability at least $r^{40}$, intersect its failure event with count-good data and call the result $A$. Then $P_A:=\mathbb P(A)\ge r^{41}$ for small $r$. Write $P$ for the original joint law of raw variables and all noisy arrays, and $Q=P(\cdot\mid A)$. Its raw marginal is the law of $\psi_A=\sqrt{p_A/P_A}\Psi$, with $\delta\le a^{-7+.02}$. Apply Lemma <a href="#m:lem:screen-patch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:screen-patch">[m:lem:screen-patch]</a> to this state, choosing $b=a^{1+.2}$ and $\beta=b/a=a^{.2}$. Once selected by shell averaging, the patch radius is deterministic. Extend $Q$ by sampling the fresh cut labels conditionally on the raw variables with the cut’s squared partition probabilities. The raw-label marginal is exactly the patch construction for $\psi_A$. Write $X$ for its cut data, $\rho_X^c$ for its conditional core density, $\rho$ for its local Thomas–Fermi minimizer, and $\sigma$ for its retained fine density. These are conditional objects under this tilted patch law. In contrast, $\mu_j$ remains the conditional density defined under $P$.

<div class="center">

| Law           | Construction and conditional quantities                                                                                                    |
|:--------------|:-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| $P=\mathbb P$ | The raw state $\Psi$ with independent noisy arrays. The fields $\mu_j,H_j$ retain their definitions using $\mathcal F_j$ under this law.   |
| $Q$           | $P(\cdot\mid A)$, extended by the fresh cut labels. The quantities $\rho_X^c,\rho,\sigma$ use its raw-label marginal and the cut data $X$. |

</div>

<a id="m:eq:cond-fine-matching"></a>
<a id="m:eq:cond-density-errors"></a>
The fine smearing moves retained centers by at most $\sqrt3b$. Every particle relevant to the master kernel is in the retained central region. Thus <a href="#m:eq:cond-matching" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-matching">[m:eq:cond-matching]</a> implies, $Q$-almost surely, $$\label{m:eq:cond-fine-matching}
 |R\sigma(y)-\mu_j(y)|\le Ca^{-3}(\ell_j+b)t^{-4}=o(a^{-6}).$$ Let $G=\{D(\sigma-\rho)\le a^{-7+.01}\}$. Lemma <a href="#m:lem:screen-density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:screen-density">[m:lem:screen-density]</a> gives <a id="m:eq:cond-patch-budget"></a>$$\label{m:eq:cond-patch-budget}
 \mathbb E_Q\left[D(\sigma-\rho)+\int W_-\sigma\right]
       \le Ca^{-7+.02},\qquad
 \mathbb E_Q\int\sigma^{5/3}\le Ca^{-7}.$$ In particular $Q(G^c)\le Ca^{.01}$. On $G$, Lemma <a href="#lem:loc-coulomb" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:loc-coulomb">10</a> gives $$|R\rho(y)-R\sigma(y)|\le Ct^{-5/2}a^{-3.5+.005}=o(a^{-6}).$$ For clarity, after division by $a^{-6}$ these two errors are bounded by $$\label{m:eq:cond-density-errors}
 C\{a^{.01}\tau^{-4}+a^{.2}\tau^{-4}
                         +a^{.005}\tau^{-5/2}\}=o(1).$$ The lower bound $\tau\ge c_{L_1}a^w$, $w=10^{-5}$, makes every power positive.

<a id="pm-h-48"></a>
###### The local inverse comparison and rare negative fields.

<a id="m:eq:cond-good-high"></a>
<a id="m:eq:cond-good-low"></a>
Put $h_X=W-V_c$. On $G$, in the high-density case one must have $$\label{m:eq:cond-good-high}
 h_X(y)\ge I_y(\mu_j(y))-\tfrac14\delta_0a^{-4}.$$ Indeed, the opposite inequality and Lemma <a href="#m:lem:barrier-oscillation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:barrier-oscillation">34</a> imply on the master kernel $h_X(z)\le I_y(\mu_j(y))-\delta_0a^{-4}/8$. To cover arbitrarily negative $h_X(y)$, note that $h\mapsto h+C\tau h_-$ is increasing for small $\tau$; evaluate it at the proposed upper threshold, which is bounded in $a^{-4}$ units. The oscillation error there is $o(a^{-4})$. The Thomas–Fermi equation, <a href="#m:eq:cond-inverse-derivative" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-inverse-derivative">[m:eq:cond-inverse-derivative]</a>, and the first part of <a href="#m:eq:cond-clipping" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-clipping">[m:eq:cond-clipping]</a> then yield $R\rho(y)\le\mu_j(y)-c\delta_0a^{-6}$, contradicting <a href="#m:eq:cond-density-errors" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-density-errors">[m:eq:cond-density-errors]</a>. In the low-density case the lower oscillation estimate and the increasing function $h\mapsto h-C\tau h_-$ similarly give, on $G$, $$\label{m:eq:cond-good-low}
 h_X(y)\le I_y(\mu_j(y))+\tfrac14\delta_0a^{-4}.$$ Here the second clipping inequality is used.

<a id="m:eq:cond-negative-error"></a>
The deterministic upper bound $h_X(y)\le Ca^{-4}$ controls the positive error on $G^c$ in the low-density case. The high-density case requires a bound on negative fields there. By kernel positivity, $\mu_j\ge T_y(h_b)\ge ca^{-6}$. Equation <a href="#m:eq:cond-fine-matching" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-fine-matching">[m:eq:cond-fine-matching]</a> and $\mathcal K_z(y)\le Ct^{-3}$ force, on every configuration, $$m_\sigma:=\int_{B(y,2t)}\sigma\ge ca^{-6}t^3=ca^{-3}\tau^3.$$ The upper oscillation bound implies on this ball $$W_-\ge(1-C\tau)h_X(y)_--V_c-Ca^{-4}\tau^{1/2}.$$ Integrate against $\sigma$, divide by $m_\sigma$, and average over $G^c$. The patch bound controls the $W_-\sigma$ numerator by $Ca^{-7+.02}$. For the nuclear term, weighted convexity and the uniform integral of $|z-R_m|^{-5/2}$ on a radius-$2t$ ball give $$\int_{B(y,2t)}V_c^{5/2}\le Ca^{-7}\tau^{1/2}.$$ Hölder’s inequality on probability times space therefore gives $$\begin{split}
 \mathbb E_Q\mathbf 1_{G^c}\int_{B(y,2t)}V_c\sigma
 &\le\left(\mathbb E_Q\int\sigma^{5/3}\right)^{3/5}
       \left(Q(G^c)\int_{B(y,2t)}V_c^{5/2}\right)^{2/5}\\
 &\le Ca^{-7+.004}\tau^{.2}.
 \end{split}$$ We conclude that $$\label{m:eq:cond-negative-error}
 \mathbb E_Q\mathbf 1_{G^c}h_X(y)_-
 \le Ca^{-4}\{a^{.02}\tau^{-3}+a^{.004}\tau^{-2.8}
                              +Q(G^c)\tau^{1/2}\}=o(a^{-4}).$$ Since $|I_y|\le Ca^{-4}$, the bounds on $G$ now imply the corresponding one-sided comparisons in expectation, with error $\delta_0a^{-4}/4+o(a^{-4})$.

<a id="pm-h-49"></a>
###### Transfer of averaged fields.

<a id="m:eq:cond-transfer"></a>
The remaining step compares the expectation of this fresh patch offset with that of the master offset under the event law $Q$. Apply Lemma <a href="#lem:obs-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:obs-transfer">27</a> with the nuclear splitting $V=V_s+V_c$, master width $t=t_y$, and fine width $b=a^{1.2}$. Lemma <a href="#m:lem:screen-density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:screen-density">[m:lem:screen-density]</a> gives the raw local density and Coulomb error bounds; the deleted shell estimate is <a href="#m:eq:screen-deleted-shell" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:screen-deleted-shell">[m:eq:screen-deleted-shell]</a>. The pointwise density bound required for the nuclear remainder follows from $\rho=kW_+^{3/2}$ and <a href="#m:eq:screen-interior-upper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:screen-interior-upper">[m:eq:screen-interior-upper]</a>; its potential on a radius-$t$ ball is $Ca^{-4}(t/a)^{1/2}$ by <a href="#m:eq:local-power-potential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:local-power-potential">[m:eq:local-power-potential]</a>. Thus the explicit transfer estimate is $$\label{m:eq:cond-transfer}
 |\mathbb E_Q(H_j-h_X)(y)|
 \le Ca^{-4}\{\tau^{1/5}+\beta^{1/5}+\beta^{1/2}
                +a^{.01}\tau^{-1/2}+\tau^{1/2}\}=o(a^{-4}).$$ Here $\beta=a^{.2}$, $c_{L_1}a^w\le\tau\le c_1r_*^w$, and $.01-w/2>0$. The two expectations in the common lemma use different conditional laws; this verification identifies neither posterior pathwise.

On the high failure event, every path has $H_j(y)<I_y(\mu_j(y))-\delta_0a^{-4}$. Equations <a href="#m:eq:cond-good-high" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-good-high">[m:eq:cond-good-high]</a> and <a href="#m:eq:cond-negative-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-negative-error">[m:eq:cond-negative-error]</a>, together with <a href="#m:eq:cond-transfer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-transfer">[m:eq:cond-transfer]</a>, instead give $$\mathbb E_QH_j(y)\ge\mathbb E_QI_y(\mu_j(y))
                 -\tfrac14\delta_0a^{-4}-o(a^{-4}),$$ a contradiction. The low failure event is contradicted in the same way by <a href="#m:eq:cond-good-low" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-good-low">[m:eq:cond-good-low]</a>, the deterministic upper bound on $h_X$, and <a href="#m:eq:cond-transfer" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-transfer">[m:eq:cond-transfer]</a>. All remainders are uniform: $a\asymp_{L_1}r$, $\tau\ge c_{L_1}a^w$, and the smallest power in <a href="#m:eq:cond-negative-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-negative-error">[m:eq:cond-negative-error]</a> is $.004-2.8w>0$. The constants were fixed before letting $r_*\to0$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-50"></a>
#### Moments for the integrated exceptional cost

The inverse estimates concern each deterministic spatial point. Their use in the next section requires integrable costs for failed comparisons, not a single event on which all points succeed.

<a id="stmt-m-lem-cond-moments"></a>
<div id="m:lem:cond-moments" class="lemma">

<a id="m:eq:cond-field-moments"></a>
<a id="m:eq:cond-integrated-cost"></a>
**Lemma 41** (Conditional-density and reaction moments). *Fix $j<n$, put $r=r_j$ and $R_+=2r$, and let $r\le a_y\le2L_1R_+$. Under the original joint law $P=\mathbb P$, $$\label{m:eq:cond-field-moments}
 \|\mu_j(y)\|_2\le Ct_y^{-3}a_y^{-3},\qquad
 \|f_y(H_j(y))\|_2
        \le Ca_y^{-6}(1+\tau_y^{-3/2}).$$ Consequently, if $F_y$ is either failure event from Lemma <a href="#m:lem:cond-inverse" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:cond-inverse">40</a>, then $$\label{m:eq:cond-integrated-cost}
 \int_{\{r\le a_y\le2L_1R_+\}}
 \mathbb E\big[\mathbf 1_{F_y}(\mu_j(y)+f_y(H_j(y)))\big]\,\mathrm dy
       \le CM r^{20-3-3w}.$$ The same bound holds with $F_y$ replaced by $\{y\in\mathcal E\}$, and the expected integral over all of $\mathbb R^3$ of $r^{-2}\sum_QB_Q\mathbf 1_{Q'}$ is bounded by the right side as well.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-m-lem-cond-moments"><summary>Proof of stmt-m-lem-cond-moments</summary>

*Proof.* Conditional Jensen, the master-kernel support, and Lemma <a href="#m:lem:screen-count" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:screen-count">19</a> give $$\|\mu_j(y)\|_2\le Ct_y^{-3}
       \|\#\{i:x_i\in B(y,a_y)\}\|_2
       \le Ct_y^{-3}a_y^{-3}.$$ The tail bound implies $\|(H_j(y)_+)^{3/2}\|_2\le Ca_y^{-6}$. For the nuclear part of the reaction, weighted convexity and the uniform integral of $|z-R_m|^{-3/2}$ on a radius-$2t_y$ ball give $$\int\mathcal K_z(y)V_c(z)^{3/2}\,\mathrm dz
       \le Ct_y^{-3}a_y^{-9/2}t_y^{3/2}
       =Ca_y^{-6}\tau_y^{-3/2}.$$ This proves <a href="#m:eq:cond-field-moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-field-moments">[m:eq:cond-field-moments]</a>. Cauchy–Schwarz against $\mathbb P(F_y)<r^{40}$ contributes $r^{20}$. Use $a_y\asymp_{L_1}r$, $\tau_y\ge c_{L_1}r^w$, and <a href="#m:eq:cond-band-volume" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-band-volume">[m:eq:cond-band-volume]</a> to obtain <a href="#m:eq:cond-integrated-cost" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:cond-integrated-cost">[m:eq:cond-integrated-cost]</a>. The arbitrarily high polynomial bounds in Lemma <a href="#m:lem:cond-cap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:cond-cap">39</a> give the other assertions, choosing their exponent larger if necessary. All integrations are licensed by the joint measurable versions already constructed. ◻

</details>

<a id="m:sec:outward"></a>
### Outward propagation and the excess-charge bound

The inverse comparisons now allow us to replace a reaction term by the conditional electron density on one more layer of local scales. We perform this replacement before forgetting the observations at the current scale. The failed comparisons contribute a nonnegative error density. Its expected integral, rather than the probability of a simultaneous comparison throughout space, is the quantity that we shall sum.

We use the scales $r_j=2^j r_0$, $r_n=r_*$ and the retained data $\mathcal F_j$ of Section <a href="#m:sec:conditioning" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:sec:conditioning">8</a>. The data $\mathcal F_n$ are trivial. Write $\mu_j$ for their conditional smoothed density. At each step we take a local maximum of the previous comparison field and the observed field $H_j$, after lowering them by different constants. The previous field supplies the nonlinear reaction; $H_j$ supplies the actual density through its Poisson equation. The unequal shifts let the inverse comparisons prove the missing source inequality for each branch that can attain the maximum.

After the last observations are forgotten, the resulting positive exterior field will bound the smoothed electron mass in $\{a<r_*\}$ by $Z$ plus the accumulated error. The exterior-count estimate of Lemma <a href="#m:lem:void-count" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:void-count">30</a> controls the remaining smoothed mass, while the error has integral $o(M)$. These are the two contributions to the final excess-charge estimate.

<a id="m:eq:outward-invariant"></a>
<a id="m:eq:outward-bounds"></a>
The invariant at scale $r=r_j$ is a continuous locally $H^1$ offset $u$ and a nonnegative bounded compactly supported error density $d_j$ such that $$\label{m:eq:outward-invariant}
 \Delta u\ge -4\pi\nu_s
       +4\pi\mathbf 1_{\{a<r\}}\mu_j-4\pi d_j
       +\mathbf 1_{\{a\ge r\}}f_y(u)
 \quad\text{in distributions},$$ and $$\label{m:eq:outward-bounds}
 b_r\le u\le C_{\mathrm{inv}}a^{-4}\quad\text{on }\{a\ge r\},
 \qquad u=b_r\quad\text{on }\{a>L_1r\}.$$ Here and below an inequality between spatial functions is asserted for every positive-density retained path, using the versions of Lemma <a href="#lem:obs-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:obs-data">25</a>. The functions $u,d_j$ are jointly measurable in the retained data and space. We also maintain deterministic bounds, for the fixed physical system and step, on the modulus of continuity and the $H^1$ norm of $u$ on every compact set, and on the size and support of $d_j$. These additional bounds justify conditional integration. The integral estimate for $d_j$ will instead have universal constants.

<a id="pm-h-52"></a>
#### Replacing one layer of reaction by density

<a id="m:eq:outward-shifts"></a>
For this subsection put $r=r_j$, $R_+=2r$, and $H=H_j$. All parameters are fixed in the order specified in Section <a href="#m:sec:conditioning" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:sec:conditioning">8</a>. We record the inequalities that enter the argument: $$\label{m:eq:outward-shifts}
 \lambda_1<\frac\gamma4,\qquad
 16\delta_0+2e_0<\lambda_1-\lambda_2,\qquad
 \delta_0<\lambda_2,\qquad
 2e_0<c'(2L_1)^{-4}.$$ The constant $c'>0$ is the margin in $b_r\ge h_b+c'a^{-4}$. Set $\eta=e_0R_+^{-4}$ and $T_y=f_y/(4\pi)$.

For the current data, denote the high and low failure sets of Lemma <a href="#m:lem:cond-inverse" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:cond-inverse">40</a> by $$\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal B^{\mathrm h}
  &=\{y:\mu_j(y)\ge T_y(h_b(y)),\quad
        H(y)<I_y(\mu_j(y))-\delta_0a_y^{-4}\},\\
 \mathcal B^{\mathrm l}
  &=\{y:\mu_j(y)\le T_y(h_{\mathrm{top}}(y)),\quad
        H(y)>I_y(\mu_j(y))+\delta_0a_y^{-4}\}.
\end{aligned}$$ These sets are used only within the participating band $r\le a\le2L_1R_+$. Let $\Pi$, $B_Q$, $Q'$ and $\mathcal E$ be the penalty, its cube coefficients, the enlarged cubes and their bad union from Lemma <a href="#m:lem:cond-cap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:cond-cap">39</a>. In particular $\Pi=0$ off $\mathcal E$ and $H-\Pi\le C_{\mathrm{cap}}a^{-4}$ throughout this band.

<a id="stmt-m-prop-outward-step"></a>
<div id="m:prop:outward-step" class="proposition">

<a id="m:eq:outward-error"></a>
**Proposition 42** (One outward step). *Suppose that $u,d_j$ satisfy the inductive conditions above, including <a href="#m:eq:outward-invariant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-invariant">[m:eq:outward-invariant]</a>–<a href="#m:eq:outward-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-bounds">[m:eq:outward-bounds]</a>, at scale $r=r_j$. There are an offset $u'$ and a nonnegative error density $\widetilde d$, measurable with respect to the same data $\mathcal F_j$, that satisfy these inequalities at scale $R_+$ with $\mu_j$ still in the core term. One may take $$\begin{aligned}
\label{m:eq:outward-error}
 \widetilde d={}&d_j+C r^{-2}\sum_Q B_Q\mathbf 1_{Q'}\notag\\
 &+\mathbf 1_{\{r\le a<R_+\}}\mu_j
                       \mathbf 1_{\mathcal E\cup\mathcal B^{\mathrm h}}\notag\\
 &+\mathbf 1_{\{R_+\le a\le2L_1R_+\}}T_y(H)
                       \mathbf 1_{\mathcal E\cup\mathcal B^{\mathrm l}},
\end{aligned}$$ where $C$ is a universal constant large enough to dominate the penalty Laplacian.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-m-prop-outward-step"><summary>Proof of stmt-m-prop-outward-step</summary>

*Proof.* Define two shifted offsets $$u_1=u-\lambda_1R_+^{-4},\qquad
 u_2=H-\lambda_2R_+^{-4}-\Pi.$$ Use the following open cover to define $u'$: <a id="m:eq:outward-branches"></a>$$\label{m:eq:outward-branches}
\begin{array}{c|c}
 \text{open region}&u'\\ \hline
 a<(1+\kappa)R_+&\max(u_1,u_2)\\
 (1+\kappa/2)R_+<a<2L_1R_+&\max(u_1,u_2,b_{R_+})\\
 a>L_1R_+&b_{R_+}.
\end{array}$$ The definitions agree on overlaps. On the lower overlap, Lemma <a href="#m:lem:barrier-gap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:barrier-gap">37</a> and <a href="#m:eq:outward-shifts" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-shifts">[m:eq:outward-shifts]</a> give $$u_1\ge b_r-\lambda_1R_+^{-4}>b_{R_+}.$$ On the upper overlap the shifted cap inequalities, ensured by the choice of $L_1$, put both $u_1$ and $u_2$ below $b_{R_+}$. Thus the local maxima glue to a continuous locally $H^1$ function. No assertion about the measure or regularity of a level set of $a$ is required. The new exterior bounds follow from the same comparisons: for $R_+\le a<(1+\kappa)R_+$ the lower bound is supplied by $u_1$, and elsewhere the barrier is an included branch.

<a id="m:eq:outward-common"></a>
We verify the differential inequality locally. At a fixed point in one of the open regions, discard branches more than $\eta$ below the maximum. There is a neighborhood on which these discarded branches cannot attain the maximum, while every retained branch is within $2\eta$ of it. We show that each retained branch satisfies the common inequality $$\label{m:eq:outward-common}
 \Delta v\ge -4\pi\nu_s+4\pi\mathbf 1_{\{a<R_+\}}\mu_j
             -4\pi\widetilde d+\mathbf 1_{\{a\ge R_+\}}f_y(v)$$ on that neighborhood.

For $u_1$, the old core inequality suffices on $a<r$, and monotonicity of $f_y$ gives the required reaction on $a\ge R_+$. On the gain region $r\le a<R_+$, the error in <a href="#m:eq:outward-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-error">[m:eq:outward-error]</a> pays $\mu_j$ at points of $\mathcal E\cup\mathcal B^{\mathrm h}$. At every other gain point, $\Pi=0$ and near activity yields $$H\le u-(\lambda_1-\lambda_2)R_+^{-4}+2\eta.$$ Here $h_b\le u\le h_{\mathrm{top}}$. If $\mu_j>T_y(u)$, then the high comparison applies and $I_y(\mu_j)\ge u$, including the case where the inverse is clipped at its upper endpoint. It would follow that $$H\ge u-\delta_0a^{-4}\ge u-16\delta_0R_+^{-4},$$ contrary to <a href="#m:eq:outward-shifts" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-shifts">[m:eq:outward-shifts]</a>. Thus $4\pi\mu_j\le f_y(u)$. The old reaction supplies precisely the new core term on the gain region.

For $u_2$, the identity $\Delta H=-4\pi\nu_s+4\pi\mu_j$ gives the required core term on all of $a<R_+$, while the first increment in <a href="#m:eq:outward-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-error">[m:eq:outward-error]</a> pays the Laplacian of $\Pi$. Consider the remaining points where $u_2$ is retained. The lower splice or the included barrier gives $$u_2\ge b_{R_+}-2\eta
       \ge h_b+c'a^{-4}-2e_0R_+^{-4}>h_b.$$ The last inequality uses $a\le2L_1R_+$ and <a href="#m:eq:outward-shifts" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-shifts">[m:eq:outward-shifts]</a>. On $\mathcal E\cup\mathcal B^{\mathrm l}$ the last error term pays the reaction because $u_2\le H$ and $f_y$ is nondecreasing. Otherwise $\Pi=0$ and the cap gives $H<h_{\mathrm{top}}$. If $\mu_j<T_y(u_2)$, the low comparison applies and $I_y(\mu_j)\le u_2$, also when the inverse is clipped at its lower endpoint. We would obtain $$H\le u_2+\delta_0a^{-4}\le u_2+\delta_0R_+^{-4},$$ contradicting $H=u_2+\lambda_2R_+^{-4}$. Therefore $4\pi\mu_j\ge f_y(u_2)$, as required.

The barrier branch appears only where $a>R_+$, and Lemma <a href="#m:lem:barrier-subsolution" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:barrier-subsolution">36</a> gives <a href="#m:eq:outward-common" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-common">[m:eq:outward-common]</a> there. The old error density and every additional error are nonnegative, so they can only weaken the required lower bound.

These comparisons cover the exact level sets as well: $a=r$ belongs to the gain region and uses the old reaction, whereas $a=R_+$ belongs to the new exterior and uses its reaction. All right-side comparisons hold almost everywhere on the chosen neighborhood and therefore upgrade each retained branch’s distributional inequality to <a href="#m:eq:outward-common" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-common">[m:eq:outward-common]</a>. The source $\nu_s$ is a smooth bounded density for the fixed system, and all branches here are continuous and locally $H^1$. The reaction is also bounded on bounded ranges of its argument: for a fixed system, $t_{\min}:=\inf_z t_z>0$ and $V_c\in L_c^{5/2}$ give, for every $A>0$, $$\sup_{y,\,|h|\le A} f_y(h)
 \le C t_{\min}^{-3}\int V_c^{3/2}
       +CA^{3/2}\sup_y R1(y)<\infty.$$ Thus the first, nonsingular part of Lemma <a href="#lem:maximum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:maximum">28</a>, followed by a partition of unity, proves the inequality for $u'$ on all of $\mathbb R^3$. No vanishing of the reaction near the original nuclei is needed in this step. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-53"></a>
#### Averaging the data and summing the error

The next step explains why the nonlinear reaction survives the removal of observations. It also records the integrability needed in that operation.

<a id="stmt-m-lem-outward-average"></a>
<div id="m:lem:outward-average" class="lemma">

**Lemma 43** (Forgetting the current observations). *Set $$u_{j+1}=\mathbb E[u'\mid\mathcal F_{j+1}],\qquad
 d_{j+1}=\mathbb E[\widetilde d\mid\mathcal F_{j+1}].$$ Then $u_{j+1},d_{j+1}$ satisfy <a href="#m:eq:outward-invariant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-invariant">[m:eq:outward-invariant]</a>–<a href="#m:eq:outward-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-bounds">[m:eq:outward-bounds]</a> at scale $r_{j+1}$, with conditional density $\mu_{j+1}$. They retain continuity and local $H^1$ regularity for the offset and bounded compact support for the nonnegative error density.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-m-lem-outward-average"><summary>Proof of stmt-m-lem-outward-average</summary>

*Proof.* For the fixed system there are finitely many scales and cubes, and all master smoothing widths have a positive minimum. Lemma <a href="#lem:obs-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:obs-data">25</a> therefore gives deterministic bounds for the conditional densities, their potentials and their gradients. The offsets and error densities in the assertion are jointly measurable: maxima, clipped inverses and failure flags preserve measurability, and each cube supremum may be taken over a fixed countable dense subset.

Inductively the branches have deterministic continuity moduli and local $H^1$ bounds on each compact set. Their open definitions agree on overlaps; a finite subcover of a compact set supplies a Lebesgue number and hence a modulus for the glued function. The gradient of a finite maximum is almost everywhere one of the branch gradients. Conditional averaging consequently preserves continuity. It preserves the local $H^1$ bound by testing weak derivatives against compactly supported vector fields, using Fubini and Cauchy–Schwarz. The error terms have deterministic bounded size and support for the fixed system and step, so the same is true after averaging.

Integrate the distributional inequality of Proposition <a href="#m:prop:outward-step" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:prop:outward-step">42</a> against a fixed nonnegative compact test and then against the explicit positive-parent conditional law of Lemma <a href="#lem:obs-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:obs-data">25</a>. The preceding bounds justify Fubini. The tower property gives $\mathbb E[\mu_j\mid\mathcal F_{j+1}]=\mu_{j+1}$, while convexity gives $$\mathbb E[f_y(u')\mid\mathcal F_{j+1}]
     \ge f_y\bigl(\mathbb E[u'\mid\mathcal F_{j+1}]\bigr).$$ This is the required lower bound for the reaction. The exterior bounds and the exterior equality are deterministic and pass to the average. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-m-lem-outward-error"></a>
<div id="m:lem:outward-error" class="lemma">

<a id="m:eq:outward-total-error"></a>
**Lemma 44** (Integrated error). *Starting with $u=b_{r_0}$ and $d_0=0$, the preceding construction reaches scale $r_n=r_*$ and produces a deterministic error density with $$\label{m:eq:outward-total-error}
 \int d_n\le C M r_*^{17-3w}=o(M)
 \qquad\text{along the contradiction sequence}.$$ The constant is independent of the number of scales and of the nuclear configuration.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-m-lem-outward-error"><summary>Proof of stmt-m-lem-outward-error</summary>

*Proof.* Since $r_0<Z^{-1/3}\le a$, Lemma <a href="#m:lem:barrier-subsolution" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:barrier-subsolution">36</a> gives the initial invariant everywhere; there is no initial core region.

At scale $r=r_j$, apply Lemma <a href="#m:lem:cond-moments" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:cond-moments">41</a> to the three increments in <a href="#m:eq:outward-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-error">[m:eq:outward-error]</a>. The high-failure estimate pays $\mu_j$ on the gain region; the low-failure estimate pays $T_y(H)=f_y(H)/(4\pi)$ on the remaining participating region. The same Lemma pays membership in $\mathcal E$ and the penalty Laplacian. For $\mathcal B\in\{\mathcal B^{\mathrm h},\mathcal B^{\mathrm l}\}$, use $\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E\cup\mathcal B}\le\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E}+\mathbf 1_{\mathcal B}$. All these integrands are nonnegative, so restriction to the indicated subregions only improves their bounds. We obtain $$\mathbb E\int(\widetilde d-d_j)\le C M r^{17-3w}.$$

Conditional averaging preserves the unconditional expected integral of a nonnegative density. Because $\beta=17-3w>0$, $$\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}r_j^\beta
   =r_*^\beta\sum_{h=1}^{n}2^{-h\beta}
   \le\frac{r_*^\beta}{2^\beta-1}.$$ At the last step the data are trivial, so $d_n$ is deterministic. This proves <a href="#m:eq:outward-total-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-total-error">[m:eq:outward-total-error]</a>, uniformly in $n$. The argument has used the failure probability separately at each spatial point and then integrated it; it has imposed no simultaneous good-data event in space. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-54"></a>
#### The total-charge comparison

We have reached the unconditional density $\mu_n$ and replaced the reaction by this density throughout $\{a<r_*\}$, at total error $o(M)$. A final potential comparison converts this statement to a bound on the number of electrons in that region.

<details id="proof-stmt-m-lem-outward-error-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-m-lem-outward-error</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* Use the contradiction sequence and sector eigenstates of Section <a href="#m:sec:conditioning" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:sec:conditioning">8</a>. Its terminal scale is $r_*=(B_0M/K_{\mathrm{exc}})^{1/3}$. The universal $B_0$ will exceed the fixed count constant below. All deterministic parameters and tolerances are chosen before taking the sequence limit. The smallness requirements of the preceding lemmas then hold for all its sufficiently late members, uniformly over their scales.

At the final step put $$U=V_c+u_n,\qquad
 S=\nu-\mathbf 1_{\{a<r_*\}}\mu_n+d_n.$$ The signed measure $S$ has compact support. In fact $\{a<r_*\}$ lies in a finite union of balls around the nuclei, the error density has compact support, and the nuclei are finite. Since $\Delta V_c=-4\pi\nu+4\pi\nu_s$, the final invariant implies $$\Delta(U-K*S)\ge\mathbf 1_{\{a\ge r_*\}}f_y(u_n)\ge0.$$ The nuclear singularities cancel in the explicit identity <a id="m:eq:outward-cancellation"></a>$$\label{m:eq:outward-cancellation}
 U-K*S
   =u_n-V_s+K*(\mathbf 1_{\{a<r_*\}}\mu_n)-K*d_n.$$ The right-hand side is continuous and locally $H^1$ on all of $\mathbb R^3$: the last two potentials have bounded compactly supported densities, and the other terms have the stated regularity from the construction.

<a id="m:eq:outward-mass"></a>
Far from the nuclei, <a href="#m:eq:outward-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-bounds">[m:eq:outward-bounds]</a> gives $U=B_{r_*}=W_0+q_0-\Gamma_{r_*}$, which tends to zero by the deterministic field estimates. The compact source potential $K*S$ also tends to zero. For every $\epsilon>0$, the function $(U-K*S-\epsilon)_+$ consequently has compact support and is an admissible $H^1$ test in the subharmonic inequality. Testing gives a nonpositive squared gradient norm, so this positive part vanishes. Letting $\epsilon\downarrow0$ proves $$U\le K*S\qquad\text{away from the nuclear points}.$$ On every sufficiently large sphere $U=B_{r_*}\ge .9W_0>0$. The spherical mean of the potential of a compactly supported signed measure, on a sphere centered at the origin and enclosing its support, equals its total mass divided by the sphere radius. Hence $$\label{m:eq:outward-mass}
 0<\int\,\mathrm dS
   =Z-N+\int_{\{a\ge r_*\}}\mu_n+\int d_n.$$ Only positivity on a far sphere is used here; no asymptotic charge formula for $W_0$ is needed.

At the last scale the data have been completely averaged away, so $$\mu_n(y)=\mathbb E_\Psi\sum_i\mathcal K_{x_i}(y).$$ If a kernel centered at $x$ contributes to a point with $a_y\ge r_*$, then its support and the Lipschitz scale estimate imply $$a_y\le a_x+\frac{|x-y|}{L}
       \le a_x\left(1+\frac{c_1r_*^w}{L}\right).$$ For small $r_*$ this forces $a_x\ge r_*/2$. Each kernel has integral one, and Lemma <a href="#m:lem:void-count" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:lem:void-count">30</a> therefore gives $$\int_{\{a\ge r_*\}}\mu_n
   \le\mathbb E_\Psi\#\{i:a(x_i)\ge r_*/2\}
   \le C M r_*^{-3}.$$ Combining this with <a href="#m:eq:outward-total-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-total-error">[m:eq:outward-total-error]</a> and <a href="#m:eq:outward-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="m:eq:outward-mass">[m:eq:outward-mass]</a> yields $$K_{\mathrm{exc}}<C M r_*^{-3}+o(M)
                  =\frac{C}{B_0}K_{\mathrm{exc}}+o(M).$$ Choose $B_0>2C$, increasing it also to ensure the initial scale choice in Section <a href="#m:sec:conditioning" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="m:sec:conditioning">8</a>. Since $K_{\mathrm{exc}}/M\to\infty$, the last inequality is impossible. This proves the existence of one universal finite constant bounding the excess per nucleus in every strictly bound sector.

For an arbitrary strictly bound sector, the largest strictly bound sector of the same system has at least as many electrons, so the same bound applies. For every integer $N>Z+CM$, strict adjacent binding is therefore impossible. Sector monotonicity gives $E_N\le E_{N-1}$, and exclusion of the strict inequality gives $E_N=E_{N-1}$. Taking $M=1$ proves both atomic assertions for every real nuclear charge $Z\ge1$. ◻

</details>

<a id="a:sec:conditioning"></a>
### Conditional density and field comparisons

We now specialize to one nucleus at the origin and use $V(y)=Z/|y|$, $d_y=|y|$, and the atomic distance scale $a_y=d_y/L$ with $L=10^5$. The comparisons in this section allow every real $Z\ge1$; the neutral-sector conclusions below use integer charges.

Fix $0\le D_0<\infty$ and a normalized ground state $\Psi$ in a sector with $n\le3Z$ and $E_n\le E+D_0$. The ground-state assumption in this section is used in the multiplier identity <a href="#eq:sector-multiplier" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sector-multiplier">[eq:sector-multiplier]</a>; the patch comparisons themselves apply to the resulting states with an energy offset. All probabilities below initially refer to the raw position-and-spin law of $\Psi$, enlarged by the explicitly specified independent random variables. The common likelihood and event-cost estimates of Section <a href="#sec:observations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:observations">5</a> now give a simultaneous comparison throughout an atomic annulus. This strengthens the pointwise molecular comparison by using the absence of nuclear singularities from these annuli.

<a id="pm-h-56"></a>
#### Nested observations and master smoothing

<a id="a:eq:cond-observations"></a>
Choose $0<\varepsilon<1$ and $0<s<1$, and suppose $Z$ is large enough that $2r_0\le s$. Set $$\label{a:eq:cond-observations}
 r_0=\varepsilon Z^{-1/3},\qquad r_j=2^jr_0,\qquad
 m=\max\{j:r_j\le s\},\qquad \ell_j=r_j^{1.01}\quad(j<m).$$ Thus $s/2<r_m\le s$. At scale $j<m$ observe the positions $x_i+\ell_j U_{ji}$ and then forget the particle labels separately in each array. All Cartesian coordinates of all $U_{ji}$, at all scales, are independent with density $$\zeta(u)=c_\zeta\exp\!\left(-\frac1{1-u^2}\right)
                  \mathbf 1_{\{|u|<1\}}.$$ Represent the unordered array by its lexicographically sorted tuple $Y_j$. Ties have probability zero. Write $\mathcal F_j=\sigma(Y_j,\ldots,Y_{m-1})$ and let $\mathcal F_m$ be trivial. These sigma-fields decrease with $j$.

<a id="a:eq:cond-master"></a>
Fix $w=10^{-5}$ and $0<c_1<(10L)^{-1}$. With the same smooth radial packet $g$ as in Section <a href="#sec:localization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:localization">3</a>, define $$\label{a:eq:cond-master}
 \begin{gathered}
 D_x^0=\max(d_x,r_0),\qquad
 t_x=c_1D_x^0\min(D_x^0,s)^w,\qquad
 \mathcal K_x(y)=g_{t_x}(y-x)^2,\\
 \mu_j(y)=\mathbb E\!\left[\sum_{i=1}^n\mathcal K_{x_i}(y)
                    \,\middle|\,\mathcal F_j\right],\qquad
 H_j=V-K*\mu_j,\qquad
 \mathcal R f(y)=\int\mathcal K_z(y)f(z)\,\,\mathrm dz.
 \end{gathered}$$ The lower clamp in $D_x^0$ is retained also for particles close to the nucleus; the upper clamp is only on the factor raised to $w$. In particular, $t_x\ge t_{\min}:=c_1r_0^{1+w}>0$ and $t$ is Lipschitz with constant at most $c_1(1+w)s^w$.

Use the likelihood-ratio versions of Lemma <a href="#lem:obs-data" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:obs-data">25</a> for these arrays and kernels. They give joint measurability, fixed-system derivative bounds, $\mu_j\ge0$, $\int\mu_j=n$, and <a id="a:eq:cond-tower"></a>$$\label{a:eq:cond-tower}
 \mathbb E[\mu_j\mid\mathcal F_{j+1}]=\mu_{j+1},\qquad
 \mathbb E[H_j\mid\mathcal F_{j+1}]=H_{j+1}.$$ The offset $H_j-V$ is locally Lipschitz and <a id="a:eq:cond-poisson"></a>$$\label{a:eq:cond-poisson}
 \Delta H_j=-4\pi\nu+4\pi\mu_j.$$ Lemma <a href="#lem:obs-tilt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:obs-tilt">26</a> applies with baseline $E_n$ and offset $D_0$. In particular, an event of probability $p>0$ at index $j$ has tilted energy at most $E+D_0+C\ell_j^{-2}\log^5(e/p)$. We retain this full baseline dependence throughout the argument.

<a id="pm-h-57"></a>
#### The simultaneous inverse comparison

<a id="stmt-a-prop-cond-comparison"></a>
<div id="a:prop:cond-comparison" class="proposition">

<a id="a:eq:cond-inverse"></a>
**Proposition 45** (Conditional density determines the field). *There is a universal constant $C_{\rm cap}$ with the following property. Fix $L_1\ge2$, $0<h_l<h_h<\infty$, and a sufficiently small $\xi>0$; it suffices to require $\xi<h_l/16$. There exists $s_*=s_*(D_0,L_1,h_l,h_h,\xi)>0$ such that, whenever $0<s\le s_*$, at each $j<m$, with $r=r_j$, there is an event $\mathcal G_j\in\mathcal F_j$ satisfying $\mathbb P(\mathcal G_j^c)\le Cr^{25}$ on which, simultaneously for $r\le d_y\le4L_1r$ and $h_l\le h\le h_h$, $$\label{a:eq:cond-inverse}
 \begin{aligned}
 d_y^6\mu_j(y)>kh^{3/2}
       &\quad\Longrightarrow\quad d_y^4H_j(y)\ge h-\xi,\\
 d_y^6\mu_j(y)<kh^{3/2}
       &\quad\Longrightarrow\quad d_y^4H_j(y)\le h+\xi.
 \end{aligned}$$ On the same event, $H_j(y)\le C_{\rm cap}d_y^{-4}$ throughout that band. The constant $C$ may depend on the fixed parameters, whereas $C_{\rm cap}$ does not depend on $D_0,L_1,h_l,h_h,\xi$. All estimates are uniform in $Z$, the ground state, $j$, and $m$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-a-prop-cond-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-a-prop-cond-comparison</summary>

*Proof.* We first control the observed count and the positive field throughout the band. At a fixed point and height, a hypothetical failure then defines a tilted state. Its fresh patch compares the observed density with the patch field, including the contribution of rare negative fields; the two expectation towers turn this into a contradiction. Finally, spatial and height nets give the simultaneous statement. Every occurrence of $o(1)$ below tends to zero as $s\downarrow0$, uniformly for $r_0\le r=r_j\le s$ and the stated bands, after the fixed parameters in the proposition have been chosen.

<a id="a:eq:cond-kernel"></a>
<a id="a:eq:cond-tau"></a>
*Kernel geometry.* Put $a=a_y=d_y/L$, $t=t_y$ and $\tau=t/a$. If $\mathcal K_z(y)\ne0$, the Lipschitz bound on $t$ gives $|z-y|\le t_z\le t_y+Cs^w|z-y|$, hence $|z-y|\le2t_y$. On this support, $t_z/t_y=1+O(s^w)$. Comparison with the fixed-width kernel $g_t(y-z)^2$ therefore gives $$\label{a:eq:cond-kernel}
 \int\mathcal K_z(y)\,\,\mathrm dz=1+O(s^w),\qquad
 |\mathcal K_z(y)|\le Ct^{-3},\qquad
